# SSOT Card UX Phase 2

Status: **PR #10 production baseline plus local-only follow-up**. PR #10 was owner-merged on 2026-09-10 and the resulting `main` build was successfully deployed to GitHub Pages. Changes made after that merge remain local until a later explicit publication request. Builds on the Health-honesty and result-card-v2 changes from base `b85fcbaa17a5261989818ad3cd72fde852a1215a`.

Current local candidate snapshot (2026-09-22): **196 catalog APIs / 193 structured-agent runnable / 184 automated-verification-eligible / 0 generic `DataTablePreview` routes / 0 active generic `MediaGalleryPreview` routes / 5 native WebMCP tools**. The latest deterministic product gate is recorded in the newest milestone below. Earlier entries preserve historical gate counts and blockers at the milestone where they were recorded. Use `npm run test:publish-readiness` for the bounded pre-publication gate. Live-provider verification remains scope-selected and policy-aware rather than one unconditional release command.

## Goal and scope

Replace the least useful DataTablePreview compositions with domain-specific experiences, not different decoration around arbitrary properties. Phase 2 began incrementally: **9 of the original 64 DataTablePreview routes** were upgraded across two bounded batches; one generic route (MusicBrainz) was removed locally because its required application-identifying User-Agent cannot be satisfied by this browser-only architecture. Later focused RDAP, OSRM, GLEIF, FDIC, and mempool.space remediations reduced the generic DataTable set further, and the later NWS browser-admission correction removed another generic route. The 2026-09-09 terminal semantic migrations removed the final active generic previews, so **0 `DataTablePreview` catalog routes remain** in the current local candidate. A separate Yahoo Finance market-chart route is also removed locally because it depended on a third-party compatibility relay to automate an unsupported Yahoo endpoint whose direct browser request is CORS-blocked and whose provider terms restrict automated collection. Gutendex is also removed locally as a durability/policy admission decision rather than a current outage claim. An earlier audit saw two independent Pages-origin timeouts plus direct probe timeouts, but a later same-day recheck recovered to HTTP 200 twice from the Pages origin, so that failure is classified as transient. The hosted site explicitly positions itself for test queries and recommends self-hosting for long-term use; its current `robots.txt` also disallows `/books/` for the generic crawler user-agent. Because Open Library already preserves browser-native book discovery, the candidate avoids depending on this duplicate hosted test surface for structured-agent support. After those admission corrections, the later quality-gated five-API capability expansion, the 2026-09-16 Europe PMC browser-admission removal, and the 2026-09-17 Zenodo browser-admission removal, the current local catalog has 197 APIs. Batch 1 covered six APIs and batch 2 covers three diagnostic APIs. A later unpublished provider-policy follow-up preserves the request definitions for admitted APIs while restricting structured agent execution for policy-bound endpoints and adds a generated machine-readable catalog from the same SSOT.

| API ID | Composition | User benefit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `color-api` | Color swatch and specifications | Actual requested color, exact hex copying, all supplied color spaces, nearest-name distinction |
| `google-dns-doh` | DNS diagnostic panel | Readable record types, TTL in seconds, complete TXT values, DNS status distinct from HTTP status |
| `npm-download-counts` | Package download summary | Package identity, exact total, inclusive UTC reporting window, explicitly calculated daily average |
| `endoflife-date` | Release support explorer | Filter and reveal all returned cycles, independent support/EOL flags, latest version, native support-date disclosure |
| `exchange-rate-current` | Daily FX conversion board | Client-side amount conversion, searchable currencies, full supplied rate precision and update times, required attribution |
| `ecb-fx-rates` | Coinbase fiat/crypto conversion board | Same calculation primitives, separate Coinbase adapter, tiny crypto rates preserved, no invented update timestamp |

The legacy ID `ecb-fx-rates` remains stable but its current provider is Coinbase. Do not label it ECB.

### Batch 2: diagnostic evidence

| API ID | Composition | User benefit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `openssf-scorecard` | Security practice evidence board | Provider aggregate, all individual checks, true zero versus inconclusive/unknown, complete reasons and expandable evidence, official per-check guidance |
| `languagetool-grammar-check` | Writing review | Full explanations, context-local highlights, explicit flagged text, selectable/copyable suggestions, no-issues versus malformed/partial results |
| `nhtsa-vehicle-recalls` | Recall investigation report | Campaign identity, complete defect/consequence/remedy, positive do-not-drive/park-outside flags, unknown flags kept distinct, model-level versus VIN scope |

Before this batch, the live NHTSA adapter exposed arbitrary early properties and omitted the entire defect/consequence/remedy narrative. The Scorecard adapter reduced documentation to `2 properties`. These were reproduced in the existing candidate before replacement.

Diagnostic rules:

- Scorecard `-1` is inconclusive, not `0`. Missing/out-of-range scores have no meter or numeric value. The aggregate is supplied by the provider, never recomputed from the visible subset. Score/date/commit are snapshot evidence, not certification or a new scan initiated by the browser.
- Grammar requires a usable `matches` array. An empty valid array means no issues returned, not guaranteed error-free text. Missing/malformed matches or an explicitly incomplete result cannot become a green all-clear. Highlights use context-local bounds, with plain text when the bounds are invalid; replacements never silently modify or resubmit text.
- Recall arrays, malformed rows, missing safety narratives and count/list mismatches stay distinct. A model search never determines whether a particular VIN is affected, repaired or safe. Provider dates are preserved verbatim, avoiding a locale guess for values such as `10/12/2020` from an endpoint also returning `25/03/2021`.
- Complete safety text is visible by default; only supporting evidence/notes use native disclosure. No line clamps or property-count placeholders hide critical information.
- Unknown boolean flags are not rendered as false. Flag counts include the unknown count explicitly.
- Public LanguageTool prohibits automated requests. Its documentation/usage warning and attribution are explicit. The current local follow-up adds the restriction to the API SSOT, exposes it through WebMCP discovery and Agent-Readable DOM metadata, excludes the endpoint from the structured run-tool enum, and independently fail-closes direct structured execution attempts. Human interactive Request Lab use remains available.


## Data and interaction rules

- Domain adapters return typed view models. Visual content and machine-readable metadata use the same values; no parallel agent payload or duplicate catalog.
- Missing/null/invalid numbers are not zero. Genuine zero downloads and zero-second TTL remain valid.
- Rate strings preserve supplied decimal digits. Numeric JSON rates preserve the parsed numeric value, not their original wire formatting. Converted amounts use finite JavaScript arithmetic and are reference estimates, not financial settlement or trade quotes.
- A DNS HTTP 200 can contain NXDOMAIN or SERVFAIL. Expose that domain outcome through `data-result-state` and DNS code text without calling it a transport failure.
- A period download total cannot support a historical sparkline or growth percentage. Derive only the labeled average over valid inclusive UTC dates.
- Lifecycle `isEol` and `isMaintained` are independent. Maintenance may include extended/paid support. Unknown dates do not imply unlimited support.
- Color swatches use validated six-digit provider hex values. A nearest named color and suggested text color are not an exact-name match or a verified contrast certification.
- Native inputs/selects/details/buttons expose clear labels, keyboard operation and >=44px control height. Local conversion, filtering, show-more and copy actions do not refetch providers.
- New styles live under `.domain-card` and load with the existing lazy response-preview chunk. Single-result content is not forced into a half-empty grid.
- Global body min-width no longer forces a 320px document into a smaller content area when a desktop scrollbar is present. This baseline issue was reproduced before changing the one rule. Color swatches explicitly fit their container instead of letting aspect-ratio/min-height create hidden horizontal clipping.

## Verification and reproducibility

```sh
# Bounded pre-publication gate (no full live-provider sweep)
npm run test:publish-readiness

# Scope-selected browser/provider evidence when relevant
npm run test:browser:domain-cards
npm run test:browser:diagnostic-cards
npm run test:browser:sdg-celestrak
npm run test:browser:biodiversity
npm run test:browser:geo-network
npm run test:browser:jolpica-f1
npm run test:browser:sports-schedules
npm run test:browser:uk-parliament
npm run test:browser:huggingface-models
npm run test:browser:canada-open-data
npm run test:browser:package-icon-cards
npm run test:browser:nvd-cve-search

# Broad live health is observational and policy-constrained, not an unconditional publish gate
npm run test:browser:health-sweep
```

The browser commands need Chromium. Set `CHROME_BIN` when it is not `/usr/bin/google-chrome`; optional `EVIDENCE_DIR` selects a local evidence folder. By default evidence is saved to a unique OS temporary directory. The harness uses a new isolated profile, not the user's authenticated browser.

It serves the candidate's local built HTML/JS/CSS through bounded browser interception **only for the GitHub Pages application path**. The page origin remains `https://yapweijun1996.github.io`; the first batch and the live diagnostic cases do not mock, proxy or rewrite provider responses. Diagnostic negative cases and LanguageTool UI checks use explicitly labelled synthetic fixtures in a separate browser context. Those are not live-health evidence. This verifies an unpublished candidate under the real browser origin; it is **not evidence of production deployment**.

Coverage includes six default live flows at 1440, 900, 390 and 320px Chromium viewport widths; document/card overflow; value clipping; control height; required accessibility-tree names; real clipboard write/readback; Enter-driven disclosure/show-more; rate calculations and filtering without additional requests; preview JS/CSS lazy loading; and live DNS NXDOMAIN/TXT cases. Country, Weather and GitHub provide unaffected-flow smoke coverage. Tests use synthetic edge fixtures separately from live provider evidence.

Initial local acceptance on 2026-09-05: unit suite 110/110; build/diff checks passed; six flows x four widths passed; three unaffected smokes passed; actual clipboard and keyboard checks passed; live DNS NXDOMAIN remained HTTP 200 with DNS status 3; live TXT values stayed complete. This is targeted evidence, **not a full-catalog health sweep or a Safari/iOS device certification**. Provider health is a point-in-time observation.

Acceptance snapshot after the 2026-09-08 semantic/provider-contract and preview-reliability remediations: Vitest **227/227 PASS**; `npm run build:pages` and `git diff --check` PASS. The earlier 12-route request-binding sweep passed **12/12 first-pass with zero retries**, the USAspending/Treasury sweep passed **2/2 first-pass with zero retries**, and the `wikidata-sparql` + `opencitations-index` targeted Pages-origin sweep passed **2/2 first-pass with zero retries**. The complete live domain-card regression remains **PASS with 41 interaction checks and zero browser-console errors**. The new SDG/CelesTrak verifier passed with the real GitHub Pages origin: `un-sdg-goals` used the live provider and matched all 17 goal code/title/description/URI records exactly to semantic DOM; `celestrak-satellites` used an explicitly synthetic official-documentation-shaped OMM fixture intercepted at the exact provider URL, with zero live CelesTrak request because its SSOT is cadence-limited. Native WebMCP/provider-policy verification remains **195 catalog IDs / 193 runnable IDs / 192 automated-verification-eligible IDs**, and both machine catalog and WebMCP discovery expose the bounded CelesTrak group set `stations` / `gps-ops`. The new biodiversity sweep passed **3/3 first-pass with zero retries** for OBIS, WoRMS, and Paleobiology Database. The focused biodiversity browser contract also passed from the real GitHub Pages origin, including a live WoRMS unaccepted-name case (`Manta birostris` → accepted `Mobula birostris`), exact OBIS occurrence/status/provenance/QC metadata, exact PBDB taxon/extancy/`n_occs` semantics, 390px reflow, and zero unnamed actionable controls. The new geo/network sweep also passed **2/2 first-pass with zero retries** for `ipwhois-lookup` and `geoboundaries-admin-boundaries`. Its focused Pages-origin verifier matched live IP location/network fields exactly, confirmed that reserved `127.0.0.1` is HTTP 200 with `success=false` and now becomes semantic state `invalid`, and verified a live Singapore ADM1 boundary layer with 5 administrative units, exact per-unit mean area/perimeter statistics, exact provider GeoJSON download URL, 390px reflow, and zero unnamed actionable controls. The new Jolpica F1 focused verifier also passed all three live dataset modes (`drivers`, `constructors`, `races`) with `limit=3`, exact primary identity/calendar semantics, 390px reflow, and zero unnamed actionable controls. The prior Openverse custom-request timeout and isolated GitHub `network-or-cors` interruption remain preserved as recovered transient evidence rather than durable provider drift. This remains evidence for the unpublished local candidate, not a production deployment claim.

## Next batch

The nine redesigned result experiences are already part of the PR #8 production baseline. A new local-only orchestration follow-up now derives LanguageTool's manual-only policy from the same catalog SSOT and enforces it for structured WebMCP runs. This follow-up is not published. Browser agents also receive the policy through stable DOM metadata and accessible text; they must not trigger the human-only control for manual-only providers.

Batch 2 local acceptance on 2026-09-05: 137/137 unit tests, including 27 diagnostic tests; Pages-base build and diff checks passed. Two real provider journeys (OpenSSF and NHTSA) and one explicitly synthetic LanguageTool journey passed at 1440/900/390/320 widths, named AX contracts and >=44px interactive controls. Full live recall narratives were compared to the response, all returned checks/campaigns were reachable, keyboard filters/disclosures and actual replacement clipboard readback passed without additional fetches. Eleven synthetic edge cases verified no-issues/empty/invalid/partial states and HTTP429. Removing the LanguageTool fixture explicitly tested fail-closed network blocking.

The prior six-card regression passed its six default live cases and Country smoke on the first batch-2 run, then stopped on a 20-second Weather timeout. Controlled follow-up returned successful Weather results twice and a successful GitHub result without source/request changes. Preserve the failed first-run evidence rather than relabeling it a clean pass. This remains point-in-time provider variability, not a full-catalog sweep.

Focused semantic remediation on 2026-09-06: RDAP Domain Lookup no longer routes through the generic DataTable composition. The catalog description promises registrar, nameserver, status, and registration-event context, while the prior card exposed only domain/status/handle. The local candidate now maps the live RDAP object into a dedicated semantic registration summary containing registrar identity, registration/expiration/last-changed dates, all returned nameservers, registry handle, and the complete status set. This is a semantic-correctness fix, not a provider/admission change; a Pages-origin RDAP.org domain probe remained browser-ready and exposed the expected structured fields. The preview profile now also reports the dedicated `domain-registration` layout instead of the stale generic `data-table` label, so visual composition and agent-readable `data-preview-layout` metadata describe the same semantics. The Pages-origin domain-card regression now replays the live RDAP default, compares domain/registrar/nameservers/status exactly against the provider response, checks the dedicated layout identifier, verifies 390px overflow behavior, and audits unnamed actionable accessibility nodes.

Focused semantic remediation on 2026-09-06: OSRM Route no longer routes through the generic DataTable composition. The prior live Pages-origin card reduced a successful route to `OSRM Route record 1` and surfaced low-level `legs`, `weight_name`, `geometry`, and `weight` while omitting the provider's actual distance, estimated duration, snapped endpoints, geometry size, and turn steps. The local candidate now uses a dedicated `route-summary` layout and semantic adapter. It exposes provider distance/duration, leg/step counts, geometry point count, snapped start/destination, weight profile, and a bounded turn-step list while retaining the complete response in Raw JSON. Stable `data-primary-*` values come from the same response model for browser-agent verification. The Pages-origin domain-card regression replays the live OSRM default and compares raw distance, duration, step count, and GeoJSON coordinate count exactly against the semantic DOM, plus 390px reflow and AX-name checks. This is a response-semantics fix; the OSRM request definition and browser admission status are unchanged.

Focused semantic remediation on 2026-09-06: GLEIF LEI Explorer no longer routes through the generic DataTable composition. A real Pages-origin default search returned legal name, LEI, active entity status, headquarters, jurisdiction, registration dates/status, mapped BIC identifiers, and relationship links, while the prior card showed only `type`, `id`, and nested `attributes`/`relationships` property counts. The local candidate now uses a dedicated `legal-entity` layout and maps those returned Level 1 identity/registration facts into semantic cards. Relationship tags describe only relationship links already advertised by the returned record and do not imply that parent/child entities were fetched. The catalog description is tightened accordingly. Stable `data-primary-*` metadata exposes the primary LEI, legal name, entity status, and registration status for browser-agent verification. The request definition and provider admission policy are unchanged.

Focused semantic/request-definition remediation on 2026-09-07: FDIC BankFind Suite had two aligned SSOT defects. The catalog field was labelled as a bank-name search, but its request builder sent an undocumented `q=Wells Fargo`; the live FDIC response ignored that parameter, reported no `search` parameter in `meta.parameters`, and returned the unfiltered institution universe. FDIC's current OpenAPI specification documents `search` for fuzzy institution-name search and uses the canonical `https://api.fdic.gov/banks/institutions` route. The local candidate now sends `search=NAME: WELLS FARGO` to that canonical endpoint, and live response metadata acknowledges the search. The generic nested record/property view is also replaced by a dedicated `bank-institution` composition exposing bank name, FDIC certificate, operating status, main office, establishment/insurance dates, reported assets/deposits in the provider's `$000s` units, office count, regulator, report date, bank class, and FDIC region. Stable machine metadata exposes the primary name, certificate, active state, assets/deposits, and office count. The catalog description no longer claims branch history from the institutions endpoint. This is a request/semantic correctness fix, not a provider-admission change.

Focused semantic remediation on 2026-09-07: openFDA Drug Labels no longer routes through the generic DataTable composition. Official openFDA documentation defines browser-callable product-label queries with `search=field:term` and documents the structured label fields. Its authentication page is internally inconsistent: it states that an API key is required, but the same page publishes explicit no-key request limits; the real GitHub-Pages-origin default request also succeeds without a key. A default `Advil` request returned 39 matching labels with harmonized brand, generic name, manufacturer, product type, route, active substances, plus label sections such as active ingredients, indications, warnings, and dosage directions. The prior generic card reduced those records to a few long text cells. The local candidate now uses a dedicated `drug-label` layout implemented in its own lazy preview module, exposes primary identity and provider-match metadata through stable `data-primary-*` attributes, presents bounded semantic label sections, and keeps the complete provider response in Raw JSON. The request definition and browser admission status are unchanged.

Semantic-layout metadata alignment on 2026-09-07: seven APIs already had dedicated semantic adapters but their preview profiles still reported the stale generic `data-table` layout. The candidate now aligns `data-gov-carpark` → `availability-board`, `met-museum-search` → `collection-index`, `nhtsa-vpic` → `manufacturer-directory`, `gbif-species-search` → `taxonomy-directory`, `openf1-historical` → `motorsport-results`, `animechan-random-quote` → `quote-card`, and `swapi-people` → `character-dossier`. No provider request or visual adapter changed; this repairs Agent-Readable DOM metadata so `data-preview-layout` describes the composition actually rendered. At that point the true generic `DataTablePreview` component count remained 47; the later VATComply remediation reduces it to 46, and the openFDA Food Recalls remediation reduces it to 45, the AlAdhan Prayer Times semantic remediation reduces it to 44, the RxNorm Drug Search remediation reduces it to 43, NOAA Tides & Currents reduces it to 42, UniProt Protein Lookup reduces it to 41, RCSB PDB Entry reduces it to 40, and ChEMBL Molecule Profile reduces it to 39, while PubChem Compound Lookup reduces it to 38.

VATComply semantic remediation on 2026-09-07: the catalog entry calls only VATComply’s documented `GET /rates` endpoint, but the prior description/profile implied VAT-number and IBAN validation and the generic card collapsed the returned `rates` object to a property count. The candidate narrows the catalog wording to the actual exchange-rate demo, reuses the existing `exchange-rates` semantic workbench through a VATComply-specific response model, preserves the provider’s base/date/rate values, and explicitly states that VAT/IBAN validation are separate endpoints. `data-preview-layout` now reports `exchange-rates`; no provider URL or request parameter changed.

Focused semantic remediation on 2026-09-07: openFDA Food Recalls no longer routes through the generic DataTable composition. A real GitHub-Pages-origin default request returned FDA enforcement records with recall number, classification, enforcement status, recalling firm, initiation/report dates, product description, reason, lot/code details, distribution pattern, and quantity, while the prior card rendered generic `openFDA Food Recalls record N` headings and only a few arbitrary early fields. The local candidate now uses a dedicated `food-recalls` layout implemented in its own lazy preview module. The primary enforcement record exposes stable `data-primary-*` identity/status metadata, full product/reason/code narratives, and bounded related returned records; Raw JSON keeps the complete response. This is a response-semantics change only: the admitted openFDA request definition and browser execution policy are unchanged.


Focused semantic remediation on 2026-09-07: AlAdhan Prayer Times no longer routes through the generic DataTable composition. The earlier date-path correction already kept the Human/agent SSOT as native `YYYY-MM-DD` and converted only at the provider boundary to AlAdhan’s `DD-MM-YYYY` path. A fresh real GitHub-Pages-origin response for the selected Singapore coordinates returned Fajr, Sunrise, Dhuhr, Asr, Maghrib, Isha, additional timing markers, Gregorian/Hijri dates, `Asia/Singapore`, coordinates, and Method 11 (`Majlis Ugama Islam Singapura, Singapore`). The old generic composition scattered timing rows and attached method/calendar context only to the first record. The candidate now uses a dedicated `prayer-schedule` layout and standalone semantic adapter: an ordered daily schedule with native `<time>` values, Gregorian/Hijri and timezone/method facts, bounded additional timing markers, and stable `data-primary-*`/calendar/method metadata for browser agents. The card also states AlAdhan's documented caveat that its Hijri date is mathematically calculated and can differ from locally adopted dates; it does not present that calendar value as a universal authority. The live browser regression compares the provider date echo and Fajr/Dhuhr/Asr/Maghrib/Isha values exactly with the DOM, verifies 390px reflow and unnamed-actionable accessibility nodes, and preserves Raw JSON for the complete response. This semantic follow-up does not change the provider request definition, CORS policy, or admission status.

Focused semantic remediation on 2026-09-07: RxNorm Drug Search no longer routes through the generic DataTable composition. NLM's documented `getDrugs` response groups associated products by RxNorm term type (`SCD`, `SBD`, `GPCK`, `BPCK`) and exposes stable RxCUI identifiers, concept names, and synonyms. A real GitHub-Pages-origin `ibuprofen` request succeeded first-pass and returned substantial SBD and SCD drug-product groups, while the prior generic card reduced the nested response to arbitrary record/property output. The local candidate now uses a dedicated `drug-terminology` layout and standalone semantic adapter that groups concepts by term type, preserves exact concept names/RxCUIs/synonyms, exposes stable query/result/primary-concept metadata for browser agents, bounds visible records while retaining Raw JSON, and keeps the existing medical-advice caution. The catalog usage note now also identifies NLM RxNorm as the data source. The provider request definition and browser admission status are unchanged.

Focused semantic/request-contract remediation on 2026-09-07: NOAA Tides & Currents no longer routes through the generic DataTable composition. A real GitHub-Pages-origin `date=latest` request for The Battery was browser-ready and returned station ID/name/coordinates plus a water-level observation with measured height (`v`), observation time (`t`), one-second sample sigma (`s`), data flags (`f`), and QA/QC level (`q`), but the prior card exposed only station name, height, timestamp, and the raw one-letter quality code. NOAA documents `water_level` as preliminary or verified six-minute water-level data, requires a datum for water-level products, defines `date=latest` as the last data point available within 18 minutes, and documents `q=p/v` plus the preliminary/verified flag meanings. The candidate now uses a dedicated `coastal-water-level` layout that preserves the exact returned station/height/time/sigma/flags, expands `MLLW` and GMT/metric request context semantically, humanizes the provider QA/QC level, and exposes stable station/measurement/quality metadata for browser agents. The request also now supplies NOAA's optional `application=Public_API_Workbench` identifier so the provider can distinguish this application in automated activity/error logs; NOAA explicitly documents this parameter as recommended for identification but not required. Browser admission remains based on the unchanged browser-native endpoint/CORS behavior. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 42.

Focused semantic remediation on 2026-09-07: UniProt Protein Lookup no longer routes through the generic DataTable composition. UniProt documents UniProtKB as the central protein-annotation knowledgebase, with reviewed Swiss-Prot and computationally annotated unreviewed TrEMBL entries; its REST return fields expose stable accession, protein/gene/organism identity, sequence length/mass, and functional annotations. A real GitHub-Pages-origin request for P05067 returned primary accession `P05067`, entry `A4_HUMAN`, reviewed Swiss-Prot status, APP gene, human taxonomy, sequence facts, and multiple `FUNCTION` comments, while the prior generic card showed only entry name, organism, entry type, and annotation score. The local candidate now uses a dedicated `protein-annotation` layout and standalone semantic adapter that preserves the stable primary accession, protein/gene/organism identity, provider entry type, annotation score, exact sequence length/molecular mass, bounded complete function comments, and stable `data-primary-*` metadata for browser agents. Raw JSON retains the full sequence/features/comments payload. The provider request definition and browser admission policy are unchanged. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 41.


Focused semantic remediation on 2026-09-07: RCSB Protein Data Bank Entry no longer routes through the generic DataTable composition. RCSB documents the Data API `core_entry` object as entry-level PDB/structure data following the mmCIF dictionary plus RCSB annotations, and the current provider response exposes experimental method and refinement resolution alongside archive/entity/citation metadata. A real GitHub-Pages-origin `4HHB` request succeeded first-pass before the change, while the prior generic card displayed only the primary citation title, journal, three authors, and DOI—omitting the catalog-promised experimental method and structure identity/quality facts. The local candidate now uses a dedicated `molecular-structure` layout and standalone semantic adapter that preserves the PDB entry ID and structure title, exact experimental method, refinement resolution, polymer/entity and deposited-atom counts, bound components, archive dates/status, and the complete returned primary-publication authors/DOI/PubMed identity. Stable `data-*` metadata exposes the primary structure facts for browser-agent verification. Raw JSON retains the complete core-entry response. This is a response-semantics change only: provider URL, request definition, browser admission, CORS, and execution policy are unchanged. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 40.


Focused semantic remediation on 2026-09-07: ChEMBL Molecule Profile no longer routes through the generic DataTable composition. The admitted `CHEMBL25` request was browser-ready before the change, but the generic card exposed only preferred name/ID, molecular formula/weight, maximum phase, and first approval year even though the same molecule resource returned molecule type, therapeutic/withdrawn/natural-product and administration flags, calculated molecular properties, canonical structure identifiers, ATC classifications, and cross references. ChEMBL documents its REST web services as programmatic access to its drug-discovery molecule/bioactivity data. The local candidate now uses a dedicated `molecule-profile` layout and standalone semantic adapter that preserves provider identity, development metadata, exact property strings, unknown-vs-false boolean semantics, SMILES/InChI identity, ATC codes, and bounded cross references while retaining the full raw response. Stable `data-*` metadata exposes the primary ChEMBL ID/name/type, phase/year, therapeutic/withdrawn flags, molecular formula/weight, and ATC count for browser-agent verification. This is a response-semantics change only: provider URL, request parameters, browser admission, CORS, and execution policy are unchanged. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 39.

Focused semantic remediation on 2026-09-07: PubChem Compound Lookup no longer routes through the generic DataTable composition. PubChem official PUG REST defines compound property tables with multiple requested property tags, including standard molecular formula, molecular weight in g/mol, and IUPAC name. The existing Pages-origin `aspirin` request was browser-ready before this change, but the response still used the generic record renderer. The local candidate now uses a dedicated `compound-properties` layout and standalone semantic adapter that preserves CID, IUPAC name, molecular formula, and molecular weight as one chemical reference card with stable machine metadata for browser agents. The request URL, requested provider fields, CORS/admission, execution policy, and Raw JSON remain unchanged. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 38.


Focused semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: Ensembl Gene Lookup no longer routes through the generic DataTable composition. Ensembl's documented `GET lookup/id/:id` endpoint resolves a stable identifier to species/database information and supports gene objects with full response fields; the current browser-ready default response for `ENSG00000157764` returns stable ID/version, BRAF symbol/description, protein-coding biotype, human species, GRCh38 assembly coordinates, strand, annotation source, and a canonical transcript. The prior generic card exposed only symbol, biotype, an unqualified chromosome range, and description, omitting the stable/versioned identity, assembly context, strand, species, and canonical transcript that make the locus interpretable. The local candidate now uses a dedicated `gene-locus` layout and standalone semantic adapter that preserves those exact provider fields, keeps assembly-bound coordinates explicit, shows canonical transcript only when supplied, and exposes stable `data-*` metadata for browser-agent verification. Raw JSON retains the complete lookup response. This is a response-semantics change only: provider URL, request parameters, browser admission, CORS, and execution policy are unchanged. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 37.

Focused semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: Carbon Intensity GB no longer routes through the generic DataTable composition. NESO documents `GET /intensity` as the current half-hour Great Britain record and returns separate `forecast` and estimated `actual` carbon-intensity values in gCO₂/kWh, an intensity index, and the full UTC `from`/`to` interval. A real GitHub-Pages-origin request reproduced the defect before the change: the provider returned forecast `78`, estimated actual `76`, index `low`, and a `02:30Z`–`03:00Z` interval, while the generic card displayed only the actual value and interval start, silently dropping the forecast and interval end. The local candidate now uses a dedicated `carbon-intensity` layout and standalone semantic adapter that preserves both provider values independently, the index, and the complete UTC interval, with matching stable `data-*` metadata for browser agents. If an estimated actual is not yet supplied, the forecast remains usable rather than being mislabelled as an actual. Raw JSON remains the complete source response. This is a response-semantics change only: provider URL, request definition, CORS/admission, and execution policy are unchanged. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 36.

Focused semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: Open-Meteo Elevation no longer routes through the generic DataTable composition. Open-Meteo documents `/v1/elevation` as accepting required WGS84 latitude/longitude coordinates while a successful JSON response contains only an `elevation` array; the official source is Copernicus DEM 2021 GLO-90 at 90 m resolution and the provider requires clear attribution to both the Copernicus programme and Open-Meteo. A real GitHub-Pages-origin request reproduced a semantic defect before the change: the default Singapore request returned `{ elevation: [46] }`, but the generic adapter looked for latitude/longitude inside that response and rendered both coordinates as `—`, disconnecting the terrain value from the point that produced it. The local candidate now uses a dedicated `terrain-elevation` layout and standalone adapter that pairs the provider elevation with the exact request URL coordinates, exposes stable elevation/coordinate/dataset metadata for browser agents, keeps the 90 m dataset context visible, and updates the SSOT usage note with the current attribution requirement. Provider URL, request parameters, browser admission, CORS, and structured-execution policy are unchanged. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 35.

Focused provider-contract + semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: the stable `usgs-water-legacy` catalog ID is retained for deep-link compatibility, but its request no longer uses the retiring WaterServices `/nwis/iv/` endpoint. USGS released the modern Water Data API V1 on 2026-09-04 and directs WaterServices applications to migrate before WaterServices is decommissioned in early 2027. The candidate now calls `/ogcapi/v1/collections/latest-continuous/items` and explicitly filters both `monitoring_location_id` and `parameter_code`, with a bounded selector for streamflow/discharge (`00060`) or gage height (`00065`). This fixes a semantic flaw in the prior request/card pair: the legacy request asked for every instantaneous-value time series at a site while the generic adapter silently rendered only the first series, so the displayed measurement could be unrelated to the user's intended river-gauge quantity. The new dedicated `water-gauge` adapter preserves the provider value/unit, parameter, observation time, provisional/approved status, qualifier, monitoring-location identity, time-series ID, and coordinates in shared Human/Agent-readable DOM. A direct V1 probe with the GitHub Pages Origin returned HTTP 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`; API keys remain optional and only raise rate limits. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 34.

Focused provider-contract + semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: UK Flood Monitoring no longer treats a successful station-list response as a generic flood/readings card. The Environment Agency reference documents `riverName` as an **exact-match** station filter; the prior default `Thames` therefore returned HTTP 200 with an empty `items` array from the real GitHub Pages origin, while the catalog description also overclaimed alerts/readings that this `/id/stations` request does not return. The local candidate changes the default to the documented canonical-style value `River Severn`, tightens the field/help/usage semantics around exact matching, and adds a dedicated `flood-stations` adapter. A real Pages-origin request returned seven River Severn stations and the card preserves each station label/reference, exact river name, town/catchment, WGS84 coordinates, provider operational status, and available measure metadata while explicitly stating that live readings and flood warnings are separate API resources. Mobile 390px and accessibility-tree checks passed with zero unnamed actionable controls. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 33.

Focused provider-contract + semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: Eurostat Population Statistics no longer freezes the demo at reference year 2023 or renders a generic record. Eurostat's current `demo_pjan` dataset is “Population on 1 January by age and sex”, reports unit `NR` (Number), and currently covers through 2025; the dataset page shows a 2026-08-14 data update. The local candidate moves the default/max reference year from 2023 to 2025 and adds a dedicated `population-statistic` adapter that preserves the exact filtered cell together with geography, reference date, unit, total-age/total-sex dimensions, annual frequency, dataset update timestamp, and any provider status flag. This fixes both stale input capability and semantic context loss. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 32.

Focused provider-contract + semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: UNHCR Refugee Statistics now makes its ISO input contract executable instead of merely labelling the field “ISO”. UNHCR documents that `coo` uses UNHCR country codes by default and requires `cf_type=ISO` for ISO3 searches; the previous request omitted that flag while telling users to enter ISO codes. The local candidate sets `cf_type=ISO`, constrains the origin field to exactly three characters, advances the default year to the currently returned 2025 end-of-year snapshot, and removes the meaningless result-count input because omitting country of asylum causes UNHCR to aggregate that dimension into one row. The new `refugee-population` adapter preserves ISO/UNHCR origin identity, reporting year, refugees, asylum-seekers, IDPs, stateless people, returned refugees, and returned IDPs. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 31.

Focused provider-contract + semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: FEMA Disaster Declarations no longer presents each `DisasterDeclarationsSummaries` row as if it were a distinct disaster. OpenFEMA's area-level dataset repeats one federal declaration across its designated geographic areas; a live Pages-origin request reproduced this directly, with all five newest rows sharing `DR-4943-KS` while naming different Kansas counties. The candidate now describes the request as declared-area rows and uses a dedicated `disaster-declared-areas` adapter that preserves federal declaration ID/number, designated area, state, declaration and incident types, declaration/incident dates, assistance-program flags, FEMA region, and provider refresh. Stable metadata exposes both row count and derived unique-declaration count so humans and agents cannot confuse repeated area rows with separate disasters. The card also preserves OpenFEMA's caveat that the underlying raw NEMIS data can contain a small percentage of human error. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 30.

Focused provider-contract + semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: Malaysia Core CPI fixes both a silently ignored input and a dataset-shape error. The catalog field is named `limit`, but the previous request builder destructured `count`, so changing the visible Records control never changed the provider request. More importantly, `cpi_core` is a monthly CPI **index** with base 2010=100 and one row per `date + division`; the old unfiltered `limit=12` could truncate an arbitrary subset of one month's 14 division rows and present them as a time series. The candidate now reads the real `limit` input, sends data.gov.my's exact `filter=overall@division`, bounds 6–60 monthly observations, and uses a dedicated `core-cpi-index` adapter that exposes the latest overall index/date and the complete bounded date/index trend in semantic DOM as well as a labeled sparkline. The card explicitly states that the returned number is an index level, not an inflation percentage, and preserves DOSM's 2010=100/core-CPI context. A live Pages-origin browser run changed Months to 18 and received exactly 18 numeric `overall` rows, proving both the input and provider filter are effective. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 29.

Focused semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: UK Food Hygiene Ratings now gives the Food Standards Agency response an explicit rating model instead of generic records. The live API returns overall rating, rating date, local authority, address/geocode, scheme type, new-rating-pending state, and FHRS intervention component scores. Those scores have an easy-to-invert meaning: for FHRS the overall 0–5 rating is higher-is-better, while Hygiene/Structural/Confidence intervention scores are lower-is-better; FHIS does not use those component scores, and scores can be absent after a rescore. The new `food-hygiene-ratings` adapter preserves provider count, FHRS ID, business/rating/inspection/authority/location identity, exposes `data-score-direction=lower-intervention-score-is-better`, maps documented component-score meanings only for FHRS, and fails closed rather than fabricating components for FHIS. The request also makes first-page semantics explicit with `pageNumber=1`. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 28.

Focused provider-contract + semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: Malaysia Household Income fixes the same ignored-input pattern found in Core CPI: the visible field is `limit`, while the old builder read `count`, so changing Records never affected the request. The route now reads the real `limit` field and uses a dedicated `household-income` adapter. It preserves DOSM HIES mean and median gross monthly household income in nominal RM, exposes the survey observation years explicitly, and states that the values are not inflation-adjusted and are not a complete consecutive annual series. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 27.

Focused provider-contract + semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: Malaysia Population no longer truncates arbitrary rows from the latest multidimensional year. The source is keyed by `date + sex + age + ethnicity` and reports population in thousands of people; the old unfiltered `limit=10` mixed demographic slices from one year. The request now uses `filter=both@sex,overall@age,overall@ethnicity`, reads the real `limit` field, and the dedicated `population-total` adapter preserves raw thousand-person values plus their exact people conversion as one national total per year. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 26.

Focused provider-contract + semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: IFRC GO Emergency Events now orders the official `/api/v2/event/` list by `-disaster_start_date` rather than event-record creation time and no longer collapses field-report impacts into one ambiguous number. The dedicated `humanitarian-events` adapter keeps event identity, disaster type, countries, severity, start date, GLIDE, deployments and sanitized summary, then separates latest field-report IFRC, government and other-source affected/dead/displaced figures. Missing or zero figures from one source are never presented as an event-wide humanitarian total. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 25.


Catalog-wide request-control binding remediation on 2026-09-08: an AST-backed audit found 12 routes where the shared Request Lab control exposed machine field `limit` but the request builder still destructured the old name `count`, making the Human UI and WebMCP parameter appear editable while the provider request silently kept its default. The candidate repairs Packagist (`per_page`), AniList (`variables.perPage`), Openverse (`page_size`), Apple iTunes (`limit`), Jolpica F1 (`limit`), HN Algolia (`hitsPerPage`), Swiss Transit (`limit`), openFDA Food Recalls (`limit`), Iconify (`limit`), and UK Parliament (`limit`). OSRM's semantically different route-alternative count now has stable field ID `alternatives` instead of pretending to be a generic result limit. Iconify's control is aligned to its documented minimum of 32 results while retaining the local browser payload cap of 60. A permanent catalog invariant now requires every declared `limit` field to change either the provider URL or POST body when a valid alternate value is supplied. A second catalog-wide runtime audit exercised 275 alternate field values; six initial no-op candidates were normalization false positives and all six changed the request when retested with valid alternate dates/postcode values. No second material no-op input class was found.

OpenDota provider-contract + semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: `/proMatches?limit=3`, `?limit=8`, and `?limit=20` each returned the same 100-row provider batch in direct browser-origin research, while the current Swagger-derived contract documents only optional `less_than_match_id` pagination for older match IDs. The candidate therefore removes the fabricated Matches-limit control and uses the fixed `/api/proMatches` endpoint rather than exposing a parameter the provider ignores. The former generic table is also replaced by a dedicated `pro-match-results` adapter. OpenDota's response schema defines `radiant_score` and `dire_score` as match-end kill counts and `radiant_win` as the winner flag, so the card labels the scores as kills, keeps winner identity separate, preserves match/team/league/series IDs, converts Unix start time exactly to UTC ISO, retains duration seconds in machine metadata, and leaves numeric `series_type` as an uninterpreted provider code instead of inventing a best-of format. The UI bounds visible matches to 12 while Raw JSON retains the complete provider response. The live Pages-origin regression compared the first returned match to semantic DOM exactly, verified the fixed no-query endpoint, 390px overflow, and accessibility-tree names. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now 24.


USAspending provider-contract + semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: two long-standing generic routes mixed agency metadata with spending terminology and obscured the award-search request semantics. `GET /api/v2/agency/020/` is the Agency Details **overview** endpoint; its live Treasury response supplies agency identity, fiscal-year context, mission, subtier count, official links, and Disaster Emergency Fund Codes, but it does not itself return award obligations or budgetary-resource totals. The candidate therefore renames the route to `U.S. Treasury Agency Overview`, removes the old “federal spending profile” overclaim, and uses a dedicated `federal-agency-overview` adapter with stable agency/fiscal-year/subtier/DEFC metadata and accessible official-reference links. `POST /api/v2/search/spending_by_award/` previously hard-coded the previous calendar year, requested only Award ID / Recipient Name / Award Amount, and left sorting to the provider default, so the generic card showed bare numbers and internal IDs without date, currency, agency, contract-type, or filter context. The candidate now exposes `fiscalYear` plus bounded `limit`, converts a selected federal fiscal year to October 1 through September 30, and explicitly sends `date_type=new_awards_only`. That qualifier is material: USAspending documents asymmetric `action_date`/`date_signed` defaults when `date_type` is omitted, while `new_awards_only` guarantees that only awards whose base transaction date falls within the selected period are returned. The request keeps prime contract award codes A-D explicit, requests Base Obligation Date / awarding and funding agencies / contract type / description, and sorts by Base Obligation Date descending. USAspending's current contract mapping defines `Award Amount` as `total_obligation` and Base Obligation Date as `date_signed`; the dedicated `federal-awards` adapter therefore labels the value as award-level total obligation rather than a transaction amount or potential award ceiling, preserves pagination/messages, and exposes stable award/date/agency metadata. A real Pages-origin FY2024 run returned five provider-acknowledged results, every Base Obligation Date stayed within `2023-10-01..2024-09-30`, dates were descending, and the first award's identity/total obligation/date/agencies matched semantic DOM exactly. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now **22**.

Wikidata provider-policy + semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: the public WDQS endpoint remained browser-ready from the real GitHub Pages origin, but the generic card rendered `Wikidata SPARQL record N` plus raw entity URIs and did not preserve QID identity as a first-class semantic fact. Wikimedia's current external-endpoint guidance says automated clients should follow API etiquette and use identifiable client information; its API access policy explicitly provides `Api-User-Agent` for browser JavaScript that cannot control `User-Agent`. A Pages-origin probe confirmed WDQS accepts the `Api-User-Agent: Public-API/0.1 (https://yapweijun1996.github.io/Public-API/)` header with HTTP 200 and eight JSON SPARQL bindings. The catalog now owns that identification header plus `Accept: application/sparql-results+json`, and a shared-runtime regression proves those headers enter the actual fetch initialization. The dedicated `knowledge-entities` adapter preserves each returned entity URI, stable QID, English label, label language, and declared SPARQL variables, while explicitly describing the response as a bounded query result rather than a complete/ranked city list. A 2026-09-11 local-only semantic-honesty follow-up now treats only a contract-valid `results.bindings: []` response as `empty`; missing `results.bindings` or rows without a provider-owned Wikidata entity URI/QID plus literal label fail closed as `invalid`, while mixed valid/malformed rows and incomplete English-label metadata remain visible only as `partial`. The real GitHub Pages origin still returned eight `ready` city bindings with exact QID/URI/label/language mapping, and synthetic HTTP-200 malformed/mixed fixtures proved invalid identities are not rendered as plausible entities; 390px reflow and actionable-control accessibility remained clean. This follow-up remains unpublished local work.

OpenCitations semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: `/index/v2/citation-count/doi:{doi}` returns only an index-scoped incoming-citation count, so the prior generic card displayed `98` without retaining which DOI produced that number. The dedicated `citation-count` adapter now derives the DOI from the exact request URL and binds it to the provider count in both visible semantic DOM and stable `data-*` metadata. The card explicitly says the count is scoped to OpenCitations Index v2 rather than a universal citation total. The SSOT usage note records the official anonymous API limit of 180 requests/minute per IP and the provider's large-scale-dump recommendation. A live Pages-origin default returned count `98` for DOI `10.1109/5.771073`, and the domain regression verified exact DOI/count/index semantics, 390px reflow, and zero unnamed actionable controls. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now **20**.

CelesTrak semantic-honesty hardening on 2026-09-11: the dedicated `satellite-orbits` adapter no longer uses permissive row coercion that could turn a malformed HTTP-200 object or an all-malformed GP batch into a plausible empty result, or silently keep a mixed batch `ready`. The response must now be a JSON array; a genuine empty array is `empty`, a non-array or a non-empty batch with no usable OMM-style records is `invalid`, and mixed valid/malformed records are `partial`. Trusted rows require provider-owned `NORAD_CAT_ID`, `EPOCH`, and the core OMM mean-element values used by the card (`MEAN_MOTION`, `ECCENTRICITY`, `INCLINATION`, `RA_OF_ASC_NODE`, `ARG_OF_PERICENTER`, `MEAN_ANOMALY`); malformed records are omitted rather than replaced with plausible orbital values. The semantic root now exposes provider/valid/invalid record counts for browser agents. Verification remained policy-safe: the existing exact-URL Pages-origin CelesTrak harness used only official-documentation-shaped synthetic fixtures, including malformed-envelope and mixed-row HTTP-200 cases, and sent **zero live CelesTrak requests** because the catalog SSOT remains cadence-limited to one automated verification per 7200 seconds with no retry after non-2xx. The focused CelesTrak suite passed **5/5**, the complete Vitest gate passed **353/353 across 52 files**, `npm run build:pages` passed, 390px reflow and accessibility checks passed, and `git diff --check` remained clean. This is unpublished local-candidate evidence, not a new live-provider health claim.

UN SDG + CelesTrak semantic/provider-policy remediation on 2026-09-08: `un-sdg-goals` no longer routes through the generic DataTable composition. The official UN Statistics Division `Goal/List` endpoint returned the full latest-release 17-goal catalogue from the real GitHub Pages origin; the dedicated `sdg-goals` adapter preserves every returned goal code, title, full description, and provider URI in semantic DOM, exposes stable goal-count/code metadata, and explicitly avoids implying that target/indicator/series/observation resources were included. `celestrak-satellites` keeps its stable catalog ID but is renamed `CelesTrak Orbital Elements` because the GP JSON response is orbital-element data at a supplied epoch rather than a live tracker. The dedicated `satellite-orbits` adapter maps CelesTrak's CCSDS OMM-style JSON fields to NORAD/object identity, epoch, mean motion in rev/day, eccentricity, inclination, node/pericenter/anomaly angles, classification, element-set number, and revolution-at-epoch metadata; it explicitly states that these values are not real-time satellite positions. A provider-policy audit also tightened the Human/WebMCP selector from `stations/starlink/gps-ops/active` to the bounded `stations/gps-ops` set because CelesTrak asks clients to retrieve only needed data once per update and specifically identifies the large Active and Starlink datasets in enforcement guidance. The existing `automatedVerification` contract remains cadence-limited at 7200 seconds with no retry after non-200. This run therefore did **not** send a live CelesTrak request: Pages-origin browser verification used an explicitly synthetic official-documentation-shaped ISS OMM fixture intercepted at the exact request URL and proved raw-to-semantic-DOM identity, 390px reflow, zero unnamed actionable controls, and zero live provider contact. The UN route used a live provider response and matched all 17 records exactly. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now **18**.


Biodiversity semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: `obis-marine-occurrences`, `worms-species-lookup`, and `paleobiodb-taxa` no longer use the generic DataTable composition. A real Pages-origin OBIS response showed that the old card dropped material Darwin Core occurrence context: `occurrenceStatus`, `basisOfRecord`, WGS84 coordinates, source dataset, WoRMS identity, and provider QC flags. The dedicated `marine-occurrences` adapter keeps those fields attached to each occurrence; a QC flag such as `ON_LAND` remains a provider review signal rather than being converted into an unsupported invalid-record judgment. WoRMS exposed an even clearer semantic defect: the live exact query `Manta birostris` returns the queried AphiaRecord as `unaccepted` with `valid_AphiaID=1026118` and current accepted name `Mobula birostris`; the old generic card showed only the old name/status and hid the accepted-name resolution. The dedicated `marine-taxonomy` adapter now preserves queried and accepted identities separately, keeps unknown habitat/extinction flags distinct from false, and exposes stable Aphia metadata. PBDB's official `taxa/list` documentation defines `n_occs` as fossil occurrences identified as belonging to the taxon **or any of its subtaxa**; the dedicated `fossil-taxon` adapter therefore labels that scope explicitly instead of presenting an ambiguous `Occurrences` number, while also preserving accepted-name identity, rank, extancy, parent/reference IDs, and stable taxon IDs. Targeted Pages-origin health passed 3/3 first-pass with zero retries, and a permanent biodiversity E2E matched live provider values to semantic DOM exactly at desktop/mobile widths with zero unnamed actionable controls. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now **15**.


IP geolocation + administrative-boundary semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: `ipwhois-lookup` no longer routes through the generic DataTable composition. The live free ipwho.is response for `8.8.8.8` supplies IP type, approximate WGS84 location, country/region codes, timezone, ASN, organization, ISP and network domain, while the old generic card retained only city/country/IP/ISP/timezone. The dedicated `ip-geolocation` adapter keeps those provider facts together and explicitly labels the coordinates as approximate network-derived geolocation rather than device GPS or a precise personal address. The provider's free endpoint allows 1,000 requests/day and counts browser CORS traffic per domain, so the SSOT usage note now records that Public-API shares the domain quota. A separate live reserved-address probe reproduced another important contract: `127.0.0.1` returns HTTP 200 with `success=false` and `message=Reserved range`; the semantic card now fails closed to `data-result-state=invalid` instead of making an HTTP-success body look like a valid geolocation result.

`geoboundaries-admin-boundaries` also moves from generic `data-table` to dedicated `boundary-layer` semantics. The provider endpoint returns layer metadata and download URLs, not an embedded GeoJSON polygon, so the catalog description no longer claims the response itself loads polygons. Official geoBoundaries documentation defines `admUnitCount` as the count of administrative units and `meanAreaSqKM` / `meanPerimeterLengthKM` as means across units; the old generic card's `area_sq_km` label could therefore be misread as total country area. The new card labels those metrics as per-unit means, preserves layer ID, represented year, source/build dates, source provenance and original source license, and exposes accessible Full GeoJSON / Simplified GeoJSON / TopoJSON / archive links. The admin-level selector now reflects the provider's documented ADM0–ADM5 vocabulary while warning that level availability varies by country. A live Singapore ADM1 Pages-origin request returned 5 units with exact provider mean area/perimeter and download URLs. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now **13**.

Jolpica F1 semantic remediation on 2026-09-08: `jolpica-f1` no longer routes three structurally different provider datasets through one generic property dump. A live Pages-origin default driver request reproduced the defect: the response supplied stable driver ID, given/family name, number, code, birth date and nationality, while the generic card was titled `Jolpica F1 Data record 1` and stopped after early properties, omitting the actual driver name and nationality. The same Request Lab selector can return `DriverTable`, `ConstructorTable`, or `RaceTable`, so the candidate now uses a dedicated `f1-season-catalog` adapter that detects the returned dataset and preserves the corresponding semantics. Drivers keep provider ID, full name, number, code, nationality and birth date; constructors keep provider ID/name/nationality; races keep round, race/circuit identity, locality/country, WGS84 coordinates and provider date/time, with UTC `Z` timestamps kept explicit. The card states that these routes are season participation/calendar catalogues rather than standings or race-result endpoints. A permanent Pages-origin verifier exercised all three live 2025 datasets with `limit=3`, matched primary identities and race timing/circuit/coordinates exactly to raw provider responses, verified 390px reflow, and found zero unnamed actionable controls. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now **12**.

Sports schedule semantic remediation on 2026-09-09: `openligadb-matches` and `mlb-stats-api` no longer use the generic DataTable composition. OpenLigaDB's official API exposes machine-readable result kinds such as `HalfTime`, `After90Minutes`, `AfterExtraTime`, and `AfterPenalties`; a real Pages-origin Bundesliga matchday response returned both halftime and after-90-minute result records for the same match. The dedicated `football-matchday` adapter keeps provider match/team IDs, UTC and provider-local kickoff context, finished state, goals returned, and the typed result set while selecting the most final typed result available for the headline score instead of flattening multiple result stages together. MLB's browser-ready Stats schedule surface is undocumented by MLB and remains marked Review/unofficial in the catalog; the dedicated `baseball-schedule` adapter therefore limits its claim to the returned schedule object and preserves gamePk, UTC start, status, home/away identities, venue, W-L records, series context, innings, and scores only when the schedule response supplies them. Scheduled games with no provider score render a dash rather than inventing 0–0. A permanent live Pages-origin verifier matched OpenLigaDB match/result semantics and an MLB 2025-04-15 completed schedule game exactly to raw responses, verified 390px reflow and zero unnamed actionable controls. Both routes remain in the existing Sports async bundle, so no second API-to-bundle registry was added. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now **10**.


UK Parliament Members provider-contract + semantic remediation on 2026-09-09: `uk-parliament-members` had a silently ignored result-count control. The Human/WebMCP field allowed 1–50 `limit`, but the official Members Search contract uses `take` with a maximum of 20. A real GitHub Pages-origin baseline set the visible count to 3 with a broad name query and still received 20 rows with provider `take=20`, proving the stale `limit=3` parameter was ignored. The candidate now bounds the shared count field to 1–20 and translates it at the request boundary to official `Name`, `skip=0`, and `take`. The same Pages-origin browser run after the fix returned exactly 3 rows with provider `take=3`, so the provider acknowledged the intended input. The route also moves from generic `data-table` to dedicated `parliament-members` semantics: current Commons/Lords member ID and display/full title, party identity, latest house membership, provider-supplied membership-from value, membership dates/status, plus `totalResults`/`take`/`skip` context are preserved in semantic DOM and stable machine metadata. The card explicitly does not infer parliamentary offices/roles or invent a constituency/location when the Lords/current-members response omits one. Targeted mobile and accessibility verification passed at 390 px with zero unnamed actionable controls. Final gates after this change: Vitest **233/233 PASS** across 40 files; `npm run build:pages`, preview-bundle isolation/CSS-budget/failure-recovery, native WebMCP/provider-policy, and `git diff --check` all PASS; the machine/agent invariants remain **195 catalog / 193 runnable / 192 automated-verification-eligible**. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now **9**.

Preview orchestration modularization on 2026-09-08: after the dedicated-adapter expansion made `responsePreview.tsx` a second implementation monolith, the local candidate moved the existing shared media/location/calendar/developer/security/research families plus generic result fallback into `src/previews/CatalogFamilyPreviews.tsx`, and moved the remaining compact/specialized catalog compositions plus bounded `DataTablePreview` into `src/previews/SpecializedCatalogPreviews.tsx`. The implementation layer fell from the prior 1,817-line mixed registry/implementation file to a bounded registry/composition module; after the later on-demand bundle declarations, `responsePreview.tsx` remains a bounded registry/composition module and still contains no API-specific `*Preview` implementation other than `ResponseDemoPreview`. A permanent architecture regression test enforces that boundary.

Preview runtime code-splitting follow-up on 2026-09-08: the static-import candidate that emitted one approximately 313.74 kB response-preview JS chunk is replaced locally by seven on-demand implementation bundles behind the same synchronous `apiPreviewComponents` / `apiSsotCardRegistry` identities: Catalog Family, Specialized Catalog, Weather, Operational, Market, Semantic, and Diagnostic. The Pages build now emits a 56.28 kB response-preview orchestration chunk plus one selected implementation family (currently about 8.61 kB Market, 10.12 kB Operational, 15.50 kB Weather, 31.47 kB Specialized, 32.62 kB Diagnostic, 45.66 kB Catalog Family, or 114.21 kB Semantic) and small shared primitives as needed. The browser verifier starts from the real GitHub Pages origin with explicit synthetic provider fixtures, proves that the selected result loads only its expected implementation family, and records decoded result assets between about 104 kB and 216 kB across representative families; those fixtures are performance/architecture evidence, not provider-health evidence. Vite continues to own async chunk/CSS loading; no second API-to-adapter registry was introduced.

The first draft of the loader suspended by manually throwing a bundle Promise from render. That path was replaced before acceptance because React documents `lazy` as the supported component-code loading integration for Suspense, while custom Suspense data-source requirements are unstable. First-use browser rendering now uses `React.lazy`; the existing module cache provides a synchronous fast path after a bundle has already resolved (and for unit tests, whose setup intentionally preloads all bundles). A regression test rejects the former `throw loadPreviewBundle(bundle)` pattern. Final acceptance: Vitest 224/224 PASS, Pages build and `git diff --check` PASS, seven-family Pages-origin bundle isolation PASS, native WebMCP/provider-policy PASS at 195 catalog / 193 runnable / 192 automated-verification-eligible, and real Pages-origin health for Color, GitHub, and Jolpica passed 3/3 first-pass with zero retries. The largest remaining preview implementation family is `SemanticPreviewBundle` at about 114.21 kB (29.39 kB gzip), so any further splitting should be measured against request/waterfall/parse cost rather than pursued only to create more files.

Preview chunk failure containment on 2026-09-08: the accepted on-demand preview architecture introduced a new failure mode that did not exist when every preview implementation lived in one already-loaded chunk. `React.lazy` rejection previously had no local Error Boundary around Request Lab, so an implementation-chunk network/deploy failure could escape the semantic-preview surface even though the provider request and Raw JSON had already succeeded. The candidate now wraps the semantic result in `PreviewLoadBoundary`: a rejected preview chunk or render failure becomes an accessible `Semantic preview unavailable` alert, Request Lab stays in `success`, the parameter form and Raw JSON remain usable, and the recovery action is an explicit `Reload application` rather than depending on same-document dynamic-import retry behavior. The boundary resets on the shared request runtime’s next successful run ID, including POST APIs whose URL stays constant while the request body changes. The preview-bundle pending cache also drops settled promises so a rejected loader is not retained indefinitely. A permanent architecture regression requires the boundary and cache cleanup. The Pages-origin bundle verifier now injects a one-shot failure into the local `DiagnosticPreviewBundle` asset after a successful synthetic Color API response, proves semantic-preview containment + Raw JSON retention + named reload action with zero live-provider requests, then performs a real page reload and verifies the same `color-swatch` card recovers. This is chunk/deployment reliability evidence, not provider-health evidence. Final gates after this change: Vitest **227/227 PASS** across 38 files, Pages build and `git diff --check` PASS, seven-family bundle isolation + injected failure recovery PASS, native WebMCP/provider-policy PASS at **195 catalog / 193 runnable / 192 automated-verification-eligible**, and a fresh Pages-origin health check for Color, GitHub, and Jolpica passed **3/3 first-pass with zero retries**.

Hugging Face model-search provider-contract + semantic remediation on 2026-09-09: `models-dev` previously sent `full=false` while the current Hub model endpoint interprets the presence of `full` as the expanded/full response path. A real GitHub Pages-origin comparison showed the same one-result search at about 507 response characters with `full` omitted versus about 1,552 with either `full=false` or `full=true`, and the expanded shape adds `author`, `gated`, `lastModified`, `sha`, and `siblings`. Because the Public-API contract already promises update timestamps and renders `lastModified`, the candidate now declares the intended expanded contract explicitly with `full=true` instead of depending on contradictory presence semantics. The route also moves from generic `data-table` to dedicated `ai-model-catalog` semantics preserving model ID, author, pipeline task, library, access/gating state, provider `license:*` tag, downloads, likes, last-modified date, and bounded provider tags. The card states that provider access/license metadata is not a suitability or unrestricted-reuse guarantee. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now **8**.

Canada Open Data semantic remediation on 2026-09-09: `canada-open-data-search` no longer uses generic `data-table`. The Government of Canada portal exposes a CKAN catalogue that can return both datasets and open-information/publication records, so the dedicated `open-data-catalog` adapter preserves stable record ID, provider `type`, collection, publisher, licence/restrictions, published and portal-release dates, bilingual English/French title/notes, plus resource formats/languages/types without implying every match is a dataset. The existing documented CKAN `package_search?q=...&rows=...` request contract was retained because live Pages-origin research did not show an ignored parameter. A live `climate` request with `rows=3` returned exactly 3 of 2,863 matches and matched the first record's dataset/collection/licence/resources into semantic DOM at 390 px with zero unnamed actionable controls. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes became **7**.

Iconify + Homebrew semantic remediation on 2026-09-09: live GitHub Pages-origin baselines proved both generic cards were dropping material provider meaning. Iconify `/search?query=home&limit=32` returned 32 `prefix:name` icon identifiers plus `collections` metadata carrying icon-set name, author and licence (including SPDX), while the old card rendered one `Iconify Search record 1` and stopped at the icon array/count. The dedicated `icon-catalog` adapter now binds each visible identifier to its returned collection identity, author and licence, preserves query/limit/start metadata, and marks the provider's documented `returned count == limit` pagination signal without claiming the SVG body was returned. Homebrew was worse: a live formula lookup for `node` rendered almost no useful generic metadata, while a cask lookup for `postman` surfaced uninstall/app/zap artifact objects as separate generic records. The dedicated `homebrew-package` adapter now distinguishes formula and cask provider shapes: formulae preserve package/tap/version/licence/bottle and dependency semantics; casks preserve token/name/version/auto-update/macOS requirement and install-artifact semantics while leaving uninstall/zap detail in Raw JSON. The Human/WebMCP field copy now calls the input an exact Homebrew formula/cask **token** and explicitly says the endpoint is a direct lookup, not free-text search; the request URLs themselves are unchanged. Permanent unit tests cover Iconify, formula and cask shapes, and a live Pages-origin browser regression matched all three flows raw-response → semantic DOM exactly, passed 390px reflow, and found zero unnamed actionable controls. Final gates after this change: Vitest **239/239 PASS** across 44 files; Pages-base build, preview-bundle isolation/CSS budgets/failure recovery, native WebMCP/provider-policy, and `git diff --check` PASS; machine/agent invariants remain **195 catalog / 193 runnable / 192 automated-verification-eligible**. The Specialized async implementation is now about **41.59 kB JS / 11.78 kB gzip**, a bounded increase that does not justify another bundle boundary on its own. Generic `DataTablePreview` component routes are now **5**.

Open5e provider-contract + semantic remediation on 2026-09-09: the remaining `open5e-monster-search` demo no longer depends on the provider's legacy V1 monster endpoint. Current Open5e documentation marks V1 deprecated and V2 actively maintained, with V2 creature name filtering expressed as `name__icontains` and optional `fields` / `document__fields` response projection. The local candidate now uses `/v2/creatures/`, performs case-insensitive partial-name matching, caps the browser payload at eight source records, and requests only the identity/source/combat/movement/perception fields consumed by the card. The dedicated `monster-statblock` adapter preserves creature key/name, size/type, challenge rating, armor class, hit points/dice, movement, alignment, passive perception, source document, and game-system identity; repeated familiar monster names remain distinct when different source documents or game systems provide separate records. A dedicated live Pages-origin verifier checks the V2 request contract and raw-response-to-semantic-DOM identity, plus 390 px reflow and accessibility-tree control naming. The bounded generic `DataTablePreview` implementation was also cleaned of stale branches for APIs that already migrated to dedicated semantic adapters, so it now contains only actually routed generic cases.

Datamuse provider-contract + semantic remediation on 2026-09-09: the stable `datamuse-rhymes` catalog ID is retained for deep-link compatibility, but the current Datamuse documentation no longer lists the old `rel_rhy` relation code even though a live legacy request still returned HTTP 200. The candidate therefore stops depending on that undocumented behavior and uses the documented `sl` sounds-like constraint with `max=8`, `md=psr`, and `ipa=1`. The dedicated `lexical-matches` adapter preserves returned word order, syllable count, part-of-speech tags and IPA pronunciation; provider `score` is explicitly labelled as ordering-only because Datamuse says its magnitude is not otherwise interpretable. The usage contract now also surfaces Datamuse attribution and the known lifecycle boundary: current keyless access continues only through 2026-12-31, with API keys required for every request starting 2027-01-01, so browser-native admission must be re-evaluated before that date. Real Pages-origin verification returned eight sounds-like records and matched response word/pronunciation/POS values into semantic DOM exactly, with 390 px no-overflow and zero unnamed actionable controls. Final gates: Vitest **242/242 PASS** across 46 files; Pages-base build, preview-bundle isolation/CSS budgets/failure recovery, native WebMCP/provider-policy, targeted live Datamuse E2E, and `git diff --check` PASS. Machine/agent invariants remain **195 catalog / 193 runnable / 192 automated-verification-eligible**.

Newton provider-contract + semantic remediation on 2026-09-09: `newton-math-solver` no longer pays a redirect through the legacy `newton.now.sh` hostname and no longer renders a generic property card. The upstream Newton repository still documents the V2 path contract as `/api/v2/:operation/:expression`, while the legacy hostname now redirects to `newton.vercel.app`; direct live probes from the GitHub Pages origin returned CORS `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` and the expected `{ operation, expression, result }` JSON for simplify/factor/derive/zeroes.
