The candidate now targets the Vercel deployment directly, keeps the community-maintained service at `Review`, and uses dedicated `symbolic-math` semantics that preserve the provider-returned operation, input expression, and result (including bounded zero arrays) in human- and agent-readable DOM. A permanent Pages-origin verifier checks the direct hostname/path, raw-response-to-semantic-DOM identity, 390 px reflow, and unnamed actionable controls. Generic `DataTablePreview` routes are now **2**.

### Historical checkpoint: final DataTablePreview routes before terminal migration

- `ipify-public-ip`
- `catfacts`


## Provider execution policy follow-up

Historical status at this checkpoint: **local-only candidate after the PR #8 production baseline**.

LanguageTool's public service explicitly says not to send automated requests. Public Nominatim permits deliberate end-user-triggered use under strict limits, but its current LLM/platform policy forbids generic platform integration. The catalog models both public endpoints as `manual-only` for structured agent execution, including official policy links, while keeping the human Request Lab available. `list_public_api_demos` returns the execution policy and provider `usageNote` constraints; after the NWS browser-admission correction, `run_public_api_demo` advertises only the 193 structured-agent-enabled IDs and also re-checks policy at execution time. The Request Lab and catalog expose `data-agent-execution`, and the manual-only form adds an accessible `Agent execution restriction` notice referenced by the human `Try live API` button. Catalog quick actions for manual-only providers navigate to that policy-bearing form without auto-running the provider. Generic fetch-code tabs/actions are withheld for manual-only endpoints. The human form remains executable because the provider permits interactive use; automated agents must abstain.

Verification command:

```sh
npm run test:browser:provider-policy
```


Provider-policy audit note: MusicBrainz requires client applications to identify themselves with a meaningful application User-Agent and limits normal clients to one request per second. A normal browser controls the `User-Agent` header, so Public-API cannot meet that application-identification contract directly from GitHub Pages. The local candidate removes the MusicBrainz demo instead of hiding this incompatibility behind a warning. This is a policy/architecture decision, not a provider-health failure.

Provider-policy audit note: Yahoo Finance SGX History was also removed from the local candidate. The chart route was not a supported Yahoo public API, direct Pages-origin browser access remained CORS-blocked, and the demo only worked through a Jina Reader compatibility relay. Jina explicitly leaves target-site terms compliance to the caller, while Yahoo terms restrict automated collection. Relaying the request therefore did not make the endpoint browser-native or policy-compatible. This is a provider-admission correction, not a transient Yahoo outage.

Provider-health/admission audit note: Gutendex Book Search is removed from the local candidate, but it must not be described as persistently down. On 2026-09-05 its hosted `/books` endpoint first timed out in two independent isolated Pages-origin attempts and direct bounded probes. A later fresh Pages-origin recheck returned HTTP 200 twice (about 104 ms and 451 ms), proving the earlier outage was transient. The hosted site's current documentation nevertheless says `gutendex.com` is for test queries and recommends running your own server for long-term use. Its current `robots.txt` also disallows `/books/` for `User-agent: *` and blocks several named AI crawlers. Public-API already has Open Library for browser-native book discovery, so the local candidate removes this duplicate hosted test surface as a durability/policy admission choice, not as a claim that Gutendex is currently unavailable.

The bounded full structured-agent health pass that exposed this issue covered all 196 then-enabled IDs from the Pages origin: 195 default requests produced successful `result-card-v2` results with no generic fallback or horizontal document overflow; Gutendex timed out after 20 seconds and again in a fresh isolated browser. Later same-day Pages-origin probes recovered twice with HTTP 200, so preserve both observations and classify Gutendex's availability failure as transient. After its removal the local candidate has 197 total APIs: 195 structured-agent-enabled and two explicit `manual-only` endpoints. Its removal is based on the hosted-test/self-host durability boundary, generic-crawler restriction, and duplicate capability—not a permanent health label. This remains point-in-time admission evidence, not health telemetry.

The browser harness now enables Chrome's WebMCP testing surface and the provider-policy E2E exercises native `document.modelContext.getTools()` / `executeTool()` rather than injecting a fake registration object. That conversion exposed a real React lifecycle defect: changing the selected API recreated the navigation callback, which caused the `useWebMcp` effect cleanup to abort and re-register the native tools while `run_public_api_demo` was still in flight. The candidate now keeps navigation callback identity stable through a selected-ID ref, with a unit regression asserting ordinary API selection does not re-register or abort the five tools. Native E2E proves structured LanguageTool and Nominatim runs fail before provider requests, policy discovery and machine-catalog runnable IDs stay aligned, and a permitted Color API completes through native WebMCP using a deterministic network fixture. A separate real Pages-origin Color health probe passed first-pass, so orchestration correctness and provider-health evidence remain deliberately separated. No live LanguageTool or Nominatim request is sent.

A later full Pages-origin health audit of the 195 structured-agent-enabled candidate IDs produced 192 first-pass successes. DOAJ and DBLP initially failed in-browser. DBLP then recovered in fresh isolated browser runs; DOAJ also returned a successful fresh browser result but remained intermittent, with a later retry timing out. Eurostat Population remained HTTP 404 across the first pass plus three fresh isolated browser retries, and direct bounded probes reproduced the same 404 for `demo_pjan` and two other Eurostat Statistics API dataset codes. Preserve those observations as point-in-time failures rather than rewriting the first pass.

A subsequent same-day recovery check changed the current provider classification without erasing that history. From the real GitHub Pages origin, the unchanged Eurostat default request returned HTTP 200 with the expected `demo_pjan` JSON-stat dataset, and additional `demo_pjan`, `DEMO_R_D3DENS`, and `nama_10_gdp` Statistics API requests also returned HTTP 200. Two fresh isolated Request Lab runs each then succeeded for Eurostat Population, DBLP Search, and DOAJ Search with their expected semantic layouts and no generic fallback. Eurostat's current user guide still documents the Statistics API, so no request-definition change is justified by the earlier incident. Treat Eurostat, DBLP, and DOAJ as recovered after transient/provider-side failures in this evidence window; this remains point-in-time browser evidence, not durable health telemetry.

Provider-policy audit note: crates.io Package Lookup is removed from the local candidate. Its API can currently return HTTP 200 from the GitHub Pages origin, but crates.io's data-access policy requires API clients to stay at or below one request per second and send a `User-Agent` that uniquely identifies the application. Current Chrome verification from the Pages origin showed that a custom `User-Agent` supplied by page JavaScript is dropped and replaced by the browser's own generic Chrome user agent, while ordinary custom headers survive. Public-API therefore cannot satisfy the provider's application-identification contract from this browser-only architecture. This is a browser-admission/policy correction, not a crates.io availability failure. The local candidate now has 196 total APIs: 194 structured-agent-enabled and two manual-only endpoints.

Provider-policy audit note: NWS Weather API is removed from the current local candidate for the same browser-readiness boundary, not because the provider is unavailable. A fresh GitHub Pages-origin probe of `https://api.weather.gov/alerts/active?area=CA` succeeded first-pass and returned active CAP-derived alert records, including event, severity, certainty, urgency, affected area, timing, description, and instruction fields. However, the current official NWS API authentication documentation requires a `User-Agent` that identifies the consuming application, while Chrome silently drops a custom `User-Agent` supplied by page JavaScript and substitutes the browser-controlled value. Public-API therefore cannot truthfully satisfy the application-identification contract from its static browser-only architecture. The candidate removes the NWS catalog/profile/card route rather than treating HTTP 200 as sufficient browser admission. At that NWS-removal milestone the catalog became 195 APIs: 193 structured-agent-enabled and two manual-only endpoints, with 48 generic DataTablePreview routes remaining at that time.

Repeated same-day full Pages-origin audits of those 194 structured-agent-enabled IDs also exposed a verifier-classification problem rather than a new durable provider defect. One full pass was 194/194 first-pass clean. Other independent passes observed first-pass failures for Spaceflight News plus DBLP (both recovered within the bounded retry window), DOAJ (recovered within the bounded retry window), and Canada Open Data (three consecutive 20-second timeouts in one run). Canada Open Data then passed 3/3 later fresh-browser targeted checks without a request-definition change. The health-sweep reporter therefore treats retry exhaustion as `unresolvedAfterRetries` with an `INVESTIGATE` verdict, not as proof of a persistent provider failure. First-pass and retry evidence stays immutable; durable drift requires a later independent browser confirmation.

A later independent 194-ID maintenance sweep produced 192 first-pass successes. Canada Open Data timed out on the first pass and recovered on its first fresh-browser retry. DBLP failed the first pass plus both bounded retries as `network-or-cors`, so that run correctly ended `INVESTIGATE`; a later independent fresh-browser DBLP check then succeeded with the unchanged request definition and SSOT card. Preserve the 192/194 first-pass evidence and the exhausted same-run retries, but classify DBLP as recovered rather than durably broken. This run also motivated a health-sweep observability improvement: future failures retain sanitized Chromium `Network.loadingFailed` details (endpoint origin/path, network error text, blocked reason, and CORS status when Chrome supplies one) plus a test-only `browserFailureClass`. The earlier DBLP failures predate that instrumentation and therefore must not be retroactively called CORS failures.

A 2026-09-06 sharded Pages-origin maintenance sweep covered the complete current automated-verification-eligible set: 193 unique IDs, with 193/193 first-pass semantic successes, zero retries, zero unresolved providers, and exact aggregate coverage with no duplicates, missing IDs, or extras. LanguageTool and Nominatim were excluded because they are `manual-only`; CelesTrak was excluded from the generic sweep because its SSOT requires a minimum 7200-second automated-verification interval. One attempted final shard initially failed before launching provider requests because `dist/` had been rebuilt with the wrong Vite base; the Pages-base preflight rejected it immediately, `npm run build:pages` restored the correct bundle, and the shard then passed 46/46 first-pass. Treat the 193/193 aggregate as point-in-time browser evidence, not durable health telemetry.

DBLP browser-readiness follow-up (2026-09-07): repeated independent GitHub Pages-origin checks of the documented `https://dblp.org/search/publ/api` route now fail with Chromium `Network.loadingFailed` evidence `MissingAllowOriginHeader`, including a later isolated no-retry confirmation. This is stronger evidence than the earlier generic `network-or-cors` observations, but it remains a browser-origin contract finding rather than a claim that DBLP itself is down: the legacy search endpoint is still documented and can remain reachable outside browser CORS. The local unpublished candidate therefore changes only `dblp-search` to DBLP's official public-beta SPARQL endpoint at `https://sparql.dblp.org/sparql`, which passed direct GitHub Pages-origin CORS probes and the Request Lab first-pass health check. The replacement deliberately narrows the input promise to a bounded case-insensitive publication-title substring search, escapes the user string as a SPARQL literal, returns at most 20 publications, and reconstructs title, year, venue, DOI, DBLP record URL, and grouped author names from the SPARQL JSON response. A permanent live domain-card regression compares those provider values with the `research-library` semantic DOM at 390 px and verifies zero unnamed actionable controls. No production or owner-repository change is implied by this local candidate.

DBLP semantic-card follow-up (2026-09-17): the local candidate now gives `dblp-search` its own request-bound semantic adapter and `dblp-publications` layout. The adapter accepts only the exact generated DBLP SPARQL endpoint/query shape, validates the declared SPARQL JSON projection and provider-owned publication URI/literal title cells, groups repeated author rows, and withholds wrong-title, malformed, duplicate, conflicting, or over-limit evidence with explicit `ready`, `partial`, `empty`, or `invalid` state attributes. Focused unit coverage passed for valid grouping, query-key and modified-query rejection, mixed/wholly wrong-title batches, malformed typed cells, and coherent zero-binding empties. The browser verifier now checks the live request-bound/query/limit/count/invalid/duplicate/conflict identity and confirms that changing the form without executing a new request does not rebind the rendered result; no additional provider request is used for that check.

The same local follow-up now emits `api-catalog.json` from `apiCatalog.ts` during development/build. The HTML head and Agent Tools page advertise the artifact. Its versioned representation carries deterministic Request Lab paths, parameter metadata, bounded usage notes and the same `agentExecution` policy as WebMCP; executable request functions and live-health claims are intentionally absent. Provider-policy browser verification compares the JSON artifact’s enabled/manual-only set to the WebMCP run-tool enum so the two machine surfaces cannot silently drift.


Compact-detail accessibility follow-up (2026-09-06): the selected-API panel becomes a fixed overlay at widths up to 1180px, so it now behaves as a real modal dialog in compact mode instead of leaving keyboard/agent focus in the covered catalog. Opening it moves focus to its close control, the main workspace and visible navigation become inert, Tab/Shift+Tab remain inside, Escape or backdrop dismissal closes it and restores the invoking API control, and a scrim communicates the blocked background visually. At desktop widths the panel remains a persistent side surface and no longer exposes a close button whose state change had no visual effect. Pages-origin Chrome verification passed this contract at 900×844, while the separate 390px mobile-navigation modal contract remained green.

Responsive detail-focus continuity follow-up (2026-09-10): the earlier breakpoint handoff covered desktop → compact, where a focused control inside the now-hidden detail panel returns to the selected catalog row. The reciprocal compact → desktop transition still removed the focused compact-only Close button without choosing a visible successor, which left `document.activeElement` on the page body. The candidate now moves focus to the selected API's programmatically focusable detail heading when the drawer becomes the persistent desktop panel. A controllable `matchMedia` unit regression covers the transition, and the deterministic Pages-origin i18n/browser harness verifies both breakpoint directions with zero provider requests.

Preview CSS delivery follow-up on 2026-09-08: after the seven on-demand JS implementation bundles were accepted, `responsePreview.tsx` still statically imported the entire diagnostic + domain-card stylesheet, so every first semantic result paid about 34.3 kB of preview CSS even when the selected bundle did not use those rules. Vite's official CSS code-splitting contract preserves CSS imported by async JS chunks as separate assets and fetches it with the owning async chunk. The candidate therefore keeps only the shared `.domain-card` shell in `domainCards.css` at the registry layer and moves diagnostic/color/DNS/FX/lifecycle rules behind `DiagnosticPreviewBundle`, route-summary rules behind `OperationalPreviews`, and the dedicated semantic-adapter rules behind `SemanticPreviewBundle`; existing Drug Label and Food Recall CSS continues to merge into the Semantic bundle through its component imports. No API-to-style registry was added: CSS ownership follows the existing implementation bundle imports.

Measured Pages-origin synthetic-fixture evidence before → after this change shows decoded first-result assets falling from 129,682→105,595 B for Diagnostic Color, 143,075→112,119 B for Catalog Family GitHub, 128,880→97,924 B for Specialized ipify, 110,385→79,429 B for Weather, 106,186→76,451 B for Operational mempool.space, 104,352→73,396 B for Market, and 215,613→207,658 B for Semantic Jolpica. Generic family/specialized/weather/market results now fetch only the approximately 3.26 kB shared response-preview CSS; Diagnostic adds about 6.87 kB, Operational about 1.22 kB, and Semantic about 26.49 kB when those implementation families are actually selected. The bundle verifier now enforces CSS ownership plus bounded per-family CSS budgets so a large family stylesheet cannot silently migrate back into the registry chunk. These fixture runs are architecture/performance evidence only, not provider-health evidence. Official reference: https://vite.dev/guide/features#css-code-splitting .

Semantic sports second-level bundle split on 2026-09-08: measured first-result evidence showed that the broad `SemanticPreviewBundle` remained the dominant preview delivery cost, and Jolpica/OpenDota were a clean bounded cluster because both already owned dedicated semantic adapters and their CSS rules were isolated. The candidate now loads `JolpicaF1Preview` and `OpenDotaMatchesPreview` through `SportsSemanticPreviewBundle` instead of paying for every health/science/public-data semantic adapter. Their OpenDota/Jolpica CSS moved with the same async owner into `sportsSemanticCards.css`; the broad semantic bundle no longer exports those components or owns those styles. This is a delivery-only refactor: API IDs, preview layouts, semantic DOM, provider requests, Raw JSON and WebMCP contracts are unchanged.

The Pages-origin synthetic-fixture bundle verifier now keeps both a broad semantic representative (`carbon-intensity-gb`) and the two sports routes, preventing an unrelated Semantic bundle from loading alongside the sports chunk. Jolpica's decoded first-result assets fall from the prior 207,658 B to 79,803 B (**61.6% lower**); OpenDota uses the same 79,803 B sports path. The emitted sports implementation is 10,777 B JS + 2,683 B CSS, while the remaining broad Semantic bundle drops from about 114.21 kB JS / 26.49 kB CSS to 103,557 B JS / 23,807 B CSS. Vite's async dependency preload keeps shared primitives parallel with the selected dynamic import, so this boundary does not require a custom loader or second API registry. Official Vite guidance keeps CSS imported by async chunks with those chunks and preloads shared async dependencies.

Verification after this split: Vitest **228/228 PASS** across 38 files; `npm run build:pages` and `git diff --check` PASS; the Pages-origin preview-bundle isolation/CSS-budget/failure-recovery suite PASS for Diagnostic, Catalog Family, Specialized, Weather, Operational, Market, broad Semantic and both sports routes; real Pages-origin health for Jolpica and OpenDota passed **2/2 first-pass with zero retries**; the dedicated live Jolpica driver/constructor/race regression passed exact response-to-DOM identity, UTC race timing, coordinates, 390px reflow and zero unnamed controls; native WebMCP/provider-policy E2E remained PASS at **195 catalog / 193 runnable / 192 automated-verification-eligible**. No new provider/CORS drift was observed. This remains an unpublished local candidate.

The candidate therefore moves the already-dedicated Drug Label, Food Recall, RxNorm, UniProt, RCSB PDB, ChEMBL, PubChem and Ensembl adapters behind `ScienceSemanticPreviewBundle.ts`, with their RxNorm/protein/structure/molecule/gene rules owned by `scienceSemanticCards.css`; Drug Label and Food Recall keep their existing component-local CSS imports, which Vite merges into the same science async CSS asset. No API IDs, preview layouts, provider requests, semantic DOM, Raw JSON or WebMCP contracts change.

The remaining broad Carbon semantic path simultaneously falls from **194,563 → 159,635 B** (**18.0% lower**). The permanent preview-bundle verifier now includes PubChem and openFDA as Science representatives and proves they load exactly one Science implementation bundle/CSS while Carbon continues to load only the broad Semantic implementation. ### Terminal generic-preview migration (2026-09-09)

- `ipify-public-ip` now uses a `public-ip` semantic card that distinguishes the provider-observed public network address and IP family from geolocation or device identity.
- `catfacts` now uses a `cat-fact` semantic card that renders provider-returned trivia as readable content with its reported length.
- These migrations remove the final `data-table` profile registrations from the catalog; `DataTablePreview` is no longer part of the active preview architecture.
- Verification requires exact live raw-response-to-DOM identity, 390 px mobile reflow, accessibility-tree controls, full Vitest, Pages build, preview-bundle checks, native WebMCP/provider policy, and `git diff --check`.

### Mobile navigation focus continuity follow-up (2026-09-10)

A bounded responsive-focus audit found two disappearing-context defects in the mobile primary navigation. First, activating the already-current route closed the off-canvas modal with raw state rather than the shared modal close contract, so focus could remain in the now inert/off-screen sidebar. Second, resizing above the 760px mobile breakpoint while the mobile-only Close control held focus removed that control without a persistent focus destination.

The local candidate now closes same-route mobile navigation through the existing `useModalFocusTrap` restoration path, returning focus to the topbar menu trigger. When an open mobile navigation crosses into the persistent desktop/tablet sidebar while its mobile-only Close control owns focus, focus is handed to the active persistent navigation item (falling back to the page heading if needed). Different-route navigation continues to use the existing page-heading focus contract. Catalog search and pagination controls were also regression-tested and already preserve focus while replacing visible rows, so no extra focus management was added there. Browser-history route changes likewise continue to move focus to the destination page heading.

Deterministic unit coverage and the GitHub-Pages-origin UI-only browser verifier cover these contracts without provider requests.

### AlAdhan calculation-method contract follow-up (2026-09-10)

A catalog-wide request-effectiveness audit found that the AlAdhan calculation-method control declared `min=0`, while the shared integer normalizer used a falsy fallback (`parseInt(value) || fallback`). AlAdhan's live `/v1/methods` contract currently advertises JAFARI as method ID `0`, so selecting zero was silently rewritten to the Singapore default `11`. The same provider contract is discrete rather than a continuous `0..99` range: built-in method IDs currently cover 0–23 with gaps, while custom method `99` requires the separate `methodSettings` parameter that this demo does not expose.

The local candidate now preserves numeric zero during integer normalization and represents AlAdhan's current built-in method set as a native `select`, defaulting to method 11. Unsupported gap IDs and custom 99 fail through the existing select-option validation before provider execution. The request builder also fails closed to method 11 if called directly with an undeclared method. This keeps Human UI, machine catalog, WebMCP discovery/validation, and the provider request on one field SSOT rather than advertising invalid numeric IDs.

A real GitHub-Pages-origin run then selected built-in method `0`; the rendered endpoint contained `method=0`, AlAdhan returned HTTP success with `meta.method.id = 0`, the semantic prayer card exposed calculation method `0`, 390 px reflow had no horizontal overflow, and the accessibility tree had zero unnamed actionable controls. The permanent domain-card browser regression now covers this zero-valued alternate in addition to the default method-11/date-path contract.

Bank of Canada Valet date-contract remediation on 2026-09-10: the catalog previously told users that `startDate` / `endDate` accepted either `YYYY-MM-DD` or compact `YYYYMMDD`, but the current official Valet OpenAPI requires the query parameters `start_date` and `end_date` to match `YYYY-MM-DD` exactly. The request builder also silently replaced compact input with the rolling default range, so a visually accepted value could execute a different date range than the user requested. The local candidate now exposes both values as native `date` fields from the shared SSOT, validates real ISO calendar dates before provider execution, and uses the same calendar validator at the request boundary. Human UI, machine catalog, WebMCP discovery, and structured execution therefore share the exact provider date contract instead of preserving a misleading text-input escape hatch. The provider URL and series semantics are unchanged.

### Climate date/semantic + lazy responsive-CSS remediation (2026-09-10)

A provider-aware input audit found two related historical-climate SSOT defects and one responsive delivery regression. Open-Meteo Historical Weather and NASA POWER Climate both exposed free-text date controls even though the Human/agent contract is naturally an ISO calendar date. The candidate now uses native `date` fields with `YYYY-MM-DD` in the shared catalog SSOT. Open-Meteo sends that ISO value directly, while NASA POWER converts it only at the provider boundary to the documented `YYYYMMDD` wire format. Invalid compact values such as `20260803` now fail shared validation before a structured-agent provider request instead of creating a second accepted Human/agent date syntax.

The same audit exposed a more serious NASA POWER semantic-schema bug. The current daily API returns measurement series under `properties.parameter.<parameter-code>.<YYYYMMDD>` and publishes units/long names separately under top-level `parameters`; the prior local adapter and its fixture incorrectly expected `properties.parameters.<code>.data`, so a successful live response could collapse to an empty/zero chart. The Market adapter now reads the provider's actual series shape, joins the returned parameter metadata, normalizes compact response dates for readable semantic DOM, and drops the provider `header.fill_value` sentinel rather than charting missing data. A real GitHub-Pages-origin run for `2026-08-03..2026-08-07` matched both providers' returned date ranges and the NASA semantic series/metadata exactly.

That live 390px verification also caught a CSS code-splitting cascade bug: Market's mobile one-column override still lived in the eagerly loaded `styles.css`, while the desktop two-column base rule had moved into later-loaded `marketCards.css`. Equal specificity plus later load order restored the desktop minimum columns after the result chunk arrived, producing internal preview overflow. The fix keeps the responsive rule with its lazy owner. A follow-up static ownership audit found the same latent pattern across Weather, CatalogFamily, Specialized, DateList, station-list, and SemanticCards responsive selectors; all deterministically owned result-only media rules were moved to their existing lazy CSS owners. The initial stylesheet now has zero responsive-selector overlap with lazy preview CSS, the representative Pages-origin bundle suite remains green at 390px, and the decoded initial CSS falls from about 49.76 kB to about 45.70 kB. The permanent cold-load budget is tightened from 55 kB to 50 kB while retaining headroom. Final verification for this local milestone is **260/260 Vitest PASS across 49 files**, Pages-base build PASS, preview-bundle/CSS-budget/chunk-recovery PASS, native WebMCP/provider-policy PASS, the focused live climate Pages-origin verifier PASS, and `git diff --check` PASS; catalog/agent invariants remain **195 / 193 / 192 / 0 generic routes**.


### Strict date + declared-parameter execution boundary follow-up (2026-09-10)

A provider-aware input audit closed the remaining strict-calendar text controls and exposed a deeper one-SSOT bypass. Frankfurter v2 time-series requests use ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` `from` / `to` values, and the MLB schedule route used by this demo accepts a single schedule `date` in the same format. Both demos still exposed those values as free-text fields even though the shared field model now has a native `date` type. The candidate moves Frankfurter `from` / `to` and MLB `date` to that native date contract, validates real ISO calendar dates before execution, and keeps defensive request-boundary fallback for direct builder calls. Sunrise-Sunset remains an intentional text exception because its v2 provider contract explicitly supports relative `today` / `tomorrow` values in addition to explicit dates.

The same audit found that `mlb-stats-api` still destructured an undeclared `teamId` inside `buildUrl`. Human UI and catalog discovery never advertised that field, but the generic WebMCP `parameters` object accepted extra keys and shared validation previously ignored unknown keys, so a structured caller could alter the provider request through behavior outside the catalog SSOT. Shared validation now rejects every parameter key that is not one of the selected API's declared field IDs before network execution, and the hidden MLB `teamId` builder branch is removed. A catalog-wide unit invariant proves unknown keys fail for every API; a native WebMCP browser regression proves an attempted hidden `teamId` invocation is rejected with zero provider requests and an idle Request Lab.

Real GitHub-Pages-origin verification preserved provider behavior. MLB with native date `2025-04-15` returned the same schedule contract and exact game identity/status/score semantic mapping, with no `teamId` in the request, 390 px reflow and zero unnamed actionable controls. Frankfurter with `2026-07-01..2026-08-31`, monthly grouping and the existing SGD/MYR + ECB filters returned grouped provider dates `2026-07-01` and `2026-08-01`; the semantic market range matched those raw response dates exactly, with 390 px no-overflow and zero unnamed controls. Final verification for this unpublished local milestone is **262/262 Vitest PASS across 49 files**, Pages-base build PASS, PWA app-shell/offline-catalog PASS, native WebMCP/provider-policy PASS, live sports-schedule PASS, targeted live Frankfurter PASS, and `git diff --check` PASS. Catalog/agent invariants remain **195 / 193 / 192 / 0 generic routes**.

### BrasilAPI CEP format-SSOT validation follow-up (2026-09-10)

A provider-aware text-input audit found that `brasilapi-postcode` promised "exactly eight digits, with or without a hyphen" in Human help text but represented the value as unconstrained free text. Its request builder then stripped every non-digit character before sending the provider request. That meant malformed input such as `01310-930abc` passed Public-API's shared validation and was silently transformed into `/api/cep/v2/01310930`. This is unsafe SSOT behavior even when a provider happens to accept or normalize the resulting path: the executed request no longer faithfully represents an input that the product claimed was invalid.

The candidate adds an optional machine-enforceable `pattern` plus human-readable `patternDescription` to the existing `ApiField` SSOT. The same declaration now reaches native HTML `pattern`, shared pre-network validation, generated `api-catalog.json`, and WebMCP discovery. The BrasilAPI field uses `\d{5}-?\d{3}`: canonical `01310930` and standard formatted `01310-930` are accepted, while trailing letters or the wrong digit count fail before provider execution. The builder still removes the one optional standard hyphen at the provider boundary, so both valid Human representations map to the canonical eight-digit CEP path without creating a second request schema. The usage note is also tightened to bounded single-CEP lookups and no crawl/full-range scanning without misclassifying the endpoint as `manual-only`.

A real GitHub-Pages-origin run used `01310-930`, executed `/api/cep/v2/01310930`, received provider CEP `01310930`, rendered the existing `location-map` semantic card, passed 390×844 reflow, and exposed zero unnamed actionable controls. A second malformed-input browser check with `01310-930abc` stayed `idle`, set the field invalid, surfaced the shared format error, and sent **zero provider requests**. Native WebMCP/provider-policy verification independently proves the same pattern is visible through machine catalog/discovery and that malformed structured execution is blocked with zero network requests. Final local verification for this milestone is **263/263 Vitest PASS across 49 files**, Pages-base build PASS, PWA app-shell/offline-catalog PASS, native WebMCP/provider-policy PASS, and `git diff --check` PASS; catalog/agent invariants remain **195 / 193 / 192 / 0 generic routes**.

### Provider-documented text-format constraints follow-up (2026-09-10)

A follow-up audit applied the existing shared `ApiField.pattern` contract only where current provider documentation gives an explicit machine-verifiable format. The Color API documents both three-digit and six-digit hexadecimal inputs (`hex=ffa` and `hex=00ffa6`); the prior Human field accepted arbitrary text and relied on the provider to interpret it. The candidate now declares `#?(?:[0-9A-Fa-f]{3}|[0-9A-Fa-f]{6})`, keeps the optional leading `#` as a Human convenience, and removes that one character only after shared validation. Malformed values such as `GGGGGG` now fail before provider execution.

geoBoundaries documents the path segment as an ISO-3166-1 alpha-3 country code and also accepts the special `ALL` code. The existing `countryIso` field enforced only length 3, so values such as `123` passed Human/agent validation and reached the provider. The candidate adds the bounded `[A-Za-z]{3}` format constraint while preserving `ALL`; the rule validates representation only and does not pretend that every three-letter sequence is a real country code. Human native controls, machine catalog, WebMCP discovery, and shared request validation all derive these patterns from the same field SSOT.

Verification: the pre-fix focused regression failed because the Color field exposed no pattern; after the change the focused catalog/machine suites pass **40/40**, the complete Vitest gate passes **264/264 across 49 files**, Pages-base build and `git diff --check` pass, and native WebMCP/provider-policy browser verification rejects both `color-api.hex=GGGGGG` and `geoboundaries-admin-boundaries.countryIso=123` with zero provider requests. A real GitHub-Pages-origin Color run with valid shorthand `#fff` emitted `hex=fff`, returned provider canonical `FFFFFF`, rendered semantic `#FFFFFF`, passed 390 px reflow and had zero unnamed actionable controls. The existing focused geo/network live verifier also passed the geoBoundaries ADM1 contract with exact provider identity/geometry metadata, 390 px no-overflow, and zero unnamed controls. Catalog/agent invariants remain **195 / 193 / 192 / 0 generic routes**.

### Biomedical identifier-format SSOT follow-up (2026-09-10)

A provider-backed identifier audit extended the shared `ApiField.pattern` contract to two research routes where the provider publishes a machine-verifiable identifier syntax. UniProt's current accession documentation defines UniProtKB accessions as six or ten alphanumeric characters and publishes the combined regular expression `[OPQ][0-9][A-Z0-9]{3}[0-9]|[A-NR-Z][0-9]([A-Z][A-Z0-9]{2}[0-9]){1,2}`. `uniprot-protein.accession` now carries that format in the catalog SSOT, so Human native validation, machine catalog, WebMCP discovery, and pre-network execution validation reject malformed values such as `INVALID` instead of using the provider as the format validator. The valid default `P05067` remains unchanged and a real Pages-origin request still maps provider `primaryAccession=P05067` exactly into the semantic protein card. Official format reference: https://www.uniprot.org/help/accession_numbers .

RCSB/wwPDB is in an active PDB-ID transition. Current RCSB documentation identifies the canonical extended entry form as `pdb_` plus eight alphanumeric characters and states that entries deposited before the July 21, 2027 archive cutover additionally have a four-character ID; the transition maps an existing four-character ID such as `1ABC` to `pdb_00001abc`. The current live `core/entry` Data API behavior is narrower than that archive identifier transition: a Pages-origin/direct probe returned HTTP 200 for `4HHB` but HTTP 404 for the equivalent `PDB_00004HHB` path. The local Request Lab therefore accepts either the current four-character alphanumeric form or the official one-to-one transitional `pdb_0000XXXX` alias, then normalizes only that verified transitional representation back to the four-character ID at the provider boundary. It deliberately does not claim support for future extended-only IDs such as `pdb_1000axyz`; those must be re-evaluated against the Data API before the July 21, 2027 cutover. Official references: https://www.rcsb.org/docs/general-help/identifiers-in-pdb and https://www.rcsb.org/news/feature/69ebcdca1103c56d3f444159 .

The focused catalog/machine suites pass **40/40**. Native WebMCP/provider-policy verification exposes both identifier constraints through the generated machine catalog and discovery surface, rejects malformed UniProt and future-style unsupported PDB identifiers before network execution, and preserves **195 catalog / 193 runnable** IDs. A real GitHub-Pages-origin browser run entered `pdb_00004hhb` in the Human PDB field, observed the normalized provider URL `/rest/v1/core/entry/4HHB`, received entry `4HHB`, and matched that identity into the `molecular-structure` semantic card; UniProt `P05067` likewise remained exact raw-response-to-semantic identity. Both flows passed 390 px reflow and the final accessibility tree had zero unnamed actionable controls. This is an input/transition-contract hardening change, not a provider outage or catalog admission change.

CVE identifier + CIRCL canonical-endpoint follow-up on 2026-09-10: FIRST EPSS and CIRCL Vulnerability-Lookup both expose a single-CVE lookup in this catalog, but their Human/WebMCP fields still accepted arbitrary text. The CVE Program identifier contract uses the `CVE-YYYY-NNNN` shape with four or more sequence digits; a direct FIRST probe also showed that malformed `cve=not-a-cve` can return HTTP 200 with an empty dataset, so transport success is not evidence that the identifier was valid. The candidate now reuses one `ApiField` CVE definition with `CVE-[0-9]{4}-[0-9]{4,}` across both routes, which synchronizes native Human input constraints, shared pre-network validation, machine-catalog metadata, and WebMCP discovery. Structured malformed inputs now fail closed with zero provider requests. CIRCL is also moved from the backwards-compatible legacy `/api/cve/{id}` route to the current `/api/vulnerability/{id}` route used by the official PyVulnerabilityLookup client. Both CIRCL routes returned HTTP 200 and `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` during migration research, so this is canonical-contract maintenance rather than an outage response. A dedicated real GitHub-Pages-origin verifier passed live FIRST and CIRCL raw-response-to-semantic-DOM identity, the canonical CIRCL path, 390 px reflow, and zero unnamed actionable controls. Provider references: https://www.cve.org/about/Process ; https://api.first.org/epss/ ; https://github.com/vulnerability-lookup/PyVulnerabilityLookup/blob/main/pyvulnerabilitylookup/api.py .


### ISO country-code representation SSOT follow-up (2026-09-10)

A provider-backed text-format audit found three country-code controls whose Human help already promised ISO semantics but whose executable field SSOT did not fully enforce them. `unhcr-refugees.origin` constrained only the string length to three characters even though the request explicitly sets `cf_type=ISO`; a live probe showed malformed `coo=123` can still return HTTP 200 with an empty `items` array, making transport success unsafe evidence of a valid ISO3 lookup. `apple-itunes-search.country` and `zippopotam-postcode.country` likewise accepted arbitrary text although their provider contracts use two-letter ISO 3166-1 country codes.

The candidate now declares `[A-Za-z]{3}` for the UNHCR origin field and `[A-Za-z]{2}` for the Apple/Zippopotam country fields, with matching length bounds and format descriptions in the existing `ApiField` SSOT. Valid user representations remain case-flexible; normalization is confined to the provider boundary (`SYR` for UNHCR, lowercase storefront/path codes for Apple and Zippopotam). Human native controls, shared pre-network validation, generated machine catalog, and WebMCP discovery/execution therefore share one representation contract. The patterns validate the documented alpha-2/alpha-3 representation only and do not pretend that every letter combination is an assigned country code.

A real GitHub-Pages-origin browser run proved malformed `123` / `12` / `1x` inputs are rejected before provider execution, then exercised valid case-normalized requests: lowercase `syr` produced `coo=SYR&cf_type=ISO` and a live UNHCR row with `coo_iso=SYR`; uppercase `US` produced Apple `country=us` with live results; uppercase `US` produced Zippopotam `/us/10001` with provider country abbreviation `US`. The 390×844 check had no document/preview horizontal overflow, zero unnamed actionable controls, and zero browser errors. Native WebMCP/provider-policy verification independently proves the three patterns are discoverable and malformed structured calls generate zero provider requests. Final local gates are **265/265 Vitest PASS across 49 files**, Pages-base build PASS, native WebMCP/provider-policy PASS, and `git diff --check` PASS; catalog/agent invariants remain **195 / 193 / 192 / 0 generic routes**.


### Apple iTunes media/entity compatibility SSOT follow-up (2026-09-10)

Apple's Search API defines `entity` values relative to `media`. The prior candidate exposed `media` as a Music/Podcast select but kept `entity` as unrestricted text, so a user could choose Podcast while the default `entity=song` remained in place. A fresh provider probe from the GitHub Pages origin confirmed this is not a harmless presentation mismatch: `media=podcast&entity=song` returned HTTP 200 song results, while invalid entity text can return HTTP 400. Transport success therefore did not prove that the user's selected media intent was honored.

The local candidate removes the conflicting independent media control and represents the supported Apple music/podcast result entities as one native `entity` select in the existing field SSOT: song, musicTrack, album, musicArtist, musicVideo, mix, podcast, and podcastAuthor. The request builder derives `media=podcast` only for podcast/podcastAuthor and `media=music` for the music entities, with a defensive fallback to song for direct builder calls. Human UI, machine catalog, WebMCP discovery, validation, and provider request construction therefore advertise one set of executable combinations rather than a cross-field compatibility matrix that machine discovery cannot express. Apple’s archived Search API documentation is the contract source and also records an approximate 20 calls/minute limit subject to change.


Pre-fix regression coverage correctly failed against the old independent `media` + free-text `entity` model. After the remediation, focused catalog/machine tests pass **42/42**, the full Vitest gate passes **266/266 across 49 files**, `npm run build:pages`, PWA app-shell/offline-catalog verification, native WebMCP/provider-policy verification, and `git diff --check` all pass. A real GitHub-Pages-origin run selected `podcast`, produced `media=podcast&entity=podcast`, returned 8 live podcast results with provider identity present in the semantic `media-gallery`, passed 390 px reflow, exposed zero unnamed actionable controls, and reported zero browser errors. The same-origin provider research also observed `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`; no Apple CORS regression was found. Catalog/agent invariants remain **195 / 193 / 192 / 0 generic routes**.


### Open-Meteo Climate model/semantic contract remediation (2026-09-10)

A provider-contract audit found that `open-meteo-climate` exposed a stale model selector and a materially incorrect successful-response preview. The current Open-Meteo Climate API documentation lists seven HighResMIP / CMIP6 model IDs: `CMCC_CM2_VHR4`, `FGOALS_f3_H`, `HiRAM_SIT_HR`, `MRI_AGCM3_2_S`, `EC_Earth3P_HR`, `MPI_ESM1_2_XR`, and `NICAM16_8S`. The candidate still advertised `ecmwf_ifs04` plus `nasa_nex_gddp`; a fresh GitHub-Pages-origin probe found the latter returns HTTP 400 as an invalid model, while `ecmwf_ifs04` remained live-tolerated but is not part of the current documented Climate API model set. Bounded direct probes of all seven documented IDs each returned HTTP 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, so the field SSOT now exposes exactly those seven options and defaults to `CMCC_CM2_VHR4`. An obsolete `nasa_nex_gddp` structured invocation is rejected by shared select validation before network execution.

The same live browser baseline exposed a separate SSOT semantic defect: Open-Meteo returned valid `daily.time`, `daily.temperature_2m_mean`, and `daily.precipitation_sum` arrays, but the shared market fallback could not interpret that response shape and rendered a zero-valued chart with `Observations 1`. The candidate now routes this API-owned card through a dedicated `OpenMeteoClimatePreview` while retaining the existing lazy Market bundle. It maps the full returned daily climate series into model/range/latest mean/period mean/total precipitation/day-count semantics, uses the executed request URL as the model identity because this response does not repeat `models`, exposes stable machine-readable attributes, bounds only the visual sparkline sampling while computing metrics from the complete response, and fails semantically closed if no dated mean-temperature series is present.

Real GitHub-Pages-origin verification selected non-default `MPI_ESM1_2_XR` for `2025-01-01..2025-12-31`, received the exact 365-day provider range, and matched the requested model, returned range, observation count, and climate series into semantic DOM. The 390×844 run had no horizontal overflow and zero unnamed actionable controls. Final local gates are **269/269 Vitest PASS across 50 files**, Pages-base build PASS, preview-bundle isolation/budgets/failure-recovery PASS, native WebMCP/provider-policy PASS, focused live climate browser E2E PASS, and `git diff --check` PASS. ### Ordered date/year range SSOT follow-up (2026-09-10)

A cross-field contract audit found that Public-API validated each date/year input independently but did not represent the ordering relationship between a range start and end. This was already material across multiple admitted routes: a fresh Pages-origin-style direct probe returned HTTP 400 for a reversed Bank of Canada range, HTTP 400 for Open-Meteo Historical Weather, HTTP 422 for NASA POWER with an explicit start/end error, and HTTP 400 for Open-Meteo Climate. Frankfurter was more dangerous because the reversed range returned HTTP 200 with an empty array, while the World Bank indicator API returned HTTP 200 data for `date=2025:2015`. Transport success therefore cannot be used as evidence that a reversed range represented the user's intended request. All six probes exposed `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`; this is request-semantics hardening, not a CORS/outage remediation.

The candidate adds a generic `ApiField.minimumFromField` relationship and applies it to Bank of Canada `startDate/endDate`, Open-Meteo Historical Weather `startDate/endDate`, NASA POWER `startDate/endDate`, Frankfurter `from/to`, Open-Meteo Climate `startYear/endYear`, and World Bank Indicator Explorer `startYear/endYear`. Human native inputs derive their dynamic `min` from the linked field, shared validation rejects a reversed range before provider execution, and the generated machine catalog plus WebMCP discovery publish the same relation. A catalog invariant verifies every declared relation points to a field of the same type and that all defaults satisfy it. The Open-Meteo Climate builder no longer silently clamps an earlier end year up to the start year, and the World Bank builder no longer silently sorts reversed years; direct builder calls preserve supplied order while the shared runtime is the fail-closed execution boundary.

Regression-first coverage failed before implementation because the range relation did not exist. After the remediation, focused catalog/machine/App tests pass **123/123**. The native WebMCP/provider-policy browser verifier confirms all six reversed structured invocations fail with **zero provider requests** and an idle Request Lab. The real GitHub-Pages-origin climate verifier additionally proves the Human Open-Meteo history and climate controls update their native `min`, block reversed ranges before network, then still execute valid live ranges exactly; live Open-Meteo Historical Weather, NASA POWER, and Open-Meteo Climate response/semantic mappings remain exact with 390 px no-overflow and zero unnamed actionable controls. Final local gates are **271/271 Vitest PASS across 50 files**, Pages-base TypeScript/build PASS, native WebMCP/provider-policy PASS, focused live climate browser E2E PASS, and `git diff --check` PASS. Catalog/agent invariants remain **195 / 193 / 192 / 0 generic routes**.

### Result-shell i18n + semantic-status honesty follow-up (2026-09-10)

A focused accessibility/i18n audit found that the shared `ResponseDemoPreview` shell still hard-coded English after the surrounding Request Lab had switched to Simplified Chinese. The affected fixed chrome included the live-response badge, API-context and request-metadata accessible names, HTTP/time/size labels, the preview placeholder, and the screen-reader live announcement. The provider/API/category facts themselves remain source-faithful English, so the fix localizes only the shell labels and explicitly marks those canonical values plus preview-profile text with `lang="en"` instead of duplicating catalog facts into the translation table.

The same audit found a correctness issue in the live announcement: the shell said `Live result ready` whenever transport/runtime state was successful, but API-owned adapters can intentionally classify an HTTP-200 body as `empty`, `invalid`, or another domain state. The existing ipwho.is reserved-range contract is a concrete regression case: `127.0.0.1` returns HTTP 200 with `success=false`, and the semantic adapter correctly renders `data-result-state=invalid`. The shared announcement is therefore now transport-neutral—`Live response received` / `已收到…的实时响应`—so accessibility users and browser agents are not told that a semantically invalid payload is a ready result.

Verification for this unpublished local milestone: focused i18n/App/geo-network tests pass **85/85**; the complete Vitest gate passes **272/272 across 50 files**; Pages-base TypeScript/build, PWA app-shell/offline-catalog, preview-bundle isolation/CSS budgets/failure recovery, native WebMCP/provider-policy, and `git diff --check` all pass. The real GitHub-Pages-origin i18n browser verifier now renders a Chinese result shell using one exact synthetic World Bank response fixture: fixed shell chrome and accessible names are localized, API/provider/category/profile identities remain marked English, the announcement contains no semantic-ready claim, 390 px has no horizontal overflow, the final accessibility tree has zero unnamed actionable controls, and no live provider is contacted. Catalog/agent invariants remain **195 / 193 / 192 / 0 generic routes**.


### Live Weather semantic fail-closed follow-up (2026-09-10)

A focused HTTP-success semantic audit found that the front-door `weather` demo could render a plausible current-weather dashboard from an unusable Open-Meteo body. `CurrentConditionsPreview` recursively searched unrelated response fields and substituted labels such as `Live`, `Live reading`, and `Live station` when the provider's documented `current` measurements were missing. This meant a parsed HTTP-2xx JSON object such as `{}` could look like a valid domain result even though the requested `temperature_2m`, `relative_humidity_2m`, `wind_speed_10m`, and `weather_code` values were absent. Open-Meteo's current Forecast API explicitly supports those current-condition variables, and a fresh real-origin probe on 2026-09-10 returned all four under `current`, together with observation time and `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`; no provider/CORS drift was observed.

The local candidate now treats the Open-Meteo `current` object as the API-owned response contract instead of using recursive fallback discovery. A response with no usable requested current measurements renders `data-domain-card="current-weather" data-result-state="invalid"` and explicitly states that no live-weather conclusion can be drawn. A partly populated response is `partial`, lists the missing provider fields, and uses `—` rather than invented live values. A complete response is `ready` and exposes observation time, temperature, humidity, wind speed, and weather code as machine-readable DOM attributes from the same provider values. The permanent `test:browser:live-weather` verifier runs the unpublished local bundle under the real GitHub Pages origin, compares a live Open-Meteo response to those DOM fields exactly, checks 390×844 reflow/accessibility, then intercepts the exact provider URL with a synthetic HTTP-200 `{}` fixture and proves it remains semantic `invalid` with no fabricated Live placeholder.

Verification for this unpublished local milestone: focused CurrentConditions/App tests pass **83/83** and the complete Vitest gate passes **275/275 across 51 files**. `npm run build:pages`, the dedicated real-origin `test:browser:live-weather`, PWA app-shell/offline-catalog verification, preview-bundle isolation/CSS budgets/failure recovery, native WebMCP/provider-policy verification, and `git diff --check` all pass. The live Open-Meteo run preserved exact observation time, temperature, relative humidity, wind speed, and WMO weather code in semantic DOM, with 390×844 no-overflow, zero unnamed actionable controls, and zero browser errors. The synthetic malformed HTTP-200 regression used one exact provider fixture request and rendered semantic `invalid` rather than a fabricated weather reading. Catalog/agent invariants remain **195 / 193 / 192 / 0 generic routes**. Current Open-Meteo contract reference: https://open-meteo.com/en/docs .

### Datamuse word-identity semantic fail-closed follow-up (2026-09-10)

A focused HTTP-success semantic audit found that the dedicated `datamuse-rhymes` / sounds-like adapter still treated any non-empty object in the provider's top-level array as a lexical match. The prior adapter instead synthesized titles such as `Match 1` when a malformed HTTP-200 row omitted `word`, which could turn provider/schema drift into a plausible lexical result.

The local candidate now distinguishes transport-success payloads semantically. A real empty `[]` remains `data-result-state="empty"`; a non-array body or a non-empty array with no usable `word` identities is `invalid`; a mixed response preserves usable word records but reports `partial` and exposes provider/valid/invalid record counts in stable DOM metadata. The live request returned eight records, 390×844 had no horizontal overflow, the accessibility tree had zero unnamed actionable controls, and no browser errors were observed. Datamuse's official documentation currently also states that every request will require an API key beginning 2027-01-01, so the existing re-evaluation warning remains unchanged. ### UNSD Goal/List completeness semantic-state follow-up (2026-09-10)

A post-PR #10 semantic-honesty audit found that the dedicated `un-sdg-goals` adapter already calculated whether it had the complete official Goal 1–17 set, but did not use that fact to determine semantic readiness. Any non-empty set of usable goal rows was labelled `data-result-state="ready"`, so an HTTP-200 response containing only 16 goals, duplicate/missing goal codes, or a mixture of usable and malformed records could be presented to humans and browser agents as a ready goal catalogue. A non-array HTTP-success body was also collapsed into `empty`, which confused structural invalidity with a valid empty catalogue. The United Nations Statistics Division SDG API exposes `Goal/List`, while the official SDG framework consists of Goals 1 through 17. Contract references: https://unstats.un.org/SDGAPI/swagger/ and https://unstats.un.org/sdgs/iaeg-sdgs/metadata-compilation/ .

The local adapter now uses the provider-owned catalogue shape as its readiness contract. A non-array body, or a non-empty array with no usable `code + title` records, is `invalid`; a genuine empty array is `empty`; a response with usable goals that is not exactly one complete unique Goal 1–17 set is `partial`; and only the complete 17-goal set with no malformed records is `ready`. Partial responses preserve the trustworthy provider rows instead of inventing missing goals and expose provider-record, usable-goal, malformed-record, goal-code, and complete-set facts through stable `data-*` metadata. The visible card explicitly says when the Goal/List response is incomplete. Duplicate provider codes remain visible without creating duplicate React keys.

Regression-first unit coverage failed against the prior implementation exactly as expected: the two-goal fixture was labelled `ready`, and a non-array body was labelled `empty`. After remediation and recovery verification, the full Vitest gate passes **280/280 across 51 files** and the Pages-base build passes. The permanent real-GitHub-Pages-origin `test:browser:sdg-celestrak` verifier still receives the current live UNSD 17-goal response, matches every provider goal into semantic DOM, and reports `ready`; a separate exact synthetic HTTP-200 16-goal fixture now proves `partial` with no fabricated goals. Both cases pass 390×844 reflow and expose zero unnamed actionable controls. The cadence-limited CelesTrak branch remains fixture-only and sends no live CelesTrak request. Native WebMCP/provider-policy verification and `git diff --check` also pass; catalog/agent invariants remain **195 / 193 / 192 / 0 generic routes**. No UNSD provider/CORS drift was observed in the live Pages-origin check.

### WoRMS documented HTTP-204 no-match contract follow-up (2026-09-11)

A post-PR #10 provider-contract audit found a transport/semantic mismatch in `worms-species-lookup`. The WoRMS OpenAPI contract for `GET /AphiaRecordsByName/{ScientificName}` defines HTTP 200 as an `AphiaRecords` JSON array and HTTP 204 as `Nothing found`. The local candidate adds a narrow `successNoContent` API SSOT contract. WoRMS alone currently declares `{ statuses: [204], data: [] }`; the shared request runtime applies that mapping only when the response status is explicitly declared and the body is actually empty. Undeclared empty JSON success responses still fail closed as `invalid-response`. The Request Lab JavaScript fetch sample consumes the same declaration, so copied developer code does not call `response.json()` blindly on the documented 204 path. Catalog invariants require every declared no-content status to be a unique successful 2xx value. The WoRMS semantic adapter also stops collapsing malformed HTTP-200 shapes into `empty`: a non-array body is `invalid`, a genuine empty array is `empty`, mixed usable/identity-less AphiaRecord rows are `partial`, and fully usable records are `ready` with provider/valid/invalid counts exposed in agent-readable DOM.

Regression-first coverage failed in all intended places before implementation: WoRMS 204 was classified `invalid-response`, generated fetch code lacked 204 handling, malformed `{}` was presented as semantic `empty`, and the SSOT declaration was absent. After remediation, focused runtime/catalog/App/biodiversity tests pass **139/139** and the complete Vitest gate passes **293/293 across 51 files**. `npm run build:pages`, native WebMCP/provider-policy verification, preview-bundle isolation/budgets/failure recovery, and `git diff --check` pass. The real GitHub-Pages-origin biodiversity verifier confirms the live unaccepted-name mapping remains exact, a live nonexistent name now produces HTTP 204 → runtime `[]` → semantic `empty`, and a separate synthetic HTTP-200 `{}` fixture remains semantic `invalid`; all checked cases pass 390×844 reflow with zero unnamed actionable controls. The local adapter now requires an actual top-level array. A genuine empty array is `empty`; a non-array response, or a non-empty array with no rows carrying provider-owned `matchID`, is `invalid`; mixed identifiable and identity-less rows are `partial`; and fully identifiable rows are `ready` unless otherwise semantically incomplete. Rows without `matchID` are omitted rather than rendered as plausible matches. Team objects remain allowed to be absent because the provider schema marks them nullable; missing team names are shown as unavailable (`—`) and make the aggregate card partial rather than inventing team identity. Stable DOM metadata exposes provider/valid/malformed/incomplete match counts.

Verification: focused sports semantic tests pass **4/4** and the complete Vitest gate passes **296/296 across 51 files**. `npm run build:pages`, real GitHub-Pages-origin `test:browser:sports-schedules`, preview-bundle isolation/CSS budgets/failure recovery, native WebMCP/provider-policy verification, and `git diff --check` all pass. The live OpenLigaDB flow preserves match ID, team IDs, completion state, UTC kickoff, typed final-result kind and score exactly into semantic DOM. A separate exact synthetic HTTP-200 mixed Match array proves `partial` with providerRecords=2 / validMatches=1 / malformedMatches=1 and confirms the identity-less row is not rendered. Both live and synthetic OpenLigaDB cases pass 390×844 reflow with zero unnamed actionable controls. Initial CSS remains **45.70 KB decoded**; the lazy sports result path remains inside its existing budget at about **93.68 KB decoded result assets / 8.00 KB decoded result CSS**. Catalog/agent invariants remain **195 / 193 / 192 / 0 generic routes**. No live OpenLigaDB provider/CORS drift was observed.

### Jolpica F1 list-shape + provider-identity semantic-honesty follow-up (2026-09-11)

A post-PR #10 sports audit found that the dedicated `jolpica-f1` adapter still used the permissive `rows()` helper for `DriverTable.Drivers`, `ConstructorTable.Constructors`, and `RaceTable.Races`. A malformed HTTP-success payload with a missing/non-array documented list could therefore collapse into semantic `empty`, while fallback values such as `driver-N`, `constructor-N`, or a synthesized numeric race round could make schema drift look like a real F1 record. Jolpica's current endpoint documentation defines `MRData.DriverTable.Drivers`, `MRData.ConstructorTable.Constructors`, and `MRData.RaceTable.Races` as the response lists. Driver `driverId`, `givenName`, and `familyName` are always included; constructor `name` is always included while `constructorId` is optional; race `round`, `raceName`, and `Circuit` are always included. Official contracts: https://github.com/jolpica/jolpica-f1/blob/main/docs/endpoints/drivers.md , https://github.com/jolpica/jolpica-f1/blob/main/docs/endpoints/constructors.md , and https://github.com/jolpica/jolpica-f1/blob/main/docs/endpoints/races.md . A fresh Pages-origin-style live probe of 2025 drivers returned HTTP 200, `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, `MRData.total=36`, and identifiable driver rows; season 1900 returned HTTP 200 with `total=0` and `Drivers=[]`, establishing the genuine empty contract.

The local adapter now requires the documented list array for the selected dataset. A zero-length list is `empty` only with provider total zero; missing/non-array lists or unusable non-empty batches fail semantically closed. Mixed trusted and malformed records are `partial`; fully trusted records with a sane provider-total contract are `ready`. Drivers require provider `driverId + givenName + familyName` and no longer synthesize `driver-N`; constructors trust the documented name even when optional `constructorId` is absent and never synthesize `constructor-N`; races require provider round/race/circuit identity and no longer invent round or race labels. Stable DOM metadata exposes provider/valid/invalid record counts and count-contract validity without creating a second SSOT.

Verification: focused Jolpica semantic tests pass **7/7** and the complete Vitest gate passes **302/302 across 51 files**. `npm run build:pages`, `git diff --check`, and the real GitHub-Pages-origin `test:browser:jolpica-f1` verifier pass. The live verifier still exercises all three 2025 dataset modes with exact response-to-DOM driver, constructor, race, timing, circuit and coordinate semantics, 390×844 no-overflow, and zero unnamed actionable controls. A separate exact synthetic HTTP-200 mixed Driver array proves `partial` with providerRecords=2 / validRecords=1 / invalidRecords=1 and confirms the identity-less row is not rendered. Preview-bundle isolation/CSS budgets/failure recovery also pass; the Sports lazy path remains within its existing budget at **97,145 decoded result bytes / 8,003 decoded result CSS bytes**, with initial CSS unchanged at **45,707 bytes**. No new Jolpica provider/CORS drift was observed. This remains an unpublished local candidate.

### MLB schedule response-shape and game-identity hardening (2026-09-11)

A focused sports-adapter audit found that the dedicated MLB schedule card still used permissive `rows()` recovery for both `dates` and `dates[].games`. That made a malformed HTTP-success payload look like a legitimate empty schedule. The renderer also allowed rows without the provider's `gamePk` identity and substituted `Away team` / `Home team` labels when team names were missing, which could turn schema drift into plausible-looking game data.

Current schedule references and fresh provider evidence agree on the relevant shape: `/api/v1/schedule` returns top-level `totalGames` plus `dates[]`, each date contains `games[]`, and each normal game carries the provider-owned `gamePk`. A fresh Pages-origin-compatible probe of `sportId=1&date=2025-04-15` returned HTTP 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, `totalGames=15`, one date block, and 15 games with `gamePk`; `2025-01-01` returned HTTP 200 with `totalGames=0` and `dates=[]`, establishing the bounded semantic-empty case.

The local adapter now fails closed when `dates` is missing/non-array, rejects non-empty game batches with no usable `gamePk`, filters identity-less game rows, and reports `partial` when malformed rows, malformed date blocks, team-name gaps, or a top-level count mismatch are present. Missing team identity renders as `—` with accessible unavailable wording rather than fabricated team names. The semantic root exposes provider/valid/invalid/incomplete game counts plus count-contract state for browser agents and regression tooling.

Regression-first coverage correctly failed twice against the previous adapter. After the remediation, focused sports preview tests pass **6/6** and the full Vitest gate passes **304/304 across 51 files**. The Pages-base build passes. Real GitHub-Pages-origin browser verification preserves the live 2025-04-15 game identity/status/score mapping, proves the live 2025-01-01 zero-game response renders `empty`, and proves a synthetic HTTP-200 mixed batch renders `partial` with only the provider-identified game visible. The 390×844 checks report no horizontal overflow and zero unnamed actionable controls. Preview-bundle isolation/budgets/failure recovery, native WebMCP/provider-policy verification, and `git diff --check` also pass. Catalog/agent invariants remain **195 / 193 runnable / 192 automated-verification eligible / 0 generic routes**.

The prior dedicated adapter still used permissive `rows(root.items)` handling, so a missing/non-array `items` payload could be misreported as a legitimate empty search and a non-empty `value` object without a member ID could be rendered with a fabricated `Member N` identity. The local candidate now fails missing/non-array `items` as semantic `invalid`, accepts `items: []` as `empty` only when `totalResults === 0`, filters trusted member rows by provider-owned member ID, derives any name fallback only from that real ID, and marks mixed malformed rows, incomplete member names, or inconsistent pagination metadata as `partial`. Agent-readable DOM now exposes provider/valid/invalid/incomplete counts plus the pagination-count contract. The Pages-origin verifier retains live raw-to-DOM identity/party/house checks, adds a live no-match proof (`items: []`, `totalResults: 0` -> `empty`), and a synthetic HTTP-200 mixed-row regression proving the identity-less row is excluded rather than presented as a member. No publication is implied; all work remains in the persistent local candidate.
Verification for the UK Parliament follow-up: focused semantic tests **4/4 PASS**; full Vitest **306/306 PASS across 51 files**; Pages-base production build PASS; real GitHub-Pages-origin UK Parliament browser E2E PASS for live identity/pagination mapping, live zero-result semantics, 390 px overflow, accessibility-tree naming, and the synthetic mixed-row fail-closed regression; preview-bundle isolation/CSS budgets/failure recovery PASS; native WebMCP/provider-policy PASS with **195 catalog / 193 structured-agent runnable**; `git diff --check` PASS.

### Browser verification temp-profile lifecycle hardening (2026-09-11)

A reliability audit was triggered by the workstation filesystem reaching **99% used with only 766 MB free** while the unpublished candidate itself remained small. The largest directly attributable leak was outside the Git working tree: 18 inactive `/tmp/public-api-test-profile-*` Chromium profiles occupied about **1.3 GB**. These profiles are normally removed by `browser().close()`, but a verifier terminated by an outer runner timeout or other abrupt process exit can bypass that cleanup. The PWA verifier used the same unowned-temporary-profile pattern. Before any new build/test cycle, all inactive Public-API temporary browser/evidence artifacts were verified to have no active Chromium owner and removed, recovering **1,435,303,936 bytes** and restoring about **2.1 GB free**. No repository source, dirty candidate file, browser binary, dependency directory, or other project workspace was deleted.

The shared Pages-origin browser harness now gives every Chromium profile a small owner-PID marker and reaps an owned profile immediately when that owner process is no longer running. Legacy unowned profiles use a conservative two-hour stale window, while a profile whose recorded owner PID is still live is preserved. Normal shutdown is also stronger: the harness attempts `SIGTERM`, escalates to `SIGKILL` only if Chromium does not exit within the bounded grace period, and removes the profile only after the browser process is confirmed terminated. The PWA verifier reuses the same ownership/removal primitive. Both launch paths also disable first-run/background/component-update/default-app/sync work that is irrelevant to deterministic headless verification and had contributed large transient profile contents. These changes affect only test-browser lifecycle; provider execution, production runtime, WebMCP semantics, PWA application behavior, and retained `EVIDENCE_DIR` output contracts are unchanged.

Regression coverage for the new temp-profile primitive proves: dead recorded owners are reaped, live recorded owners are preserved even when old, legacy unowned directories are removed only after the stale window, and newly created profiles carry the current owner PID and can be removed explicitly. The complete Vitest gate passes **324/324 across 52 files**; Pages-base build and `git diff --check` pass. Real Pages-origin synthetic preview-bundle verification passes with unchanged initial CSS **45,707 bytes**, PWA install/offline-shell verification passes, and native WebMCP/provider-policy verification passes with **195 catalog APIs / 193 structured-agent runnable**. After both browser suites, `public-api-test-profile-*` and `public-api-pwa-profile-*` counts are **0**, confirming the normal cleanup path under the hardened harness. This remains local-only and unpublished.
