The prior adapter therefore had two semantic-honesty defects: a missing/non-array `data` member collapsed into a legitimate-looking empty result, and a non-empty record with no numeric forecast or actual could still render `data-result-state="ready"`. Official contract: https://carbon-intensity.github.io/api-definitions/ . A fresh GitHub-Pages-origin-compatible probe on 2026-09-11 returned HTTP 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, one current half-hour record, numeric forecast/actual, a documented intensity index, and UTC `from`/`to` values.

The local adapter now requires the documented `data[]` response boundary. A genuine empty `data: []` remains `empty`; missing/non-array `data`, or a non-empty current record with neither numeric forecast nor estimated actual, is `invalid`; a usable measurement with missing documented forecast/index/UTC interval facts is `partial`; and a complete current record is `ready`. The estimated-actual value remains legitimately optional for display because the provider can supply a forecast before an estimated actual is available. Stable agent-readable DOM now exposes provider record count plus the existing exact forecast/actual/index/interval facts, and the card no longer turns structurally unusable HTTP-success data into a plausible carbon-intensity result.

Regression-first unit coverage failed all three intended cases against the prior implementation and passes after remediation. A dedicated real GitHub-Pages-origin verifier executes the live NESO request and matches forecast, estimated actual when supplied, index, and UTC interval exactly into semantic DOM; a separate exact synthetic HTTP-200 malformed-envelope fixture proves the same transport success resolves to semantic `invalid` with exactly one intercepted provider request. Both the live path and 390x844 accessibility check pass with zero unnamed actionable controls. The complete Vitest suite passes **330/330 across all 52 test files** in three bounded shards, `npm run build:pages` and `git diff --check` pass, and preview-bundle isolation/CSS budgets/failure recovery pass with initial CSS unchanged at **45,707 decoded bytes**; the Carbon Intensity semantic result path remains inside its existing budget at **190,901 decoded result bytes / 23,904 decoded result CSS bytes**. Catalog/agent invariants are unchanged. This remains unpublished local work.

Focused semantic-honesty remediation on 2026-09-11: Malaysia Household Income now distinguishes a genuinely empty HIES result from malformed HTTP-success data. The official `hh_income` dataset is an array of annual `YYYY-01-01` observations with integer `income_mean` and `income_median` RM values; the prior adapter silently discarded malformed rows, still reported `ready` when valid and invalid rows were mixed, and reported `empty` when all returned rows were malformed. The local adapter now fails a non-array or wholly malformed response as `invalid`, preserves `[]` as the only semantic `empty`, reports mixed valid/malformed provider rows as `partial`, and exposes provider/valid/invalid row counts in Agent-Readable DOM metadata. A real GitHub-Pages-origin run returned 10 current provider rows with 2024 as the latest observation and exact mean/median/date mapping, `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, 390px no-overflow, and zero unnamed actionable controls. Synthetic HTTP-200 malformed and mixed fixtures prove schema drift cannot become a plausible income trend. The full unit suite passes **343/343 across 52 files**, Pages-base build and `git diff --check` pass, and the work remains local-only.


### Malaysia Core CPI + Population response-contract semantic-honesty follow-up (2026-09-11)

A focused follow-up on the two remaining data.gov.my national-statistics cards found the same permissive-row failure mode previously repaired in Household Income. `MalaysiaCoreCpiPreview` and `MalaysiaPopulationPreview` both used `rows(data).filter(...)`, so a missing/non-array HTTP-success body or a non-empty batch containing only malformed/mismatched rows collapsed into semantic `empty`, while a mixed valid + invalid provider batch still reported `ready`. Fresh GitHub-Pages-origin runs showed no provider drift: Core CPI returned 12 monthly `division=overall` rows through 2026-07-01 with numeric index values, while Population returned 10 annual national-total rows through 2026-01-01 with `sex=both`, `age=overall`, `ethnicity=overall`, and population in the provider's `'000 people` unit. The current data.gov.my catalogue contracts define Core CPI as monthly `YYYY-MM-01` overall-division observations with base 2010=100 and Population as annual `YYYY-01-01` multidimensional observations whose national total requires those three overall demographic dimensions.

The local adapters now require the documented top-level array before classifying the response. A genuine empty array remains `empty`; a non-array body or a non-empty batch with no trusted rows is `invalid`; mixed trusted and malformed/mismatched rows are `partial`; and a fully conforming batch is `ready`. Core CPI trusts only `division=overall`, valid monthly first-of-month dates, and numeric indexes. Population trusts only annual January 1 dates, `both/overall/overall` demographic identity, and a numeric population value. Malformed rows are excluded from trends/headlines instead of becoming plausible statistics. Both semantic roots now expose provider/valid/invalid record counts alongside their existing primary measurement and identity metadata for Agent-readable DOM consumers.

Verification: focused regression tests pass **10/10**; the complete Vitest gate passes **349/349 across 52 files**; `npm run build:pages` and `git diff --check` pass. A dedicated real GitHub-Pages-origin verifier maps live Core CPI date/index and Population date/raw-thousand/person-converted values exactly into semantic DOM, then proves malformed HTTP-200 envelopes resolve to `invalid` and mixed synthetic batches resolve to `partial` with providerRecords=2 / validRecords=1 / invalidRecords=1 while fabricated values are hidden. Both live cards pass 390x844 reflow with zero unnamed actionable controls. A bounded lazy-result budget check keeps initial CSS at **45,707 decoded bytes**, result CSS at **23,904 decoded bytes**, and each affected result path at **197,633 decoded result-asset bytes**, using the existing `SemanticPreviewBundle`. ### OpenCitations citation-count shape + zero-count semantic-honesty follow-up (2026-09-11)

A focused audit of `opencitations-index` found that the dedicated citation-count adapter still used permissive `rows(data)` coercion. That made a non-array HTTP-success body indistinguishable from an empty provider result and allowed any first record with a numerically coercible `count` to become `ready`. OpenCitations Index v2 documents `/citation-count/{id}` as returning the number of incoming citations for one DOI/PMID/OMID and shows a JSON array containing exactly one record with the `count` field. A fresh real GitHub-Pages-origin default request returned one record with count 98; a bounded request for an intentionally nonexistent DOI returned HTTP 200 with one record whose count is 0, proving that zero is a legitimate index-scoped business value rather than an empty-response state. Official contract: https://api.opencitations.net/index/v2 .

The local adapter now requires an actual top-level array containing exactly one count record, and that count must be a non-negative integer. Non-array responses, empty/multi-record arrays, negative/fractional/missing counts fail semantically closed as `invalid`. A valid zero remains `ready` and is rendered as `0 incoming citations`. The semantic root exposes provider-record/count-contract metadata alongside the existing DOI, direction, and index scope. If a valid provider count is rendered without the request URL needed to bind it to a DOI, the card is `partial` rather than claiming full identity.

Verification: focused OpenCitations tests pass **6/6** and the complete Vitest gate passes **358/358 across 52 files**. `npm run build:pages`, `npm run test:browser:opencitations`, and `git diff --check` pass. The permanent real-GitHub-Pages-origin verifier confirms the default DOI count maps exactly into semantic DOM, an unknown DOI remains `ready` with provider count zero rather than becoming `empty`, and an exact synthetic malformed HTTP-200 object is `invalid`. Live and synthetic cases pass 390×844 reflow with zero unnamed actionable controls. The affected lazy path remains within the existing performance envelope at **45,707 decoded initial CSS bytes / 23,904 decoded result CSS bytes / 199,649 decoded result bytes**. No OpenCitations request URL or provider policy was changed, and no provider/CORS drift was observed. This remains unpublished local-candidate evidence.

### 2026-09-11 · UniProt accession identity hardening (local-only)

The `uniprot-protein` direct-resource adapter now treats HTTP-success payloads without a valid `primaryAccession`, non-object payloads, and request/response accession mismatches as semantic `invalid` instead of conflating malformed provider data with an empty protein lookup. UniProt documents the primary accession as the stable citable identity and retains merged historical accessions as secondary accessions. Fresh provider probes confirm that requesting secondary accession `P09000` resolves to the current `P05067` record with `P09000` present in `secondaryAccessions`, so the adapter deliberately accepts a requested accession when it matches either the returned primary accession or a valid provider-reported secondary accession rather than requiring naive request==primary equality.

A trustworthy primary/secondary identity can remain visible as `partial` when core annotation context is absent or malformed; missing context is not inferred. The Agent-Readable DOM now exposes requested accession, whether it resolved as primary or secondary identity, primary accession, secondary-accession counts, and identity-match status. ### 2026-09-11 · RCSB PDB core-entry identity hardening (local-only)

A focused audit of `rcsb-pdb-entry` found that the direct-resource semantic adapter trusted only `entry.id`, treated an HTTP-success object without that field as semantic `empty`, and did not compare the returned structure identity with the requested core-entry URL. A fresh browser-origin-shaped probe returned HTTP 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` for `4HHB`, while an intentionally nonexistent `9ZZZ` returned HTTP 404 rather than an HTTP-200 empty object.

The local adapter now requires all three provider-owned identifiers, requires them to agree, and when request metadata is available requires the returned identity to match the requested provider entry ID. Non-object HTTP-success data, missing provider identity, internally conflicting identifiers, or request/response mismatches are semantic `invalid` and do not render plausible structure facts. A matching identity can remain visible as `partial` when title, experimental method, archive status, or release-date context is unavailable; a complete entry remains `ready`. Agent-readable DOM metadata exposes requested entry ID, identity-match status, `rcsb_id`, `entry.id`, and container `entry_id` alongside the existing method/resolution/archive facts.

### PubChem compound property-envelope + CID semantic-honesty follow-up (2026-09-11)

A focused remaining-adapter risk scan found that `pubchem-compound` still used permissive `rows(PropertyTable.Properties)[0]` handling. That collapsed a missing/non-array/empty HTTP-success property table into semantic `empty` and accepted a property record without provider-owned CID identity. PubChem PUG REST documents the compound `name` input namespace and property-table operations, returns HTTP 200 for successful property lookups, and uses HTTP 404 `PUGREST.NotFound` when the requested input record cannot be resolved. Its tutorial also explicitly warns that a chemical name may refer to multiple CIDs, so a semantic adapter must preserve provider-owned compound identities rather than assume one name always means one CID. Fresh 2026-09-11 probes with the GitHub Pages origin confirmed `aspirin` returns HTTP 200, `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, one property row with `CID=2244`, formula `C9H8O4`, molecular weight `180.16`, and IUPAC name `2-acetyloxybenzoic acid`; an intentionally nonexistent compound name returned HTTP 404 with `PUGREST.NotFound`. A separate `glucose` property probe currently resolves to the provider-selected CID 5793, consistent with PubChem documenting that some operations select from a name-derived CID set. Official references: https://pubchem.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/docs/pug-rest and https://pubchem.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/docs/pug-rest-tutorial .

The local adapter now requires the documented object -> `PropertyTable` -> `Properties[]` response envelope. An HTTP-success empty property array is `invalid`, not `empty`, because the provider's not-found contract is HTTP 404. Trusted rows require a positive integer CID; a non-empty batch with no trusted CID is `invalid`; mixed trusted and identity-less rows are `partial`; and all provider-owned CIDs are retained when more than one property record is returned. Missing requested properties make the affected response `partial` with explicit unavailable values instead of inference. The response adapter receives the same request URL used by the shared request runtime so the requested compound name can be exposed in Agent-Readable DOM alongside provider/valid/invalid/incomplete record counts, single/multiple cardinality, primary CID, formula, molecular weight, and IUPAC name. A multi-CID result renders each CID separately rather than silently choosing the first. The catalog also gives the compound-name field an explicit minimum length and trims the provider-boundary name before URL construction.

Verification: focused PubChem/catalog tests pass **49/49**; the complete Vitest suite passes **376/376 across all 52 test files** in three bounded VMMCP-safe shards (**108 + 177 + 91**); `npm run build:pages` passes. The dedicated real GitHub-Pages-origin PubChem verifier maps the live aspirin CID/formula/weight/IUPAC fields exactly from Raw JSON into semantic DOM, proves both a malformed HTTP-200 envelope and an HTTP-200 empty property table resolve to semantic `invalid`, and proves a synthetic two-CID property table preserves both provider identities as `ready`; 390×844 has no horizontal overflow and the accessibility tree has zero unnamed actionable controls. Native WebMCP/provider-policy verification passes with **195 catalog APIs / 193 structured-agent runnable**, including machine-catalog text-length alignment. The PubChem Science Semantic result path remains within the existing performance envelope at **45,707 B initial CSS**, **12,085 B result CSS**, and **115,239 decoded result-asset bytes**. No live PubChem provider/CORS drift was observed. This remains unpublished local work.

### 2026-09-11 · Ensembl stable-ID Gene identity hardening + transient-health evidence (local-only)

A remaining semantic-risk scan found that `ensembl-gene-lookup` treated any HTTP-success object carrying `id` as a ready gene record and classified a missing stable ID as semantic `empty`. Ensembl's official `GET /lookup/id/:id` contract is broader than the Public-API card: it resolves a single Ensembl stable identifier such as a **gene, transcript, or protein**. The previous adapter therefore had two correctness gaps: malformed HTTP-success data could be presented as a harmless empty lookup, and a valid transcript/protein response could be relabelled as a gene locus merely because it carried an `id`. Official contract: https://rest.ensembl.org/documentation/info/lookup .

The local adapter now receives the same request URL used by the shared request runtime, requires provider stable identity, requires the returned stable ID to match the requested ID (including the provider-returned versioned form), and requires `object_type === "Gene"`. Non-object HTTP-success data, missing stable identity, request/response identity mismatch, and non-Gene feature types are semantic `invalid` and cannot render plausible gene facts. A matching Gene with valid species/assembly/biotype, ordered genomic coordinates, and strand `+1`/`-1` is `ready`; a matching Gene with missing or malformed core locus context remains visible as `partial` with unavailable values rather than fabricated coordinates. Canonical transcript remains optional. Agent-readable DOM metadata now exposes requested stable ID, identity-match status, returned object type, provider gene ID, and the validated locus fields. The API SSOT usage note also states that the provider endpoint is generic while this demo is intentionally gene-only, so Human UI, machine catalog, and WebMCP inherit one execution/semantic explanation.

Provider-health investigation was kept separate from this deterministic semantic fix. Direct server-side probes in this cycle returned HTTP 500 HTML without CORS headers, while independent real GitHub-Pages-origin Chromium runs were intermittent: successful BRAF `Gene` responses, one `MissingAllowOriginHeader` browser failure, and one 20-second timeout were observed. A bounded three-browser check produced **2/3 successes + 1 timeout**; the first post-build dedicated live verifier also timed out, while a later independent fresh-browser verifier passed completely. This is treated as transient/unresolved provider instability rather than proven durable CORS/request drift, so browser admission was not changed and no provider error was hidden to manufacture a green result.

Verification: focused Ensembl/catalog tests pass **48/48**; the complete Vitest suite passes **380/380 across all 52 test files** in three bounded shards (**108 + 177 + 95**); `npm run build:pages` passes. The dedicated real GitHub-Pages-origin verifier ultimately passes live BRAF stable-ID/object-type/locus mapping plus exact synthetic malformed, wrong-ID, and non-Gene HTTP-200 cases; all three synthetic cases resolve to `invalid` and hide fabricated identity. The live card passes 390×844 reflow with zero unnamed actionable controls. Native WebMCP/provider-policy verification passes with **195 catalog / 193 structured-agent runnable**, and the generated machine catalog contains the gene-only usage note. The affected Science Semantic path remains inside the existing budget at **45,707 B initial CSS / 12,085 B result CSS / 116,650 decoded result-asset bytes**. This remains unpublished local work.

### 2026-09-12 · NOAA CO-OPS latest water-level identity + observation-contract hardening (local-only)

A remaining semantic-risk scan found that `noaa-tides` still treated any HTTP-success payload whose permissive `data` helper yielded no row as semantic `empty`, while any first non-empty object was rendered `ready` even when the provider station identity, observation time, measured value, or QA/QC code was missing. NOAA CO-OPS documents the Data Retrieval API `station` parameter as the station identifier, defines `date=latest` as the last data point available within the preceding 18 minutes, requires a datum for water-level products, and describes `water_level` as a six-minute preliminary-or-verified observation product. Its response-help contract defines water-level row fields `t` (observation time), `v` (measured height), `s` (one-second-sample sigma), `f` (data flags), and `q` where `p` means preliminary and `v` means verified. Official references: https://api.tidesandcurrents.noaa.gov/api/prod/ and https://api.tidesandcurrents.noaa.gov/api/prod/responseHelp.html . A fresh bounded live probe returned HTTP 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, metadata identifying station `8518750` / The Battery, and one current `t/v/s/f/q` observation.

The local adapter now fails HTTP-success bodies closed unless they contain the NOAA response object, metadata identifies the exact requested station, and `data` is an array. A structurally valid matching-station zero-observation array remains `empty`; a non-empty batch with no row carrying valid provider observation time, numeric measured height, and documented `p|v` QA/QC identity is `invalid`; mixed valid/malformed rows are `partial`; and the normal single `date=latest` row is `ready` when coordinates plus the documented sigma/flag context are present. A latest response with more than one provider row is retained but marked `partial` because the request asks for the provider's single latest point. Agent-readable DOM now exposes requested station, identity-match status, provider/valid/invalid/incomplete record counts, latest-count-contract validity, and the same station/value/time/quality/datum/unit fields rendered to humans. Malformed rows are omitted and cannot contribute a plausible water-level value.

Verification after remediation: focused NOAA Tide tests pass **5/5**. The complete Vitest suite passes **384/384 across all 52 test files** in three bounded shards (**136 + 61 + 187**); the Pages-base TypeScript/Vite build passes. The dedicated real GitHub-Pages-origin `test:browser:noaa-tides` verifier receives the live `date=latest` response, maps station identity, measured value, timestamp, QA/QC code, sigma, flags, coordinates, datum, and units exactly into semantic DOM, passes 390×844 reflow with zero unnamed actionable controls, and proves separate synthetic HTTP-200 malformed-envelope and mixed-row cases resolve to `invalid` and `partial` respectively with the fabricated measurement hidden. Preview-bundle isolation/budgets/failure recovery passes with initial CSS **45,707 decoded bytes** and the broad Semantic result representative at **201,819 decoded result-asset bytes / 23,904 decoded result CSS bytes**. `git diff --check` passes. No NOAA request URL, admission policy, or publication state changed, and no live NOAA provider/CORS drift was observed. This remains unpublished local work.

### 2026-09-12 · geoBoundaries identity/cardinality hardening + ALL-country collection support (local-only)

A focused audit found a concrete mismatch between the geoBoundaries catalog contract and the dedicated semantic adapter. The SSOT already advertised the provider's special `countryIso=ALL` value, but the provider returns an array for `/gbOpen/ALL/{ADM-level}/` while `GeoBoundariesPreview` coerced only a single object. A user selecting the documented `ALL` value therefore received a misleading semantic empty card even though the HTTP-success body contained many real layers. The same adapter also did not bind `boundaryISO` / `boundaryType` / `boundaryID` to the requested URL, so a malformed or mismatched HTTP-success object could still be presented as `ready`.

The local adapter now receives the exact request URL and validates provider-owned identity before rendering. A single-country request must return one object whose ISO/admin level match the request and whose `boundaryID` is coherent with `boundaryISO` + `boundaryType`; a collection response is accepted only when the request explicitly uses an `ALL` dimension. Structurally unusable or identity-mismatched HTTP-success data is `invalid`. Valid rows missing documented boundary metadata remain visible as `partial`; malformed rows in a collection are excluded rather than relabelled. For `countryIso=ALL`, the card now preserves the provider collection, exposes provider/valid/invalid/incomplete counts in Agent-Readable DOM metadata, and renders a bounded first 24 validated layers with exact provider IDs/ISO/admin-level identity while Raw JSON retains the complete response. The catalog help/usage note now makes the potentially large `ALL` collection explicit and recommends a specific ISO when global coverage is unnecessary.

Fresh real GitHub-Pages-origin verification on 2026-09-12 confirmed the normal `SGP/ADM1` path remains exact and browser-ready, then exercised the previously broken live `ALL/ADM1` path. The provider returned **199 records**. **198** had coherent layer identity; one aggregate row was internally inconsistent: it reported `boundaryID=IND-ADM0-90661403` together with `boundaryISO=IND` and `boundaryType=ADM1`. The direct current `IND/ADM1` endpoint separately returns the coherent `IND-ADM1-1811400` record, so this cycle treats the `ALL/ADM1` India row as point-in-time provider aggregate drift rather than weakening the identity contract. The semantic result correctly resolves the global response to `partial`, hides that inconsistent row, renders the first 24 trusted layers in provider order, and exposes the invalid-record count instead of presenting the aggregate as fully trustworthy.

Verification: focused geo/network tests pass **6/6**; the complete Vitest suite passes **387/387 across all 52 files** in three bounded shards (**136 + 64 + 187**); `npm run build:pages`, the real Pages-origin geo/network verifier, preview-bundle isolation/CSS budgets/failure recovery, and `git diff --check` pass. The live ALL-country card passes 390×844 no-overflow with zero unnamed actionable controls. Initial CSS remains **45,707 decoded bytes** and the broad Semantic representative remains inside its existing result budget at **206,919 decoded bytes / 24,514 decoded result CSS bytes**. No files were staged, committed, pushed, or published.

### 2026-09-12 · Eurostat demo_pjan single-cell JSON-stat identity hardening (local-only)

A remaining semantic-risk audit found that `eurostat-population` still selected the first `value` entry and first category code without validating the JSON-stat cube itself. That meant a malformed HTTP-success response could collapse to semantic `empty`, a response for a different geography/year could be shown as `ready`, and a multi-cell or wrong-unit cube could be reduced to one plausible-looking population number. Eurostat's Statistics API returns JSON-stat 2.0 datasets whose `id` gives dimension order, `size` gives the corresponding dimension sizes, and `dimension.*.category.index` identifies the selected category positions. A fresh GitHub-Pages-origin-compatible probe of `demo_pjan` for Germany/2025 returned HTTP 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, `version=2.0`, `class=dataset`, `source=ESTAT`, `id=[freq,unit,age,sex,geo,time]`, `size=[1,1,1,1,1,1]`, dimensions `A/NR/TOTAL/T/DE/2025`, and the exact single population cell `83,577,140`; the provider currently reports dataset update time `2026-08-14T23:00:00+0200`.

The local adapter now validates the exact single-cell `demo_pjan` contract before rendering. It requires the provider dataset/source/version metadata, exact dimension order and cardinality, exactly one indexed category per dimension at position zero, fixed annual/number/total-age/total-sex dimensions, and — when request metadata exists — exact geography/year identity with the executed URL. Wrong/missing dimensions, multi-cell cubes, request/response identity mismatches, or malformed present values are `invalid` rather than being reduced to a first cell. A structurally valid exact cell with no published observation (including JSON-stat `null`) remains semantic `empty`. A present population must be a non-negative integer. If the cube is internally coherent but request identity is unavailable, it is exposed as `partial`/unbound rather than falsely claiming request identity. Agent-readable DOM now exposes requested geography/year, identity-match state, cell count, dimension-contract validity, and the same population/dimension facts rendered to humans.

Regression-first coverage failed **5/6** against the previous adapter exactly on the intended gaps; final focused Eurostat coverage is **8/8 PASS**. The complete Vitest gate passes **393/393 across all 52 test files** in bounded shards (**136 + 70 + 187**), and the Pages-base TypeScript/Vite build passes. The permanent real GitHub-Pages-origin Eurostat verifier maps the live Germany/2025 cell and all six dimension identities exactly into semantic DOM, passes 390×844 reflow with zero unnamed actionable controls, and proves an exact synthetic HTTP-200 wrong-unit cube containing a fabricated `99,999,999` population resolves to `invalid` without displaying that value. Preview-bundle isolation/budgets/failure recovery pass with initial CSS **45,707 decoded bytes** and the broad Semantic representative at **209,561 decoded result-asset bytes / 24,514 decoded result CSS bytes**. Native WebMCP/provider-policy verification initially exposed one stale geoBoundaries help-text expectation left by the preceding local ALL-country documentation change; the verifier expectation was aligned to the current SSOT and then passed with **195 catalog APIs / 193 structured-agent runnable**. `git diff --check` passes. No Eurostat provider/CORS drift was observed. No files were staged, committed, pushed, or published.

### 2026-09-12 · Homebrew direct-token kind + identity hardening (local-only)

A focused audit of `homebrew-formula-json` found that the dedicated Homebrew card still treated nearly any non-empty HTTP-success object as a `ready` package and inferred formula versus cask only from the returned shape. It did not bind the response to the executed direct-lookup URL, so a malformed object, wrong package token, or even a cask-shaped response to a formula request could become plausible package metadata. Homebrew's official JSON API documents distinct direct endpoints `/api/formula/${FORMULA}.json` and `/api/cask/${CASK}.json`; current formula records expose provider-owned `name`, `full_name`, and `tap`, while casks expose `token`, `full_token`, and `tap`. Fresh bounded probes also confirmed current endpoint-token semantics: `node` formula and `folo` cask return HTTP 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, while the `node` alias `npm`, former cask token `follow`, and intentionally nonexistent formula/cask tokens return HTTP 404 rather than an HTTP-200 empty package. Official contract: https://formulae.brew.sh/docs/api/ .

The local adapter now receives the exact executed request URL and fails closed unless response kind and provider identity agree with it. Formula responses require `name + full_name + tap=homebrew/core`; casks require `token + full_token + tap=homebrew/cask`; request/response kind mismatches, wrong direct-lookup token identities, non-object HTTP-success bodies, and identity-less objects are semantic `invalid` and cannot render fabricated package details. A trustworthy provider identity without request metadata is `partial` rather than falsely `ready`; matching identity with malformed/missing expected package context also remains `partial`. Agent-readable DOM now exposes requested kind/token, identity-match state, package token/full token, provider tap, and the existing version/dependency/artifact facts. The token field now has an explicit minimum length, trims at the provider boundary, and explains through the shared field SSOT that aliases/former tokens are metadata rather than current direct JSON endpoint names.

The real GitHub-Pages-origin package verifier maps live `node` formula identity/version/licence/dependencies/bottles and live `postman` cask identity/version/update/artifact facts exactly into semantic DOM, then proves an exact synthetic wrong-formula HTTP-200 payload resolves to `invalid` while fabricated package/version text is hidden. ### 2026-09-12 · Google Public DNS request/question identity + answer-shape hardening (local-only)

A semantic-honesty audit of `google-dns-doh` found that the dedicated DNS card still used permissive row coercion for `Question` and `Answer` and did not bind a successful provider response to the exact executed DNS request. An HTTP-200 body with a mismatched Question, a malformed Answer shape, or identity-less answer rows could therefore become plausible DNS output. Google Public DNS's JSON DoH contract defines one required query name plus one RR type, returns the DNS-layer integer `Status`, echoes the query in `Question[]` using FQDN/trailing-dot name plus numeric RR type, and returns DNS `Answer[]` records with name/type/TTL/data when answers exist. DNS failure status such as NXDOMAIN/SERVFAIL is distinct from HTTP transport failure. Official contract: https://developers.google.com/speed/public-dns/docs/doh/json . Fresh bounded probes also confirmed two important real-provider nuances: `example.com&type=CNAME` currently returns `NOERROR` with the matching Question and no `Answer` member (legitimate NODATA), while `www.github.com&type=A` can return a CNAME followed by a terminal A record whose owner name differs from the original Question. The adapter therefore binds identity at `Question`, not by incorrectly requiring every answer owner to equal the requested name.

The local adapter now receives the exact executed request URL. HTTP-success data is `invalid` unless it is an object with a valid integer RCODE, exactly one usable provider Question, and — when request metadata exists — an exact normalized DNS-name + RR-type match. A missing `Answer` member is accepted as the provider's legitimate zero-answer form, but a present non-array Answer is invalid. Trusted answer rows require provider name, integer RR type, non-negative integer TTL, and non-empty data. A non-empty Answer batch with no trusted rows is `invalid`; a mixed trusted/malformed batch is `partial` with bad rows omitted; a matching `Status=0` zero-answer response is `empty`; and a matching nonzero RCODE remains `dns-error` rather than being mislabeled as schema invalidity. Stable Agent-readable DOM now exposes requested name/type, provider Question name/type, identity-match state, RCODE, provider/valid/invalid answer counts, and per-answer provider name/type/TTL from the same semantic model shown to humans.

DNS-focused coverage inside `domainPreviews.test.tsx` now includes seven cases covering exact TXT/zero-TTL preservation, NXDOMAIN, NOERROR/NODATA with omitted Answer, malformed/non-array response structure, request/Question mismatch fail-closed behavior, mixed-answer partial handling, a legitimate CNAME chain, and SERVFAIL diagnostics; the focused file passes **31/31**. The complete Vitest gate passes **401/401 across all 52 test files** in deterministic shards (**144 + 70 + 187**), and the Pages-base TypeScript/Vite build passes. The new permanent `test:browser:google-dns` verifier runs the unpublished bundle under the real GitHub Pages origin, executes a live `example.com A` request, and maps provider Question, RCODE, every live answer name/type/TTL/data value, and record counts exactly into semantic DOM. A separate exact synthetic HTTP-200 mismatched-Question fixture resolves to `invalid` and hides its fabricated `203.0.113.99` answer. Both browser cases pass 390x844 reflow with zero unnamed actionable controls. Preview-bundle isolation/budgets/failure recovery passes with initial CSS **45,707 decoded bytes** and the Diagnostic result path at **113,533 decoded result-asset bytes / 10,587 decoded result CSS bytes**. Native WebMCP/provider-policy verification passes with **195 catalog APIs / 193 structured-agent runnable**. Fresh live probes and the Pages-origin browser run observed HTTP 200 JSON with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`; no Google Public DNS provider/CORS drift was observed. `git diff --check` passes and all work remains local-only/unpublished.


### 2026-09-12 · ipwho.is direct-IP identity + geolocation context hardening (local-only)

A focused network-semantic audit found that `ipwhois-lookup` still treated every `success=true` HTTP-success object with a non-empty `ip` string as `ready`. The adapter did not bind the provider-owned IP identity to the exact executed direct-lookup URL, did not verify that the provider `type` matched the returned address family, and accepted numerically coercible out-of-range coordinates or ASN values as plausible geolocation/network facts. The current ipwho.is contract documents `https://ipwho.is/{IP address}` for IPv4/IPv6 lookups, identifies `ip` as the IP used for the query, returns `type` as `IPv4` or `IPv6`, and can represent invalid/reserved lookups as HTTP 200 with `success=false`. Fresh 2026-09-12 origin-shaped probes returned HTTP 200 plus `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` for both `8.8.8.8` and `2001:4860:4860::8888` with matching provider identity/type, while `127.0.0.1` returned HTTP 200 with `success=false` and `Reserved range`. Official contract: https://ipwhois.io/documentation .

The local adapter now receives the exact executed request URL and validates provider identity before exposing location/network semantics. HTTP-success non-object data, `success=true` without a parseable provider IPv4/IPv6 identity, provider `type` that contradicts the returned address family, or a request/response IP mismatch fail semantically closed as `invalid`; fabricated location/network context is not rendered. IPv4 identities are normalized by validated octets and IPv6 identities are canonicalized before comparison, so equivalent expanded/compressed IPv6 forms still match. A trustworthy matching IP remains visible as `partial` when required geolocation/network context is unavailable or malformed; latitude/longitude are trusted only as JSON numbers within WGS84 bounds and ASN only as a non-negative integer. A valid provider identity without request metadata is also `partial` rather than falsely claiming that the response is bound to a specific lookup. Only a request-bound matching identity with complete core country/coordinate/timezone/ASN/organization context is `ready`. Agent-readable DOM now exposes the requested IP and identity-match state alongside the provider IP/type and validated geolocation/network facts, preserving the same one-SSOT semantic model used by the human card.

Verification after remediation: focused geo/network tests pass **10/10**. The complete Vitest gate passes **405/405 across all 52 test files** in three bounded shards (**181 + 105 + 119**), and the Pages-base TypeScript/Vite build passes. The real GitHub-Pages-origin geo/network verifier maps live `8.8.8.8` request/response identity plus location/network facts exactly into semantic DOM, preserves the live `127.0.0.1` HTTP-200 application-error path as `invalid`, and uses an exact synthetic HTTP-200 wrong-IP fixture (`8.8.8.8` requested, `1.1.1.1` returned) to prove plausible Cloudflare/Brisbane data is hidden and the result remains `invalid`; all affected checks pass 390×844 reflow with zero unnamed actionable controls. The existing geoBoundaries `ALL/ADM1` provider aggregate still returns 199 rows with 198 identity-coherent layers and the previously observed inconsistent India row, so that independent provider drift remains correctly `partial` and was not weakened to make this cycle green. Preview-bundle isolation/budgets/failure recovery pass with initial CSS **45,707 decoded bytes** and the broad Semantic representative at **211,691 decoded result-asset bytes / 24,514 decoded result CSS bytes**. Native WebMCP/provider-policy verification passes with **195 catalog APIs / 193 structured-agent runnable**, `git diff --check` passes, and temporary Public-API browser profiles return to zero. No live ipwho.is CORS/provider drift was observed. No files were staged, committed, pushed, or published.

### 2026-09-12 · RDAP direct-domain identity + object-shape hardening (local-only)

A remaining direct-resource audit found that `rdap-domain-lookup` still rendered any HTTP-success object with a non-empty `ldhName` as a trustworthy registration card and did not bind that provider-owned domain identity to the exact `/domain/{domain}` request that produced it. RFC 9082 defines direct domain information queries as `domain/<domain name>` and permits internationalized names in A-label or U-label form; RFC 9083 defines the RDAP domain object and its `objectClassName="domain"`, `ldhName`, `unicodeName`, nameserver, entity, status, and event members. Fresh bounded provider probes on 2026-09-12 confirmed `https://rdap.org/domain/google.com` redirects to the authoritative Verisign RDAP service and returns HTTP 200 `application/rdap+json` with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, `objectClassName=domain`, and `ldhName=GOOGLE.COM`. An intentionally unserviceable direct lookup returns RDAP HTTP 404 rather than an HTTP-200 empty domain object. Official contracts: https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9082#section-3.1.3 and https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9083#section-5.3 .

The local adapter now receives the exact executed request URL and canonicalizes DNS identity case-insensitively through browser IDNA handling so equivalent A-label/U-label forms compare as one domain identity. HTTP-success non-objects, responses without usable `ldhName`/`unicodeName`, contradictory LDH-vs-Unicode identities, a non-domain `objectClassName`, an unparseable direct request identity, or a request/response domain mismatch are semantic `invalid`; registrar/lifecycle/nameserver details are withheld in those cases. A coherent provider domain without request metadata remains `partial` rather than falsely request-verified. Optional status/nameserver/event/entity shape problems also downgrade a request-bound result to `partial` and suppress the affected untrusted details, while a request-bound coherent domain object remains `ready`. Agent-readable DOM now exposes requested canonical domain, provider LDH/Unicode/canonical identity, object class, identity-match state, context-shape state, and status/nameserver/event counts from the same semantic model rendered to humans.

Verification: new focused RDAP coverage passes **8/8**. The complete Vitest gate passes **413/413 across 53 test files** in bounded shards (**110 + 116 + 187**), and `npm run build:pages` passes. The permanent real GitHub-Pages-origin `test:browser:rdap-domain` verifier maps live `google.com` provider identity/object class/registrar/nameserver/status facts exactly into semantic DOM, passes 390x844 reflow with zero unnamed actionable controls, and proves exact synthetic HTTP-200 wrong-domain and missing-identity fixtures resolve to `invalid` while fabricated registration data is hidden. Preview-bundle isolation/budgets/failure recovery passes; initial CSS remains **45,707 decoded bytes** and the Operational result representative remains **87,900 decoded result-asset bytes / 7,977 decoded result CSS bytes**. Native WebMCP/provider-policy verification passes with **195 catalog APIs / 193 structured-agent runnable**. ### 2026-09-12 · ipify public-IP syntax hardening (local-only)

A focused terminal-adapter audit found that `ipify-public-ip` treated every non-empty `ip` string in an HTTP-success JSON object as trustworthy and inferred the address family only from whether the string contained `:`. That meant malformed provider data such as `{"ip":"not-an-ip"}` became a semantic `ready` IPv4 card with a copy action. ipify's official universal endpoint contract documents `https://api64.ipify.org?format=json` as returning JSON whose `ip` value is the caller's IPv4 or IPv6 address, with examples for both families. Official contract: https://www.ipify.org/ .

The local adapter now validates the provider `ip` field as actual IPv4 or IPv6 syntax before exposing network identity. IPv4 requires exactly four decimal octets in the 0–255 range; IPv6 is accepted only when the browser URL parser recognizes it as an IPv6 literal. Missing, non-string, or malformed `ip` values fail semantically closed as `invalid` and do not expose an address family, copy action, or fabricated network identity. A valid response remains `ready`, preserves the provider-returned address text for Human UI/Agent-readable DOM, and derives `data-ip-family` only from the validated address. No request URL/catalog behavior changed because the universal ipify endpoint identifies the current browser's egress address rather than a user-supplied resource identity.

### 2026-09-12 · GLEIF LEI search-envelope + provider-identity hardening (local-only)

A focused Operational-adapter audit found that `gleif-lei` still used permissive collection coercion and treated a missing/non-array `data` member as a harmless unavailable result. It also rendered any object carrying `attributes.lei` or `record.id` without proving those two provider-owned identifiers agreed, and it did not bind the semantic result to the executed legal-name search or its requested page size. GLEIF documents its API as a search surface over Golden Copy LEI data with filters, single-field/full-text searches, and fuzzy matching; Level 1 reference data defines the LEI as a 20-character identifier. Because name search is intentionally fuzzy, this adapter does **not** invent a false exact-name identity rule. Instead it validates the actual request contract plus the provider-owned LEI identity of each returned record. Fresh 2026-09-12 origin-shaped probes of the default `Royal Bank of Canada` search returned HTTP 200 `application/vnd.api+json`, `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, eight `lei-records`, `meta.pagination.perPage=8`, `total=17`, and matching `record.id === attributes.lei` on the primary result. A deliberately nonexistent legal-name search returned HTTP 200 with `data: []`, `perPage=8`, and `total=0`, confirming that a coherent zero-row search is a legitimate semantic empty result rather than a malformed response. Official references: https://www.gleif.org/en/lei-data/gleif-api and https://www.gleif.org/en/about-lei/common-data-file-format/current-lei-cdf-format-3-1 .

The local adapter now receives the exact executed request URL and accepts only the supported `api.gleif.org/api/v1/lei-records` legal-name search with a bounded page size. An HTTP-success non-object body or missing/non-array `data` member is `invalid`; a zero-row array is `empty` only when pagination metadata is coherent; non-empty rows require `type=lei-records`, a valid 20-character provider `id`, a valid 20-character `attributes.lei`, and equality between those two LEI identities. A non-empty batch with no trusted identities is `invalid`; mixed trusted/malformed rows are `partial` and malformed rows are omitted; trusted rows missing legal name or core entity/registration status remain visible as `partial`. A coherent response without request metadata is also `partial` rather than falsely request-bound. Agent-readable DOM metadata now exposes requested legal name/page size, provider page size/total, pagination-contract validity, provider/valid/invalid/incomplete record counts, and the same primary LEI/legal-name/status facts shown to humans.

The complete Vitest gate passes **420/420 across all 54 test files** in bounded shards (**110 + 120 + 190**), and `npm run build:pages` passes. The permanent real GitHub-Pages-origin `test:browser:gleif-lei` verifier maps the live legal-name search's first provider LEI/name/status facts exactly into semantic DOM, proves an exact synthetic mixed HTTP-200 batch with one wrong LEI becomes `partial` while the fabricated entity is hidden, passes 390×844 reflow, and reports zero unnamed actionable controls. ### 2026-09-12 · FDIC BankFind search-acknowledgement + certificate-identity hardening (local-only)

A focused audit of `fdic-bankfind` found that the dedicated institution card still permissively coerced `data`, accepted undocumented direct rows, treated missing/malformed HTTP-success envelopes like an unavailable result, and rendered plausible institution facts without binding them to the exact executed search and limit. FDIC's BankFind institutions API documents an object response containing `meta` plus `data[]`, Elastic query-string search syntax with fuzzy institution-name matching, and JSON/CSV output. Fresh 2026-09-12 origin-shaped evidence for `search=NAME: WELLS FARGO&limit=6&format=json` returned HTTP 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, `meta.total=35`, exact echoed search/limit, and six nested `{data:{...},score}` rows; the primary record had `CERT=3511` and `ID="3511"`. A deliberately impossible name returned HTTP 200 with the same coherent acknowledgement, `meta.total=0`, and `data:[]`. Because institution-name search is intentionally fuzzy, returned `NAME` is not treated as an exact query identity. Official contract: https://api.fdic.gov/banks/docs .

The local adapter now receives the exact executed request URL and fails closed unless the provider `meta.parameters.search` and integer-normalized `limit` acknowledge that request. The top-level object, `meta`, `parameters`, nonnegative integer `meta.total`, returned-row/limit/total coherence, and `data` array are validated before empty or business semantics are emitted; only matching acknowledgement plus `total=0` and `data:[]` becomes `empty`. Rows must use the observed nested wrapper with a finite numeric score. A trusted institution requires a positive integer-normalizable `CERT`, and a present `ID` must normalize to the same certificate. Identity-less, contradictory, direct, or malformed rows are omitted; a mixed trusted/untrusted batch is `partial`, and a nonempty batch with no trusted row is `invalid`. Request-bound trusted rows are `ready` only when core name, status, assets, deposits, and office count are usable; missing or malformed core context remains visible as `partial` with unavailable values shown as `—`, while legitimate zero financial values are preserved. Agent-readable DOM exposes result state, requested/provider search and limit, acknowledgement match, provider/valid/invalid/incomplete counts, provider total, and the trustworthy primary certificate/name/status/assets/deposits/offices from the same ViewModel used by the human card.

The complete Vitest gate passes **428/428 across all 55 test files**, and `npm run build:pages` passes. ### 2026-09-12 · OSRM Route request-envelope + route-geometry semantic hardening (local-only)

OSRM's current HTTP API documents `/route/v1/{profile}/{coordinates}`, `code=Ok` for successful HTTP 200 responses, ordered `waypoints` for the supplied coordinates, recommendation-ordered `routes`, optional numbered alternatives, GeoJSON geometry, and route-specific `NoRoute` as an error outcome. The local adapter now receives the exact executed request URL and validates the supported HTTPS `router.project-osrm.org/route/v1/driving` two-point request plus `alternatives=1..3`, `geometries=geojson`, `overview=full`, and `steps=true`. Turn steps are emitted only when their distance/duration and maneuver identity validate; malformed step context makes the route `partial` rather than fabricating instructions. The two snapped waypoint locations must be valid coordinate pairs for `ready`, but they are deliberately not required to equal the requested coordinates because OSRM snaps inputs to the road network. A coherent response without an executed request URL remains usable as `partial` rather than falsely request-bound. Agent-readable DOM now exposes the same result state, requested start/end/alternative count, route code, provider/valid/invalid/incomplete counts, waypoint-contract validity, and primary distance/duration/step/geometry facts used by the human card.

### Go Module Proxy case-escaping + version-list semantic-honesty follow-up (2026-09-12)

A focused Developer-ecosystem audit found two evidence-backed defects in `go-module-proxy`. First, the request builder used ordinary URL encoding for a Go module path, but the GOPROXY protocol requires each uppercase ASCII letter in `$module` to be encoded as `!` followed by its lowercase form. A fresh probe proved the practical breakage: `https://proxy.golang.org/github.com/Azure/azure-sdk-for-go/@v/list` returned HTTP 404 with `bad request: invalid escaped module path`, while the protocol-correct `github.com/!azure/azure-sdk-for-go/@v/list` returned HTTP 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. Second, the semantic card treated the provider's plain-text response order as release order and labeled the last line `Latest listed`, even though a fresh `github.com/gin-gonic/gin/@v/list` response was visibly unsorted (`v1.9.0`, `v1.3.0`, `v1.7.0`, ...). The Go Modules Reference defines `$base/$module/@v/list` as one known version per line, omits pseudo-versions from that list, and defines the case-escape convention for module/version path elements. Official contract: https://go.dev/ref/mod#module-proxy .

The local request builder now applies the official GOPROXY case escaping before ordinary URL encoding, so mixed-case module paths execute correctly without changing the user's module identity. The response adapter is now request-bound to the executed `proxy.golang.org/.../@v/list` URL and exposes explicit `invalid / empty / partial / ready` semantics. Missing/non-array parsed version envelopes and invalid request identities fail closed; a request-bound zero-line list is a legitimate semantic `empty`; canonical tagged semantic versions are trusted; malformed rows, duplicates, and pseudo-version-shaped rows are hidden and make a mixed response `partial`; and an otherwise valid list without executed-request identity remains `partial`. The card no longer calls provider order “latest”: it independently applies semantic-version precedence and reports `Highest listed`, with release/prerelease and provider/valid/invalid counts exposed from the same ViewModel in Agent-Readable DOM metadata.

Verification: focused Go/catalog/App coverage passes **130/130**. The complete Vitest suite passes **453/453 across all 59 files** in three bounded shards (**126 + 132 + 195**). `npm run build:pages`, the dedicated real GitHub-Pages-origin `test:browser:go-module`, preview-bundle isolation/CSS budgets/failure recovery, native WebMCP/provider-policy verification, and `git diff --check` all pass. The live browser verifier executes `github.com/Azure/azure-sdk-for-go` through the corrected `github.com/!azure/...` endpoint, receives **276** canonical tagged versions, binds the DOM to the original mixed-case module identity with state `ready`, and reports `v68.0.0+incompatible` as the highest listed semantic version; 390x844 has no horizontal overflow and the accessibility tree has zero unnamed actionable controls. Initial CSS remains **45,707 decoded bytes** and the Specialized representative remains inside budget at **154,696 decoded result bytes / 21,798 decoded result CSS bytes**. No current `proxy.golang.org` browser/CORS drift was observed. This remains unpublished local work.


### 2026-09-12 · NuGet SemVer 2 registration + pagination honesty hardening (local-only)

A focused Developer-ecosystem audit found two concrete correctness problems in `nuget-package-lookup`. First, the catalog still called NuGet.org's `registration5-semver1` hive, which excludes SemVer 2.0.0 packages. Microsoft documents `RegistrationsBaseUrl/3.6.0` as the registration resource that includes SemVer 2 packages, and NuGet.org's current service index advertises that resource at `https://api.nuget.org/v3/registration5-gz-semver2/`. Fresh 2026-09-12 probes showed the impact rather than treating this as documentation-only drift: `NuGet.Versioning` exposes **112** registered versions through the old SemVer-1 hive but **176** through the current SemVer-2 hive. Both current service-index and SemVer-2 registration requests returned HTTP 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. Official contract: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/nuget/api/registration-base-url-resource .

Second, the old generic `DeveloperFeedPreview` selected the last registration page and last inline leaf as if those were always present and synonymous with the latest package. NuGet explicitly permits `items` to be omitted from registration-page objects as an optimization; on nuget.org, packages with 128+ versions are split into 64-version pages whose leaves are fetched separately through each page's provider-owned `@id`. The live SemVer-2 `NuGet.Versioning` index currently demonstrates that exact shape: three pages, **176** registered versions, **0** inline leaves. The old preview therefore collapsed a healthy large-package response into `Developer records unavailable`.

The local catalog now uses the SemVer-2 registration hive and a dedicated `package-registration` semantic adapter. It binds the exact executed registration-index request to the provider package identity when available, validates documented root/page counts, HTTPS NuGet page links, normalized NuGet version bounds, inline leaf/package-content URLs, case-insensitive package ID identity, page-range membership, and duplicate/malformed inline versions. Fully inlined, request-bound coherent indexes are `ready`; a documented index whose page leaves are omitted is `partial` rather than empty; mixed trusted/malformed inline leaves are `partial` with bad rows hidden; malformed response envelopes, unsupported request identities, or explicit request/provider package contradictions are `invalid`. The highest registered version is derived from validated page upper bounds using NuGet-compatible four-part/SemVer precedence rather than array position. Agent-readable DOM exposes requested/provider package identity, identity match, provider/valid/invalid page counts, registered/inline/valid/invalid version counts, omitted-page count, and lowest/highest registered versions from the same SSOT used by the human card.

Verification: regression-first NuGet coverage failed **7/7** against the prior implementation and passes after remediation. Focused NuGet/catalog/App coverage passes **130/130**. The complete Vitest gate passes **460/460 across 60 files** in deterministic shards (**126 + 132 + 202**), and `npm run build:pages` passes. The permanent real GitHub-Pages-origin `test:browser:nuget-package` verifier maps live `Newtonsoft.Json` registration facts exactly into semantic DOM (**2 pages / 84 registered versions / 84 inline leaves / highest bound `13.0.5-beta1`**), then exercises live `NuGet.Versioning` as the documented non-inlined case (**3 pages / 176 versions / 0 inline / 3 omitted pages**) and proves a synthetic wrong-package HTTP-200 registration is `invalid` with fabricated identity/version facts hidden. The live card passes 390x844 reflow with zero unnamed actionable controls. Preview-bundle isolation/CSS budgets/failure recovery pass with initial CSS **45,707 decoded bytes** and the Specialized representative at **163,569 decoded result-asset bytes / 21,798 decoded result CSS bytes**. Native WebMCP/provider-policy verification passes with **195 catalog APIs / 193 structured-agent runnable**. No live NuGet.org CORS/provider regression was observed. No files were staged, committed, pushed, or published.


### 2026-09-12 · RubyGems direct package identity + download-counter semantic hardening (local-only)

A focused Developer-ecosystem audit found that `rubygems-lookup` still routed a direct package lookup through the generic `DeveloperFeedPreview`. Any non-empty HTTP-success object could therefore become a plausible gem card without proving that the provider-owned `name` matched the actually executed `/api/v1/gems/{gem}.json` request, and permissive numeric helpers could turn missing or malformed download fields into misleading display values. RubyGems' current API documentation defines the direct gem endpoint and its provider-owned `name`, `downloads`, `version`, `version_created_at`, `version_downloads`, `platform`, `authors`, `info`, `licenses`, `yanked`, and link/dependency metadata. Fresh 2026-09-12 probes from the Pages origin context confirmed `rails` and `nokogiri` as HTTP 200 JSON with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`; `Rails` returned HTTP 404, so this candidate deliberately does not invent a lowercase/case-folded package identity rule. Official contract: https://guides.rubygems.org/rubygems-org-api/ .

The local candidate now uses a dedicated `package-release` semantic adapter. A bound request must be the exact HTTPS RubyGems direct-gem endpoint with one encoded gem path component and no query/hash. The provider response must be a plain object with non-empty provider-owned `name` and `version`; a request/provider gem contradiction fails closed as `invalid` and withholds all plausible package facts. `downloads` and `version_downloads` are trusted only when they are finite, non-negative JSON numbers; numeric strings, negatives, and missing counters are not coerced and instead make an otherwise trustworthy package `partial`. A valid provider response without executed-request identity is also `partial`. RubyGems can legitimately return `licenses: null`, so that value remains a trustworthy “licence unavailable” condition rather than schema failure. Human UI and Agent-Readable DOM share the same parsed semantic model and expose requested/provider gem identity, identity match, provider version, trusted total/version download counters, platform, licence count, and download-contract status. The obsolete RubyGems branch was removed from the generic Developer feed adapter.

### 2026-09-12 · Hex.pm direct-package identity + release/download semantic hardening (local-only)

A focused Developer-ecosystem audit found that `hexpm` still routed the official direct package response through `DeveloperFeedPreview`, but that generic family component had no Hex.pm branch. A healthy HTTP-success package response therefore collapsed to `Developer records unavailable` instead of a semantic package result, and there was no request/provider identity binding to prevent a wrong-package HTTP-200 body from becoming plausible package metadata if a future generic fallback broadened. Hex.pm's official HTTP API specification defines `GET /packages/{name}` with provider-owned package `name`, `repository`, metadata, latest version, optional downloads, optional releases, optional owners, and package URL; when present, downloads contain all-time/recent/week/day counters and releases carry version, release URL, docs availability, and insertion time. Fresh 2026-09-12 probes of `https://hex.pm/api/packages/ecto` returned HTTP 200 JSON with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, `repository=hexpm`, `latest_version=latest_stable_version=3.14.2`, **194 releases**, and at verification time **146,785,342 all-time / 4,559,972 recent-90-day downloads**. An uppercase `Ecto` direct lookup returned HTTP 404, so the local candidate deliberately does not invent a case-folded package-identity rule. Official contract: https://github.com/hexpm/specifications/blob/main/apiary.apib .

The local candidate now uses a dedicated `hex-package` semantic adapter and layout. A bound request must be the exact HTTPS `hex.pm/api/packages/{name}` direct lookup; the provider package must identify the public `hexpm` repository, provide a valid latest semantic version, and expose a provider-owned package URL identifying the same name. Request/provider identity contradictions or internal name/package-URL contradictions fail closed as `invalid` and withhold package facts. Download counters are trusted only as finite, non-negative JSON numbers and are never string-coerced. The release collection remains optional per the provider contract: an omitted collection is `partial`, not fabricated `empty`; when present, each trusted release must have a semantic version, coherent Hex release URL for the same package/version, boolean `has_docs`, and insertion timestamp. Mixed trusted/malformed releases become `partial` with malformed rows hidden, while latest/latest-stable identities must agree with the validated release set when that set is present. Missing executed-request identity is also `partial`. Human UI and Agent-Readable DOM share the same semantic model and expose requested/provider package identity, identity match, provider/valid/invalid release counts, trusted latest versions, validated download counters, and optional owner/licence counts without manufacturing zero for omitted optional members.

### 2026-09-12 · MetaCPAN exact-module request + semantic search hardening (local-only)

A focused Developer-ecosystem audit found both a request-contract defect and a missing semantic preview for `metacpan`. The catalog sent the user's text directly as Elasticsearch's unqualified `q` value (`q=Mojolicious&size=6`). Fresh 2026-09-12 Pages-origin-shaped probes showed that this does not mean “find this module”: the default query returned `hits.total=10000` with unrelated/historical file records, and an intentionally nonexistent unqualified query also returned unrelated hits. MetaCPAN's official API documentation describes its search API as Elasticsearch-based and documents field-qualified GET `q=` searches, including `status:latest`; the exact query `module.name:"Mojolicious" AND status:latest` returned one current Mojolicious hit, `module.name:"DBIx::Class" AND status:latest` returned one current DBIx::Class hit, and an intentionally nonexistent exact module returned `total=0,hits=[]`. The same provider currently executes from the real GitHub Pages origin with browser CORS enabled. Official API documentation: https://github.com/metacpan/metacpan-api/blob/master/docs/API-docs.md .

The local catalog now describes an exact latest-module lookup and builds a safely JSON-quoted `module.name:"<module>" AND status:latest` query while preserving the bounded 1..20 result limit and stable catalog ID. MetaCPAN no longer routes through the generic `DeveloperFeedPreview`, which had no MetaCPAN branch and turned healthy provider responses into `Developer records unavailable`. A dedicated lazy `cpan-module-search` adapter now binds the exact executed HTTPS search URL to the response. It requires the documented `hits.hits[]` search envelope, non-negative provider total, `timed_out=false` for readiness, provider hit identity, `source.status=latest`, distribution/release/version, and a `source.module[]` entry whose provider-owned name exactly matches the requested module. Untrusted rows are hidden rather than renamed. Human UI and Agent-Readable DOM share the same parsed model and expose request binding, provider/valid/invalid/incomplete hit counts, provider total, timeout/count-contract state, and primary module/distribution/release/version facts.

### 2026-09-12 · PyPI normalized project identity + current-metadata semantic hardening (local-only)

A focused Developer-ecosystem audit found that `pypi-json` still routed the direct `GET /pypi/<project>/json` response through the generic `DeveloperFeedPreview`. A transport-success object could therefore become a plausible Python package card without proving that provider `info.name` identified the project actually requested, and the generic card presented a release count derived from `releases` even though PyPI now marks that project-level key as deprecated. PyPI's current JSON API documentation also marks legacy `downloads`, `has_sig`, and `bugtrack_url` as deprecated and says they may be removed. The PyPA name-normalization specification requires lookup/comparison names to be lowercased with runs of period, underscore, and hyphen collapsed to one hyphen. Fresh 2026-09-12 probes of `https://pypi.org/pypi/requests/json`, its case-equivalent `Requests`, and `zope.interface` returned HTTP 200 JSON with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`; `requests` identified provider project `requests`, latest version `2.34.2`, Python requirement `>=3.10`, and an empty vulnerabilities array at verification time. Official contracts: https://docs.pypi.org/api/json/ and https://packaging.python.org/en/latest/specifications/name-normalization/ .

The local candidate now routes PyPI through a dedicated lazy `python-package` semantic adapter. A bound request must be the exact HTTPS `pypi.org/pypi/{project}/json` project endpoint with one valid project-name path component and no query/hash. Request/provider identity uses the official PyPA normalization rule rather than naive case equality, so equivalent spellings such as `Requests` / `requests` and `zope_interface` / `zope.interface` bind correctly. A wrong-project HTTP-200 response, unsupported executed URL, missing `info` object, invalid provider project name, or missing latest-version identity fails closed as `invalid` and withholds plausible package facts. Missing executed-request identity or malformed current optional metadata becomes `partial`; only a coherent request-bound current project record is `ready`. The adapter intentionally does not depend on deprecated `releases`, `downloads`, `has_sig`, or `bugtrack_url` for readiness and no longer manufactures a zero release count when `releases` is omitted. Human UI and Agent-Readable DOM share the same parsed model and expose requested/provider project names, normalized identities, identity match, current provider version, yanked state, and validated vulnerability count. The obsolete PyPI branch was removed from the generic Developer feed adapter, and the catalog usage note now documents the deprecated-field boundary.

### 2026-09-12 · pub.dev Hosted Pub v2 request + direct-package semantic hardening (local-only)

A focused Developer-ecosystem audit found that `pub-dev` still routed the direct `GET /api/packages/{package}` response through `DeveloperFeedPreview`. The generic branch accepted any non-empty object, did not bind provider `name` to the executed package URL, did not validate the documented `latest`/`versions[]` Hosted Pub response contract, and had no explicit semantic result state. A malformed or wrong-package HTTP-success body could therefore become plausible Dart/Flutter package metadata. The official Hosted Pub Repository Specification v2 defines `GET <hosted-url>/api/packages/<package>` and the package response with provider-owned `name`, `latest` metadata (`version`, `archive_url`, `pubspec`, optional checksum), and `versions[]`; it also strongly encourages clients to send `Accept: application/vnd.pub.v2+json`. pub.dev's official API help points package clients to that hosted-repository contract. Official references: https://pub.dev/help/api and https://github.com/dart-lang/pub/blob/master/doc/repository-spec-v2.md .

Fresh 2026-09-12 origin-shaped probes confirmed the practical request boundary before editing: `https://pub.dev/api/packages/riverpod` returned HTTP 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, provider `name=riverpod`, `latest.version=3.4.3`, matching latest pubspec identity, and 116 version records. `Riverpod` and an intentionally nonexistent package both returned provider HTTP 404 with browser CORS enabled, so this direct lookup does not invent an HTTP-200 semantic-empty package state or case-folded identity rule. The local candidate now uses a dedicated lazy `dart-package` semantic adapter. The adapter requires the documented package/latest/versions envelope; the latest release must carry coherent provider package/version pubspec identity plus a secure archive URL; version-history rows must preserve the same package/version identity and archive contract; duplicate or malformed rows are hidden; and the provider latest version must exist in the trusted version history. Wrong-package HTTP-200 data, malformed latest identity, unsupported executed URLs, a package with no trusted versions, or a latest/history contradiction fail closed as `invalid`. Mixed trustworthy/malformed version history is `partial`; trustworthy provider metadata without executed-request identity is also `partial`; a fully coherent request-bound response is `ready`. Optional checksum/published/retracted, SDK, topics, repository/homepage, and discontinuation/replacement metadata are validated without making undocumented release-order assumptions. Human UI and Agent-Readable DOM share the same parsed model and expose requested/provider package identity, request binding, provider/valid/invalid/incomplete version counts, trusted latest version, latest-in-history relationship, SDK constraint, and discontinuation/replacement state. The obsolete generic pub.dev branch was removed.

Exact synthetic HTTP-200 fixtures prove a wrong-package response becomes `invalid` with fabricated package/version facts hidden and a mixed malformed version batch becomes `partial` with only the trusted version visible. ### 2026-09-12 · npm Registry Search request SSOT + semantic-result hardening (local-only)

Core download counters, publisher/update metadata, and raw search score are validated without numeric-string coercion or fabricated zeros; malformed optional/core metrics make an otherwise identifiable row `partial`, and structurally unidentifiable rows are hidden. ### Packagist Search request/pagination + numeric-download semantic hardening (2026-09-12)

A focused Developer-catalog audit found a concrete semantic bug in `packagist-search`: the prior generic `DeveloperFeedPreview` treated each search result's `downloads` field as an object and read `downloads.total`, but the documented Packagist Search API returns `downloads` directly as a number. A healthy live package therefore degraded to a plausible but false `0 downloads` badge. The official search contract also exposes `results`, `total`, and optional `next` pagination metadata, while the old generic path had no explicit request binding or `invalid / empty / partial / ready` result state. The local candidate now routes Packagist through an API-owned `PackagistSearchPreview` and the domain-meaningful `composer-package-search` layout. The semantic adapter binds an executed HTTPS `packagist.org/search.json` request to `q`, `per_page`, and optional `page`; requires a documented `results[]` envelope plus a non-negative integer provider total; validates each trusted package through its provider name and matching canonical `https://packagist.org/packages/{name}` URL; preserves `downloads` and `favers` only when they are genuine non-negative integer JSON numbers; and validates `next` only as the documented next page for the same query/page size. It deliberately does not require result package names to equal the search term. A coherent request-bound zero-result page is `empty`; malformed/unusable envelope or no trustworthy package rows is `invalid`; mixed malformed/incomplete rows, missing request identity, or inconsistent count/pagination metadata is `partial`; and a coherent request-bound page is `ready`. Human cards and Agent-Readable DOM now share the same parsed model and expose query/page-size/page plus provider/valid/invalid/incomplete counts and count/pagination contract flags. ### 2026-09-12 · deps.dev GetPackage identity + capability-honesty hardening (local-only)

A focused Developer-adapter audit found that `deps-dev` still routed the direct v3 GetPackage response through the generic `DeveloperFeedPreview`. The old card trusted `packageKey` and `versions[]` without binding them to the executed `/v3/systems/{system}/packages/{name}` request, fell back to the first array element when no provider default existed, and had no explicit `invalid / empty / partial / ready` state. The catalog also overstated this endpoint as returning dependencies, licenses, and security advisories. The official deps.dev v3 contract says GetPackage returns package identity and available versions, with `isDefault`, publication timing, and deprecation metadata; licenses/advisories belong to the separate GetVersion operation. It also documents ecosystem-native package identity with explicit canonicalization exceptions for PyPI and NuGet. Official contract: https://docs.deps.dev/api/v3/ .

The local candidate now routes `deps-dev` through a dedicated lazy `package-insights` adapter. A bound result must come from the exact HTTPS deps.dev v3 GetPackage path. The response system must match the executed ecosystem and the provider-owned package name must match the request under documented ecosystem rules: PyPI names use PEP-503-style lowercase `[-_.]+ -> -` normalization, NuGet compares case-insensitively, and the other supported systems keep exact package identity. Each trusted version row must preserve the provider `packageKey` system/name, non-empty version identity, and boolean `isDefault` / `isDeprecated`; malformed or duplicate rows are hidden, malformed optional publication/deprecation fields make the result `partial`, and an explicit request/provider package contradiction fails closed as `invalid` with package facts withheld. A request-bound zero-version response is `empty`. Zero or one provider default is accepted because the contract says the default is marked only when known; multiple defaults are contradictory, become `partial`, and no default is chosen by array order. The card derives “most recently published” only from validated timestamps rather than assuming provider array order. Human UI and Agent-Readable DOM share the same parsed model and expose request/provider ecosystem and package identity, canonicalization/match state, provider/valid/invalid/incomplete version counts, default-version contract, deprecation count, and most-recent publication facts. The catalog description/keywords were narrowed from unsupported vulnerability/license/dependency claims to the actual GetPackage capability.

Fresh 2026-09-12 provider probes from the GitHub-Pages origin context showed `npm/react` HTTP 200 with browser CORS `*`, provider identity `NPM/react`, **2,945 versions**, exactly one default (`19.3.0` at verification time), and no malformed/duplicate version rows in the live response. `pypi/Requests` returned canonical provider identity `PYPI/requests`, demonstrating the documented normalization boundary, and scoped npm `@colors/colors` also executed successfully. The permanent `test:browser:deps-dev` verifier maps the live `react` response exactly into semantic DOM in `ready` state, observes **2 deprecated versions** at verification time, passes 390x844 reflow with **0 unnamed actionable controls**, and proves a synthetic wrong-package HTTP-200 response becomes `invalid` with fabricated package/version facts hidden. Focused deps.dev/catalog/App coverage passes **133/133**. The complete current Vitest set passes **517/517 across all 67 files** in deterministic exact-cover shards (**169 + 230 + 118**); a durable-job attempt was rejected only because this legacy review workspace is outside VMMCP's managed job-workspace roots, so the completed evidence comes from the bounded exact-cover shards rather than treating admission failure as a product failure. `npm run build:pages`, native WebMCP/provider-policy verification, preview-bundle isolation/CSS budgets/failure recovery, and `git diff --check` pass.
