### 2026-09-12 · GitLab public-project search identity + numeric-honesty hardening (local-only)

A focused Developer-feed audit found that `gitlab-public-projects` still used the generic `DeveloperFeedPreview`. That path converted missing or malformed `star_count` / `forks_count` into plausible zeroes, used an unavailable `language` property as display context, and had no binding between the rendered projects and the executed public search. GitLab's current Projects API documents unauthenticated `GET /projects` as returning public projects, supports `search`, `visibility`, `order_by`, `sort`, and pagination parameters, and defines search as a case-insensitive substring match over path/name/description; multiple search terms are ANDed. Fresh 2026-09-12 origin-shaped evidence for the default `visibility=public&search=artificial intelligence&order_by=star_count&sort=desc&per_page=8` request returned HTTP 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, eight public project rows, and pagination headers exposed to browser clients. All eight live rows satisfied both search terms in the documented searchable fields. Official contract: https://docs.gitlab.com/api/projects/ .

The local candidate now routes GitLab through a dedicated lazy `project-search` semantic adapter. A bound request must be the exact HTTPS GitLab v4 Projects endpoint with the supported public visibility, star-count descending sort, non-empty search, and bounded 1..20 page size. Each trusted row requires a positive integer provider project ID, public visibility, non-empty name/path identity, and a canonical `https://gitlab.com/{path_with_namespace}` project URL. When request identity is present, every search term must be acknowledged by the provider row under the documented path/name/description search semantics. Star/fork counts are trusted only as non-negative integer JSON numbers; malformed counters are withheld instead of manufactured as zero. Optional description/activity/topics are validated without inventing unsupported language or issue-count claims. A request-bound empty array is semantic `empty`; malformed envelope, unsupported request identity, or no trustworthy/search-coherent rows is `invalid`; mixed contradictory/malformed rows, malformed optional metrics, result count beyond the requested limit, or missing executed-request identity is `partial`; only a coherent request-bound result page is `ready`. Human UI and Agent-Readable DOM share the same parsed model and expose search query/limit, request binding, provider/valid/invalid/incomplete counts, and query/count-contract state. The obsolete GitLab branch was removed from the generic Developer feed.

### 2026-09-13 · Stack Exchange tagged-question request/wrapper + throttle-policy hardening (local-only)

A focused Developer-feed audit found that `stack-exchange` still depended on a permissive generic question renderer: malformed or missing engagement counters could become plausible zeroes, returned questions were not bound to the actually executed site/tag/sort/page-size request, and the provider's common-wrapper quota/backoff contract was not represented as semantic result evidence. Stack Exchange's current `/2.3/questions` documentation defines `tagged` as a semicolon-delimited **AND** constraint with at most five tags and defines `activity` sorting by `last_activity_date`. Its common wrapper requires `items`, `has_more`, `quota_max`, and `quota_remaining`, with optional `backoff`; the throttle documentation requires applications to wait the supplied backoff before calling the same method again and says semantically identical requests should not be made more than once per minute because responses are heavily cached. Official references: https://api.stackexchange.com/docs/questions , https://api.stackexchange.com/docs/wrapper , and https://api.stackexchange.com/docs/throttle .

The local catalog now exposes one-to-five required tags plus a bounded 1..20 question count as shared Human/Agent SSOT, builds the exact Stack Overflow activity request, and encodes provider automated verification as `cadence-limited` with a 60-second minimum interval and no same-run retry after non-2xx. A dedicated lazy `community-questions` adapter now accepts only the supported HTTPS `/2.3/questions` request identity (`site=stackoverflow`, `sort=activity`, `order=desc`, bounded page size and unique requested tags). Trusted rows require positive question identity, a canonical Stack Overflow question URL, a non-empty title/tag set, and—when request-bound—every requested tag. Score, answer count, view count, answer state, creation time, and last-activity time are validated without string/negative coercion; malformed metrics remain visibly unavailable and make the row `partial` rather than manufacturing zero. The wrapper validates `has_more`, quota bounds, optional positive backoff, and response-count/page-size coherence. A coherent request-bound zero-item wrapper with `has_more=false` is semantic `empty`; malformed envelopes, unsupported executed request identity, or a non-empty batch with no trusted question is `invalid`; mixed tag-contradictory/malformed rows, incomplete metrics, wrapper uncertainty, or missing request identity is `partial`; only a complete request-bound response is `ready`. Human UI and Agent-Readable DOM share the same parsed ViewModel and expose requested site/tags/sort/order/page size, provider/valid/invalid/incomplete counts, tag/count/wrapper contract flags, quota, and backoff.

Verification: focused Stack Exchange/catalog/App coverage passes **131/131**. A first large full-suite shard exposed one unrelated existing i18n workspace test that could legitimately take slightly more than Vitest's default 5-second timeout under the current VM/candidate load; the exact test passed when isolated with a wider timeout, so its own timeout was stabilized to the existing **12-second** pattern used by other heavy App integration tests rather than changing product behavior. Because this legacy review workspace is outside VMMCP's managed durable-job roots, the complete unit set was then run as deterministic exact-cover groups and passes **531/531 across 69 files**: App coverage **81/81**, plus the remaining 68 files as **164 + 115 + 171**. `npm run build:pages` passes. Exact synthetic HTTP-200 fixtures prove a tag-contradictory fabricated question is hidden and makes the mixed batch `partial`, and malformed string/negative counters remain unavailable while a valid `backoff=60` is surfaced without being treated as schema failure. Native WebMCP/provider-policy verification passes with **195 catalog APIs / 193 structured-agent runnable** and explicitly verifies Stack Exchange's 60-second cadence, no-non2xx-retry policy, tag constraint schema, and throttle policy URL. ### 2026-09-13 · HN Algolia story/comment OR request + search-acknowledgement hardening (local-only)

A focused News/developer-search audit found a real request-contract defect in `hn-search-algolia`, not merely a rendering gap. The Human/WebMCP `Content type` field offered “Stories and comments” with raw value `story,comment`. HN Search documents comma-separated tags as **AND** by default and requires parenthesized syntax for OR, for example `(story,poll)`. Fresh 2026-09-13 probes with the GitHub Pages origin reproduced the impact for the same `OpenAI` query and six-result limit: `tags=story,comment` returned HTTP 200 with **0 hits**, while the documented `tags=(story,comment)` returned HTTP 200 with a large non-zero result set; `story` and `comment` individually also returned normal results. The provider's HTTP-200 search wrapper echoes `query`, `hitsPerPage`, and serialized `params`, which gives this browser workbench a direct acknowledgement surface for the actual search it executed. Official contract: https://hn.algolia.com/api .

The local catalog now represents the combined content-type choice with the provider-correct `(story,comment)` OR syntax and fail-closes unknown tag values back to the supported story default. HN search no longer routes through the generic `DeveloperFeedPreview`; a dedicated lazy `hn-search` adapter binds the exact HTTPS `/api/v1/search` request to provider-echoed query/tag/result-limit metadata. The adapter requires the documented `hits[]` plus non-negative page/total/page-count/processing metadata and validates `objectID`, `_tags`, content type, timestamp, story engagement counters, and comment story/parent context without manufacturing zeroes. A request-bound coherent zero-result search is `empty`; malformed envelopes or provider acknowledgement that contradicts the executed query/tag/limit are `invalid`; mixed malformed/duplicate/content-type-contradictory hits or incomplete optional metrics are `partial`, with untrusted hits hidden; only a coherent request-bound batch is `ready`. Story and comment semantics remain type-aware: story points/comment counts are trusted only as non-negative integers, while the provider's normal null story-level metrics on comment hits are not rewritten as zero or treated as story data. Human cards and Agent-Readable DOM share one ViewModel and expose query/tag/limit binding, provider total/page/hit counts, valid/invalid/incomplete counts, count contract, and primary item identity/kind/timestamp/engagement.

Exact synthetic HTTP-200 fixtures prove a missing `hits` envelope becomes `invalid` and a story-only response containing a contradictory comment becomes `partial` with that comment hidden. ### 2026-09-13 · GitHub owned-public-repository identity + archive-state honesty hardening (local-only)

A focused Developer-feed audit found that `github` still routed the fixed Octocat repository listing through the generic `DeveloperFeedPreview`. That renderer treated any falsy or missing `archived` value as the badge **Active**, even though GitHub supplies an `archived` boolean and the absence of that field does not establish an independently documented “active” lifecycle state. It also displayed stars, forks, and open-issue counts without strict numeric validation and did not bind returned `owner.login`, `full_name`, or `html_url` identity to the executed `/users/octocat/repos` request. GitHub's current REST documentation defines the public user-repository endpoint, its owner/repository/status/count fields, unauthenticated public-resource access, the recommended `application/vnd.github+json` media type, and `type` / `sort` / `direction` / `per_page` request semantics. GitHub's versioning documentation currently lists **2026-03-10** as a supported REST API version and recommends the `X-GitHub-Api-Version` header. The REST API supports browser CORS, and unauthenticated requests are subject to the documented primary rate limit. Official references: https://docs.github.com/en/rest/repos/repos#list-repositories-for-a-user , https://docs.github.com/en/rest/about-the-rest-api/api-versions , https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/using-cors-and-jsonp-to-make-cross-origin-requests , and https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/rate-limits-for-the-rest-api .

The local catalog now makes the request contract explicit with `type=owner`, `sort=full_name`, `direction=asc`, `per_page=8`, the recommended media type, and `X-GitHub-Api-Version: 2026-03-10`, while preserving the stable `github` catalog ID and Octocat demo scope. A dedicated lazy `repository-list` adapter now binds the exact executed HTTPS user-repository request to provider-owned repository identity. Trusted rows require a positive provider repository ID, coherent `name` / `full_name` / `owner.login`, `private=false`, matching public visibility when supplied, and an exact canonical `https://github.com/{full_name}` URL. Stars, forks, and open-issue/PR counts are trusted only as genuine non-negative integer JSON numbers. `fork`, `archived`, and `disabled` are validated booleans; a missing archive state is displayed as **Archive state unavailable** and makes the result `partial` rather than manufacturing **Active**. A request-bound empty array is semantic `empty`; malformed envelopes, unsupported executed request identity, or a non-empty batch with no trustworthy owner-bound repository is `invalid`; mixed wrong-owner/canonical-URL rows or incomplete status/metric data are `partial` with untrusted rows or values hidden; only a coherent request-bound batch is `ready`. Human cards and Agent-Readable DOM share one parsed model and expose requested owner/page size, request binding, provider/valid/invalid/incomplete counts, owner contract, and count contract.

The same live journey passes 390x844 reflow with **0 unnamed actionable controls**. ### 2026-09-13 · JSONPlaceholder direct-post identity hardening + generic developer-feed retirement (local-only)

A final generic-preview audit found that `posts` was the only remaining catalog route still using `DeveloperFeedPreview`. The route already exposed a bounded `postId` input and executed `GET https://jsonplaceholder.typicode.com/posts/{id}`, but the generic renderer accepted any non-empty HTTP-success object and displayed its `id`, `userId`, title, and body without binding provider identity to the executed request or validating the documented resource shape. JSONPlaceholder's current official site describes the service as a fake REST API for testing/prototyping, documents exactly **100 posts** and **10 users**, and shows `GET /posts/1` returning the post fields `id`, `title`, `body`, and `userId`. Fresh 2026-09-13 probes with the GitHub Pages origin confirmed `/posts/7` and `/posts/100` as HTTP 200 JSON with browser CORS admitting `https://yapweijun1996.github.io`; `/posts/101` and an intentionally huge ID returned HTTP 404 with `{}`, so the candidate does not invent an HTTP-200 semantic-empty state for a missing direct post. Official references: https://jsonplaceholder.typicode.com/ and https://jsonplaceholder.typicode.com/guide/ .

The local candidate now routes `posts` through a dedicated lazy `rest-post` adapter. A bound request must be the exact HTTPS JSONPlaceholder `/posts/{id}` resource URL with an ID in the documented 1–100 range. A trustworthy provider post requires an integer `id` in that range; an executed-request/provider ID contradiction fails closed as `invalid` and withholds the fabricated post. `userId` is trusted only as an integer in the documented 1–10 user-resource range, and title/body must be non-empty strings. Missing or malformed author/content fields keep a trustworthy post identity visible but resolve the card to `partial`, showing unavailable content rather than coercing strings/numbers or manufacturing a user. A coherent request-bound post is `ready`; a valid post without executed-request identity is `partial`; an empty object or unusable HTTP-success payload is `invalid`. Human UI and Agent-Readable DOM share the same ViewModel and expose request binding, requested/provider post IDs, identity match, author user ID, and content/author contract flags.

This migration also retires the generic developer-feed architecture entirely: `developer-feed` is removed from the `PreviewLayout` union and profile registry, `DeveloperFeedPreview` is removed from `responsePreview.tsx` and `CatalogFamilyPreviews.tsx`, and a permanent architecture test requires zero generic developer-feed profiles or renderer references. Regression-first JSONPlaceholder coverage failed **6/6** against the previous generic implementation and passes after remediation. Focused JSONPlaceholder/catalog/architecture/App coverage passes **138/138**. The complete current Vitest set passes **573/573 across all 75 files** in deterministic exact-cover shards (**171 + 207 + 106 + 89**), and `npm run build:pages` passes. The permanent real GitHub-Pages-origin `test:browser:jsonplaceholder-post` verifier maps live post **#7 / author user #1** into semantic DOM with state `ready`, passes 390x844 reflow with **0 unnamed actionable controls**, proves a wrong-ID HTTP-200 fixture becomes `invalid`, proves malformed author/content fixtures become `partial` with untrusted values withheld, and proves HTTP-200 `{}` remains `invalid` rather than fabricated `empty`. Preview-bundle isolation/CSS budgets/failure recovery pass with initial CSS **45,707 decoded bytes** and the Specialized representative at **258,789 decoded result-asset bytes / 21,798 decoded result CSS bytes**. The removal of the last generic renderer also leaves the CatalogFamily JavaScript chunk at about **39.61 kB** in the Pages build. Native WebMCP/provider-policy verification passes with **195 catalog APIs / 193 structured-agent runnable**. No current JSONPlaceholder browser-CORS/provider-schema drift was observed. All work remains local-only and unpublished.

### 2026-09-13 · unstaged local-candidate hygiene coverage hardening (local-only)

A final P0/P1 publication-readiness review found a concrete blind spot in the release gate rather than a product/provider defect. The persistent autonomous candidate currently contains a large amount of intentionally unstaged work, including many new semantic adapters, regression tests, and browser verifiers. `test:publish-readiness` ended with plain `git diff --check`, but Git does not include untracked files in that check. Immediately before this remediation the worktree had **119 tracked candidate files plus 90 untracked candidate files**, so a substantial part of the exact local candidate could carry trailing whitespace or unresolved merge markers while the advertised release gate still passed. Staging those files only to make them visible would violate the autonomous publication boundary.

The local candidate now adds a read-only `test:candidate-hygiene` gate. It derives tracked candidate paths from `git diff --name-only HEAD` and untracked candidate paths from `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard`; tracked changes are checked with `git diff HEAD --check`, every untracked file is checked with Git's no-index whitespace checker, and all changed/untracked text files are scanned for unresolved `<<<<<<<` / `=======` / `>>>>>>>` merge markers. Binary files are skipped only for the text-marker scan. The verifier never calls `git add`, never mutates the index, and reports tracked/untracked coverage counts explicitly. `test:publish-readiness` now invokes this gate instead of relying on plain `git diff --check`. Permanent unit tests prove a clean unstaged tracked+untracked candidate passes while an untracked trailing-whitespace file and a tracked merge-marker file both fail detection.

Regression-first implementation evidence also exposed the exact Git status semantics that the helper needed to handle: `git diff --no-index --check` returns a distinct non-zero status when a whitespace defect exists, and `git diff HEAD --check` returns a non-zero status for leftover conflict markers. The helper treats those statuses as inspection results rather than command-execution failures, preserving the actual Git diagnostics. After the fix, focused helper coverage passes **3/3** and the real current candidate hygiene check passes with **0 whitespace errors / 0 unresolved conflict markers / no staging required**. This is release-evidence hardening only; no provider request, API semantics, Human UI, WebMCP contract, bundle boundary, or provider-health claim changed.

### 2026-09-13 · signal-aware browser cleanup + breakpoint focus-race hardening (local-only)

A final release-candidate run turned two previously intermittent verification warnings into concrete root causes. First, the browser harnesses decided whether Chromium had exited by checking only Node `ChildProcess.exitCode`. A direct process probe proved that a child terminated normally by `SIGTERM` reports `exitCode=null` with `signalCode='SIGTERM'`; the old condition could therefore misclassify an already-terminated browser as still running and leave its owned `public-api-*-profile-*` directory behind for the next stale reaper. Second, the first complete publish-readiness attempt in this cycle reproduced the earlier mobile-navigation-to-desktop focus timeout. The application transferred focus only when the `matchMedia` callback still observed `document.activeElement === mobileCloseRef.current`. In a real browser the CSS breakpoint can hide and blur that mobile-only close button before the JavaScript media-query listener runs, making the handoff timing-dependent even though the navigation was still known to be open.

The local candidate now centralizes Chromium termination in `scripts/lib/browser-temp-profile.mjs`. `hasChildExited` treats either a numeric `exitCode` or a non-null `signalCode` as terminal, and `terminateChildProcess` waits for graceful `SIGTERM`, escalates to `SIGKILL` only when necessary, and reports whether termination was actually observed. Both the PWA verifier and shared Pages-origin browser harness remove an owned profile only after confirmed child exit; otherwise they fail explicitly and preserve the profile for safe owner-bound cleanup. Direct regression tests cover the signal-terminated `exitCode=null` case and prove a graceful signal does not trigger a redundant force kill.

For the responsive focus race, `App.tsx` now tracks the mobile-navigation open state in a ref that the long-lived media-query listener can read without stale closure state. Crossing above 760px while that modal navigation is open always hands focus to the active persistent navigation item before closing the mobile state, rather than depending on whether the browser has already blurred the disappearing close control. The existing unit test now deliberately blurs the close button before firing the breakpoint transition, reproducing the real browser event ordering that the old implementation could miss.

Verification is complete. Focused browser-profile cleanup tests pass **6/6** and focused App integration passes **82/82**. The full current Vitest suite passes **585/585 across all 77 files**. Strict `npm run build:pages` passes. The real GitHub-Pages-origin i18n/focus verifier passes **three consecutive standalone runs** after the focus fix, preserving the active-navigation handoff, 390px no-overflow, **130/130 compact touch targets >=44x44 CSS px**, and **0 unnamed actionable controls** with zero live provider requests. PWA install/offline-shell verification passes with the owned PWA profile count returning immediately to **0**, and the Pages-origin preview-bundle/failure-recovery harness passes with its owned profile count also returning immediately to **0**. A complete `npm run test:publish-readiness` rerun then passes end-to-end: **585/585 Vitest**, Pages build, PWA, i18n/focus, preview bundles, native WebMCP/provider policy (**195 catalog / 193 structured-agent runnable / 191 generic automated-verification-eligible**), and candidate hygiene (**119 tracked + 93 untracked candidate files, 0 whitespace errors, 0 conflict markers, no staging required**). Initial CSS remains **43,113 decoded bytes**. This cycle changes no provider request or response semantics and makes no new live-provider/CORS health claim. All work remains local-only and unpublished.

### 2026-09-13 · OECD CLI startPeriod semantic-binding hardening (local-only)

A focused review of the newly admitted `oecd-cli` adapter found one remaining request-semantics gap. The catalog sends the official OECD `startPeriod=YYYY-01` filter, and the adapter already bound reference area, frequency, measure, unit, adjustment, methodology, and observation dimensions, but it did not prove that returned `TIME_PERIOD` observations respected the requested lower bound. An HTTP-200 response containing pre-start data could therefore still become semantic `ready`, which conflicts with the North Star rule that an ignored provider filter is a request-definition regression rather than a healthy result. OECD's current API documentation defines `startPeriod` as the inclusive start time period for supplied results, and a fresh provider probe confirmed `startPeriod=2025-01` begins at `2025-01` while `startPeriod=2026-06` begins at `2026-06`.

The local adapter now carries the normalized requested start period into its semantic request contract, rejects any observation whose mapped `TIME_PERIOD` is earlier than that lower bound, exposes `data-requested-start-period` plus `data-start-period-contract`, and degrades mixed HTTP-success data to `partial` while withholding the out-of-range value. A deterministic regression injects a plausible `2024-12 = 999.99` observation into a request whose lower bound is `2025-01`; the semantic DOM reports `partial`, counts the pre-start cell as invalid, marks the start-period contract false, and never displays `999.99`. The live Pages-origin branch independently asserts that every returned OECD period is `>= 2025-01` and currently remains `ready`.

Verification after the hardening: focused expansion/catalog/architecture coverage passes **58/58**; the complete deterministic exact-cover Vitest gate passes **592/592 across all 78 files** (**97 + 119 + 251 + 125**); strict `npm run build:pages` passes; `test:browser:public-api-200-expansion` passes all five live provider journeys plus the new synthetic OECD pre-start regression, with 390×844 no-overflow and **0 unnamed actionable controls**. The build still reports initial CSS **43,113 decoded bytes**; the Specialized preview bundle is about **66.39 kB JS / 19.27 kB gzip**, remaining within its existing result-path budget. No API count, WebMCP execution policy, provider admission status, or production state changed. All work remains local-only and unpublished.
### 2026-09-13 · OpenAlex HTTP 429 verification-backoff SSOT hardening (local-only)

A fresh real GitHub-Pages-origin health probe of `openalex-works-search` returned **HTTP 429** on its first request. This is preserved as unresolved point-in-time rate-limit evidence, not reclassified as a persistent outage: the current OpenAlex documentation says 429 can mean either the daily budget was exceeded or the request rate exceeded 100 requests/second, and it explicitly requires exponential backoff for 429 responses. The pre-existing generic health harness would have immediately retried any first-pass failure in fresh browsers, so an OpenAlex 429 could trigger two more same-run requests even though the provider had just asked the client to back off.

The local SSOT now supports an explicit enabled-provider rate-limit retry policy. `openalex-works-search` remains **agent enabled** and **generic automated-verification eligible**, but declares `retryOnRateLimit: false` with the official OpenAlex error-policy URL. The generated machine catalog, native WebMCP discovery, Request Lab DOM, Catalog row metadata, and compact/desktop detail surfaces all derive the same enabled policy; no second hand-maintained registry was introduced. A new pure `health-retry-policy` helper makes the retry decision deterministic: cadence-limited providers still fail closed outside the generic sweep, OpenAlex 429 evidence returns `rate-limit-backoff`, non-rate-limit OpenAlex failures remain eligible for bounded recovery, and default enabled providers preserve the existing retry behavior.

The live health harness now records `retryDeferrals`, `deferredRateLimits`, and `retryExhausted` separately. A real Pages-origin rerun with `HEALTH_RETRIES=2` again received OpenAlex HTTP 429, then logged **`retry deferred openalex-works-search: rate-limit-backoff`** and sent no same-run fresh-browser retries; the verdict remains `INVESTIGATE` because provider health is unresolved. This is a provider-respect fix, not a claim that OpenAlex is down. Current executable counts remain **200 catalog / 198 structured-agent runnable / 196 generic automated-verification eligible**, with LanguageTool/Nominatim manual-only and Stack Exchange/CelesTrak cadence-limited unchanged.

### 2026-09-13 · Open-Meteo seasonal forecast-horizon semantic correction (local-only)

A focused audit of the newly admitted `open-meteo-seasonal` contract found that the provider request was technically valid but the Human/agent SSOT described it incorrectly. The catalog exposed a synthetic `weeks=1..6` field and converted it to the provider's documented `forecast_days` parameter by multiplying by seven, while the semantic adapter allowed one extra returned weekly bucket. Fresh provider probes explained why that tolerance existed: with the same Singapore coordinates and requested weekly variables, `forecast_days=7` returned **2** calendar-labelled weekly buckets, `35` returned **6**, `42` returned **7**, and `46` returned **8**, all with browser-compatible `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. Open-Meteo's current Seasonal Forecast documentation describes EC46 as a forecast model reaching **46 days** ahead and separately states that weekly products are computed by ECMWF/EC46 for roughly the first six weeks. The returned weekly labels are therefore calendar-aligned aggregation buckets, not an exact count requested by a `weeks` input.

The local SSOT now exposes the provider concept directly as `forecastDays`: **1–46 days**, default **42**. Human form metadata, generated machine catalog and WebMCP all derive that same field instead of advertising a fictional exact week count. The usage note explicitly explains that a forecast-day horizon can intersect an extra partial boundary week. The dedicated semantic adapter now validates equal-length requested arrays, strict numeric measurements, official units/timezone, exact seven-day spacing between weekly labels, and a bounded calendar-bucket envelope of `ceil(forecastDays / 7)` through one additional boundary bucket. Agent-readable DOM exposes the requested day horizon, provider/valid/invalid bucket counts, array-length contract, weekly-cadence contract, expected bucket bounds, and horizon-count contract. A deterministic regression breaks one weekly date by one day and correctly yields `partial` rather than silently treating irregular provider data as a valid weekly series.

The permanent expansion browser verifier was also given a bounded `EXPANSION_IDS` scope so one provider can be reverified without contacting unrelated providers. This was required here because OpenAlex still has unresolved HTTP-429/backoff evidence from the prior cycle; the seasonal regression could therefore be verified without violating OpenAlex's no-same-run-retry/backoff intent. A real GitHub-Pages-origin seasonal-only run passed with **42 forecast days -> 7 calendar-aligned weekly buckets**, semantic state `ready`, 390×844 no overflow and **0 unnamed actionable controls**. Direct provider research also confirmed 43 days still maps to seven buckets and the EC46 46-day boundary maps to eight.

Verification after the correction: focused App/catalog/machine/expansion coverage passes **142/142**, the deterministic exact-cover Vitest suite passes **598/598 across all 79 files** (**132 + 94 + 120 + 252**), strict `npm run build:pages` passes, native WebMCP/provider-policy verification remains **200 catalog / 198 structured-agent runnable / 196 generic automated-verification eligible**, preview-bundle isolation/failure recovery passes with initial CSS **43,113 decoded bytes** and the Specialized representative at **168,261 decoded result-asset bytes / 23,255 decoded result CSS bytes**, and PWA install/offline-shell verification passes with a content-revisioned cache and **200 total** catalog entries visible offline. No OpenAlex request was sent during the seasonal browser recheck. All work remains local-only and unpublished.

### 2026-09-13 · SingStat CPI bounded latest-series request + provider acknowledgement (local-only)

A focused performance/semantic audit of the newly admitted `singstat-cpi-monthly` route found that its visual card only uses the latest 12 monthly All Items CPI observations, but the request still downloaded the table's complete history. The catalog request now asks SingStat directly for those latest 12 observations instead of downloading more than six decades and trimming in the browser. The dedicated adapter treats the provider's echoed `limit='12'` and `sortBy='key desc'`, the maximum-12 record envelope, and strict descending monthly keys as executable semantics. If an HTTP-success response ignores those request controls, the result now fails semantically closed instead of sorting an arbitrary bounded subset and presenting it as the latest CPI data. Agent-readable DOM exposes request/provider limit and sort values plus `data-query-contract`, `data-limit-contract`, and `data-order-contract`. The existing malformed-value path remains `partial`, so one unusable CPI value does not erase otherwise trustworthy provider observations.

Regression-first coverage includes a plausible stale-provider fixture that echoes `limit=5000`, omits descending sort acknowledgement, and returns 1961 values under the new request; the adapter resolves it to `invalid` and hides the fabricated old index. A real GitHub-Pages-origin run of the unpublished bundle then returned **12/12** validated observations, provider `limit=12`, provider `sortBy=key desc`, semantic state `ready`, latest **2026 Jul = 102.997**, 390×844 no-overflow, and **0 unnamed actionable controls**. ### 2026-09-13 · NHTSA NCAP comparison-scope semantic hardening (local-only)

A focused review of the newly admitted `nhtsa-safety-ratings` card found a semantic-interpretation gap rather than a transport or identity bug. The VehicleId request was already correctly bound to provider `VehicleId`, but the result card labelled `OverallRating` only as `Overall`, and abbreviated `OverallFrontCrashRating` / `OverallSideCrashRating` as `Front crash` / `Side crash`. Current NHTSA 5-Star Safety Ratings guidance uses the distinct term **Overall Vehicle Score** for the combined relative-injury-risk score and places important limits on cross-vehicle comparison: Overall Vehicle Scores and frontal crash ratings should only be compared within the same vehicle class and within 250 lb, while side and rollover ratings may be compared across vehicle classes. The VehicleId safety response does not expose the curb-weight/class facts required for this static browser demo to prove that an Overall/frontal competitive comparison is valid. Official references: `https://www.nhtsa.gov/ratings` and `https://www.nhtsa.gov/ratings/government-5-star-safety-ratings-motor-vehicles-advertising-and-communication-usage`.

The local candidate now uses the provider's official terminology in the Human UI: `Overall Vehicle Score`, `Overall frontal crash`, and `Overall side crash`. A visible comparison-scope note explains the same-class/±250 lb boundary and the cross-class side/rollover rule instead of allowing five-star values to look universally comparable. The same semantics are exposed to browser agents through stable metadata: `data-overall-score-meaning="relative-injury-risk"`, `data-overall-frontal-comparison-scope="same-class-plus-minus-250lb"`, `data-side-rollover-comparison-scope="cross-class"`, and `data-comparison-validation="unavailable-from-this-response"`. The catalog `usageNote` carries the same provider caveat, and the native WebMCP plus generated machine-catalog verifier explicitly proves that this note reaches structured discovery from the shared SSOT.

Fresh provider verification retained the existing admission facts: `https://api.nhtsa.gov/SafetyRatings/VehicleId/19426?format=json` returned HTTP 200 from the GitHub-Pages origin contract with provider `VehicleId=19426`, a 2024 Toyota Camry FWD record, and `OverallRating='5'`. The scoped real GitHub-Pages-origin browser journey remained semantic `ready`, matched request/response identity, exposed the comparison metadata above, passed 390×844 no-overflow, and had **0 unnamed actionable controls**. The run deliberately scoped only NHTSA and therefore did not contact OpenAlex while its independent HTTP-429/backoff evidence remains unresolved.

### 2026-09-13 · NHTSA VehicleId canonical identity validation (local-only)

NHTSA's official Ratings API documentation says the model-year/make/model lookup returns a Vehicle Id for each precise vehicle variant, then instructs clients to pass that identity unchanged through `/SafetyRatings/VehicleId/7520`: `https://www.nhtsa.gov/nhtsa-datasets-and-apis`. The local request SSOT instead exposed `vehicleId` as a number field, whose shared validation accepted any finite decimal, while its `clampInt` builder parsed and silently changed `19426.9` into a request for VehicleId `19426`. The existing 1–999999 workbench envelope is retained explicitly as a local support bound, not claimed as a provider-wide maximum. Human validation, generated machine catalog, WebMCP discovery, and structured execution derive the same contract; the builder preserves trimmed caller identity instead of parsing, clamping, or substituting another VehicleId. Unit coverage checks metadata, exact valid URL identity, rejection of decimal/alphanumeric/zero/out-of-envelope forms, and proves direct invalid builder inputs are not rewritten. Browser verifier coverage is added for machine discovery plus a decimal WebMCP invocation and a Human decimal submission, each required to remain `idle` with zero provider requests, while the existing live VehicleId `19426` journey remains unchanged.

### 2026-09-14 · Ensembl release-116 lifecycle disclosure + gene-ID preflight (local-only)

The prior cycle preserved intermittent Ensembl evidence: direct `rest.ensembl.org` lookups returned HTTP 500 HTML, while GitHub-Pages-origin Chromium alternated between a successful BRAF lookup, a missing-CORS-header failure, and a 20-second timeout. A fresh investigation first repeated the exact candidate request and could no longer reproduce the failure. The stable-ID lookup, the same lookup using only `Accept: application/json`, `/info/ping`, `/info/data`, and `/info/rest` all returned HTTP 200; each browser-origin-shaped response carried `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. The service reported Ensembl data release **116** and REST API release **15.12**, and the unpublished Pages-base bundle completed the live BRAF Request Lab journey with exact stable-ID/object-type/locus mapping, 390×844 no-overflow, and zero unnamed actionable controls.

Official Ensembl evidence classifies the earlier failures as a transient service incident rather than request drift or immediate retirement. The current lookup documentation still defines `GET /lookup/id/:id` for a single gene/transcript/protein stable identifier and still shows JSON selection through the request contract: https://rest.ensembl.org/documentation/info/lookup . Ensembl's transition notice says the classic APIs received their final data update with Ensembl 116 and remain available, while future datasets move to the new platform's GraphQL/refget services: https://www.ensembl.info/2025/12/02/updates-to-programmatic-access-to-ensembl-and-transitioning-to-the-new-ensembl-platform/ . Replacing the working REST lookup with a materially different GraphQL request/response schema is therefore not justified in this cycle. The catalog usage note now exposes the frozen-release boundary to Human UI, machine catalog, and WebMCP instead of implying that this endpoint contains future Ensembl data.

The investigation also found a durable request-validation gap. The demo and semantic adapter are intentionally gene-only, but `geneId` was unconstrained text, so transcript IDs, version-suffixed IDs that the live lookup rejects, and arbitrary strings all passed shared validation and reached Ensembl before being rejected by the provider or semantic adapter. Regression-first catalog and machine-catalog assertions failed against that old metadata. The field now derives the documented Ensembl stable-ID form into the shared `ENS[A-Z]*G[0-9]{11}` gene constraint, explains that the REST lookup requires an unversioned ID, and trims an otherwise-valid value only at the provider boundary. Human native validation and structured WebMCP execution both reject `ENST00000646891` before any provider request; the semantic adapter retains its independent non-Gene response defense for provider drift or malformed HTTP-success data.

### 2026-09-14 · CIRCL contact-bearing User-Agent policy + request-bound CVE 5 semantics (local-only)

Fresh official CIRCL policy evidence corrected an execution-boundary mismatch. `/.well-known/api-policy.json` version 1 (expires 2027-09-14) permits anonymous use up to 20 requests per minute but requires automated clients to identify themselves with a meaningful `User-Agent` containing a contact URL or email. CIRCL's `llms.txt` describes the targeted `/api/vulnerability/<id>` operation as interactive use and repeats the automated-client identification requirement; `robots.txt` directs automated consumers to the policy and disallows named AI crawler user agents. A single separately authorized direct request with a compliant identifying header returned HTTP 200 JSON plus `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` for `CVE-2021-44228`, with CVE 5.1 `dataType=CVE_RECORD`, matching published metadata, and CNA content. That direct evidence established the provider contract, but a normal Chromium page cannot set the protected contact-bearing `User-Agent`, so the generic browser/WebMCP agent surface cannot comply.

The catalog SSOT now classifies `circl-vulnerability` as `manual-only` for structured execution with the official policy URL and bounded reason. The human-triggered Request Lab remains available for targeted interactive use. Machine catalog, ordinary DOM, WebMCP discovery/run enum, catalog quick action, Fetch-code visibility, and the generic automated-verification set all derive the same policy: CIRCL is discoverable, structured execution fails closed before network, quick action navigates without auto-running, reusable Fetch code is withheld, and recurring health sweeps exclude it. Current executable counts are **200 catalog / 197 structured-agent runnable / 195 generic automated-verification eligible**; LanguageTool, Nominatim, and CIRCL are manual-only, while CelesTrak and Stack Exchange remain cadence-limited.

The former permissive CIRCL branch in the generic security-family renderer is replaced by a dedicated API-owned CVE 5 adapter. One semantic ViewModel validates the exact executed `/api/vulnerability/{CVE}` request identity against `cveMetadata.cveId`, the `CVE_RECORD` type, bounded CVE 5 data version, metadata state, and CNA container before exposing human or agent facts. Optional descriptions and affected-product data remain optional rather than being fabricated. A wrong-CVE or malformed HTTP-success payload resolves to `invalid` and withholds plausible wrong details; a valid record without executed request identity remains `partial`. Stable DOM metadata exposes requested/provider CVE, request-bound status, identity match, contract validity, and bounded provider facts from the same ViewModel.

### 2026-09-14 · NVD CVE Detail request identity + API 2.0 semantic contract (local-only)

The focused `nvd-cve-detail` audit found a product regression rather than provider drift. The request was a fixed hard-coded CVE-2024-3094 URL with no shared CVE field, and the result flowed through the generic `SecurityCenterPreview`. That generic branch accepted any `vulnerabilities` rows, did not validate the NVD envelope or exact executed request, did not bind `cveId` to returned `cve.id`, used fallback labels that could look provider-owned, and consumed display helpers that allow numeric-string coercion. An unrelated HTTP-200 CVE, malformed root counters, or a string CVSS score could therefore be shown as plausible security evidence.

Fresh official evidence supports the bounded repair. NVD's current CVE API documentation defines `cveId` as the exact single-CVE filter and identifies `https://csrc.nist.gov/schema/nvd/api/2.0/cve_api_json_2.0.schema` as the response schema. Retrieved on 2026-09-14, that official schema identifies itself as NVD Vulnerability Data API schema **2.2.4** and requires root `resultsPerPage`, `startIndex`, `totalResults`, `format`, `version`, `timestamp`, and `vulnerabilities`; each vulnerability wrapper requires `cve`, while the CVE core requires `id`, `published`, `lastModified`, `references`, and `descriptions`. NVD's Start Here guidance currently documents **5 requests per rolling 30 seconds without an API key** and recommends a **6-second sleep** between requests. The four NVD catalog routes now share a six-second `cadence-limited` automated-verification policy with no same-run non-2xx retry, excluding them from the generic burst sweep while preserving Human and structured-agent execution.

A direct browser-origin-shaped request at **2026-09-14 08:40:01 GMT** returned HTTP 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, exact API 2.0 counters `1/0/1`, `format=NVD_CVE`, `version=2.0`, and matching **CVE-2024-3094**. The record was `Modified`, published `2024-03-29`, last modified `2026-06-17`, and contained 2 descriptions, 59 references, 1 weakness group, and 1 configuration. Its selected CVSS 3.1 assessment was explicitly `Secondary`, sourced to `secalert@redhat.com`, with JSON-number base score **10**, exploitability **3.9**, and impact **6**; the response also contained the newer `ssvcV203` metric family, which the adapter does not mislabel as CVSS. One later isolated real GitHub-Pages-origin Request Lab journey independently passed browser CORS and mapped the live request/response identity, source, status, and strict JSON-number CVSS values into semantic DOM.

The local route now exposes the shared canonical `CVE-YYYY-NNNN` field to Human UI, generated machine catalog, and WebMCP, builds the documented `cveId` request, and uses a dedicated `nvd-vulnerability` layout. The API-owned ViewModel requires the exact supported request URL; strict JSON-number envelope counters; the single-result or explicit zero-result contract; canonical request/provider identity; required core arrays and timestamps; and provider-specific CVSS v4/v3/v2 vector and numeric forms. Wrong identity, malformed envelope, or malformed required core data resolves to `invalid` and withholds plausible details. A malformed optional CVSS assessment resolves to `partial` and withholds the score, while absent optional metrics remain an honest valid omission. Stable DOM evidence exposes request binding, envelope/core/metric validity, provider identity, CVSS source/type, and trusted counts from the same model used by the Human card.

### 2026-09-14 · Singapore regional air-quality metric integrity + official bands (local-only)

A focused audit of `data-gov-psi` and `data-gov-pm25` found a public-health semantic fail-open in their shared regional adapter. When the required `psi_twenty_four_hourly` or `pm25_one_hourly` object was missing, the card selected the first numeric-looking pollutant map instead; a PSI response containing only `pm10_twenty_four_hourly` could therefore become a plausible PSI card. NEA explains that one-hour PM2.5 is a current-air-quality concentration distinct from the rolling 24-hour PSI and that their longer-duration breakpoints are not interchangeable: https://www.nea.gov.sg/our-services/pollution-control/air-pollution/faqs . The official Haze portal publishes the exact bands used by the card: one-hour PM2.5 **0–55 Normal / 56–150 Elevated / 151–250 High / ≥251 Very High**, and 24-hour PSI **0–50 Good / 51–100 Moderate / 101–200 Unhealthy / 201–300 Very unhealthy / >300 Hazardous**: https://www.haze.gov.sg/home . Fresh origin-shaped probes of both admitted legacy endpoints returned HTTP 200, `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, exact metric objects, five named regions, ISO timestamps, and JSON-number readings; the drift classification is therefore product regression, not provider drift.

The shared adapter now requires the exact API-owned metric and documented item/readings envelope, trusts only north/south/east/west/central, rejects numeric strings, non-finite values, negatives, and unknown regional entries, and distinguishes `invalid`, `partial`, and `ready`. Missing or wholly unusable metrics withhold all values and descriptors; mixed malformed/missing regions remain visibly unavailable. Stable DOM evidence exposes the metric key, provider/valid/invalid/missing counts, unit, observation/update timestamps, highest reading, official band basis, and derived regional average. The Human card explicitly labels that mean as derived, while the official descriptor is based on the highest trusted regional reading.

### 2026-09-14 · GitHub Global Advisories filter contract + request-bound semantic adapter (local-only)

A focused audit of `github-global-advisories` found both a real request bug and a semantic fail-open. The catalog offered severity `moderate`, but GitHub's current Global Security Advisories REST contract accepts `unknown`, `low`, `medium`, `high`, and `critical`; a fresh Pages-origin-shaped `severity=moderate` request returned HTTP 422 while `severity=medium` returned HTTP 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` and matching npm advisories. Independently, the route still used the generic security-center renderer, so HTTP-success rows were not bound to the executed `ecosystem`, `severity`, or `per_page` request. ### 2026-09-15 · NVD CPE Search short-page pagination contract (local-only)

### 2026-09-15 · GLEIF first-page pagination coherence + strict metadata semantics (local-only)

A focused pagination audit of `gleif-lei` found a semantic false-ready gap in the existing dedicated legal-entity adapter. The executed request already binds the legal-name filter and `page[size]`, and record identity already requires `record.id === attributes.lei`, but pagination validation only checked `perPage`, `total`, and request page-size equality through a display-oriented numeric parser that also accepts numeric strings. It did not prove `currentPage`, `from`, `to`, `lastPage`, or that the returned first-page row count agrees with the provider total. A plausible HTTP-200 response containing one record, `perPage=8`, and `total=17` could therefore become `ready` even though the first page was truncated; page-2 metadata under the page-1 request or stringified counters could also be trusted.

Fresh GLEIF evidence establishes the provider shape without requiring a request-definition change. From a browser-origin-compatible request, the default `Royal Bank of Canada` search returned HTTP 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, eight rows, and `meta.pagination={currentPage:1,perPage:8,from:1,to:8,total:17,lastPage:3}`. A separate exact-ish `Royal Bank of Canada (Suisse) SA` probe demonstrated the legitimate short-page form: one row with `currentPage=1`, `perPage=8`, `from=1`, `to=1`, `total=1`, `lastPage=1`. A no-match probe returned zero rows with `from=null`, `to=null`, `total=0`, and `lastPage=1`. GLEIF's current official API page continues to describe the production API as the full LEI Data search surface, including single-field legal-entity-name filtering. The evidence therefore identifies a local response-validation gap, not provider/CORS drift.

The adapter now uses strict provider JSON safe-integer primitives for all pagination counters. Because this request does not set `page[number]`, trusted metadata must describe page 1. Non-empty responses require `returnedRows === min(perPage,total)`, `from=1`, `to=returnedRows`, and `lastPage=ceil(total/perPage)`; zero-result responses require zero rows, null `from`/`to`, and `lastPage=1`. Numeric strings are no longer silently coerced into trusted pagination evidence. The existing request page-size binding remains intact, so `perPage=8` is correctly preserved on a legitimate one-row final page. Agent-readable DOM now exposes provider current page, from/to range, total matches, last page, and the shared pagination-contract result from the same semantic ViewModel used by the Human card.

Regression-first coverage produced **3 expected failures** against the old adapter: truncated first-page metadata, non-first-page metadata, and numeric-string pagination all incorrectly resolved to `ready`. After the repair the GLEIF suite passes **9/9**, and focused GLEIF/App/catalog/machine/architecture coverage passes **157/157**. The complete deterministic four-shard Vitest gate passes **738/738 across all 88 files** (**211 + 180 + 113 + 234**), and strict `npm run build:pages` passes. The permanent real GitHub-Pages-origin GLEIF verifier passes the live default search with exact record identity, full first-page pagination coherence, 390×844 no-overflow, and **0 unnamed actionable controls**. Fixture-only browser regressions prove a coherent 1/1 short page remains `ready`, a truncated 1-of-17 HTTP-200 page becomes `partial`, numeric-string pagination becomes `partial`, and the existing mixed wrong-LEI case continues to withhold the fabricated record. Preview-bundle isolation/failure recovery also passes with initial CSS **43,113 decoded bytes**. No API, request URL, provider policy, or UI layout was changed; all work remains local-only and unpublished.

Catalog invariants require every declared step to belong to a numeric field, be finite and positive, and align with the field default.

### 2026-09-16 · Food Standards Agency establishment-search request/self-link binding + strict pagination evidence (local-only)

The implementation therefore uses that provider-owned self link as the response/request search acknowledgement rather than inventing a stricter client-side matching algorithm.

Human content and Agent-readable request/self-link/count/identity evidence derive from the same ViewModel.

### UK Flood Monitoring executed-request + exact-filter binding hardening (2026-09-16)

A request-binding audit found a remaining false-ready gap in the dedicated `uk-flood-monitoring` card. The Environment Agency Flood Monitoring reference defines `riverName=x` as an **exact-match** station filter and `_limit=x` as list pagination, while list response metadata reports the applied native integer `limit`. The prior adapter extracted `riverName` from an arbitrary URL but did not prove that the successful HTTP response belonged to the canonical request, did not reject extra query keys, did not verify that returned station rows actually matched the executed exact river filter, and ignored `meta.limit`. A successful response for a modified request or a payload containing a different river could therefore still appear `ready`. Official contract: https://environment.data.gov.uk/flood-monitoring/doc/reference .

The local candidate now binds readiness to the exact HTTPS `/flood-monitoring/id/stations?riverName=...&_limit=...` request surface: one non-empty trimmed `riverName`, one canonical safe-integer `_limit` in the catalog's 1–50 range, and no extra query keys. The provider must return an `items[]` list and a native positive-integer `meta.limit` equal to the executed limit. Trusted station rows still require provider-owned `label + notation + stationReference`; under a bound request they must also return the exact requested `riverName`. Wrong-river, duplicate, or malformed-identity rows are withheld, and the semantic root now exposes request-bound, requested/provider limit, metadata/count/filter contracts, mismatch counts, duplicate counts, and provider/valid/invalid row counts for browser agents. The shared `riverName` field SSOT also gains `minLength=1`, so Human UI and structured agent execution reject an empty filter before the builder fallback can silently substitute a different request.

Regression-first coverage proved the old behavior with **3 expected failures**: an extra `_offset=0` query still rendered `ready`, an all-`River Thames` response under an executed `River Severn` request rendered `ready`, and `meta.limit: "8"` was not rejected. After remediation, the affected six-file gate passes **172/172**, and deterministic complete Vitest coverage passes **1052/1052 across 108/108 files**. The Pages-base build passes with **165 modules**; preview-bundle isolation/budgets/failure recovery passes with initial CSS **43,113 decoded bytes**; native WebMCP/provider-policy passes with **200 catalog / 197 structured-agent runnable / 191 automated-verification eligible / 5 native tools**; candidate hygiene passes across **145 tracked + 189 untracked** local files with zero whitespace errors, zero conflict markers, and zero staged files; and `git diff --check` passes.

The real Pages-origin browser health check is intentionally **not** recorded as PASS in this milestone. ### 2026-09-16 · Google Public DNS exact-request + native integer wire-contract hardening (local-only)

A continued request-binding/wire-type audit found that the dedicated `google-dns-doh` card already verified the provider-owned `Question` name/type identity, distinguished DNS-layer errors such as NXDOMAIN from malformed data, preserved CNAME chains, and withheld malformed answer rows, but its executed-request parser remained permissive. Any parseable URL carrying `name` / `type` could establish request identity, so an HTTP-success response from a request with additional semantics such as `do=true`, a duplicate `type`, or another unsupported route could still become `ready` if the returned Question happened to match. Google Public DNS's current official JSON DoH specification states that all calls are GET, duplicate parameters use only the first value, `name` is required, `type` accepts RR codes/names, and additional parameters such as `cd`, `ct`, `do`, `edns_client_subnet`, and `random_padding` can materially change request behavior. The same documented response examples define `Status`, `Question[].type`, `Answer[].type`, and `Answer[].TTL` as JSON integers. The adapter nevertheless still used the display-oriented `finite()` coercer, allowing numeric strings to masquerade as native provider integers.

The local candidate now binds semantic trust to the exact catalog-admitted HTTPS `dns.google/resolve` surface: exactly one non-empty `name` and one `type`, no extra or duplicate query keys, the exact host/path, and only the six RR types exposed by the shared Human/Agent field SSOT (`A`, `AAAA`, `MX`, `TXT`, `CNAME`, `NS`). Query parameter order is not treated as semantic. Unsupported executed URLs fail `invalid` before DNS answers are presented. Provider `Status` and answer TTL accept only native non-negative safe integers, while Question/Answer type codes accept only native positive safe integers within DNS's 1..65535 RR-type range. Matching NXDOMAIN/SERVFAIL remain DNS-layer states rather than being mislabeled as application corruption; valid CNAME chains remain allowed. Human UI and Agent-readable DOM continue to derive from the same ViewModel, now with explicit `data-request-bound` evidence.

The shared DNS `name` field now declares `minLength=1`, so Human UI/WebMCP/structured-agent validation rejects an empty name before execution. The URL builder no longer silently substitutes `example.com` when an explicitly supplied value trims to empty; provider-specific escaped-label, per-label, punycode, and optional-trailing-dot length rules remain provider-validated instead of being approximated by an unsafe generic `maxLength` rule.

## 2026-09-17 — Nominatim policy-admission removal + request-bound Open-Meteo geocoding (local-only)

A Geo-family audit selected the remaining generic geocoding path because the local catalog exposed two overlapping place-search capabilities with materially different provider-admission characteristics. The current OpenStreetMap Foundation Nominatim public-service policy permits bounded direct end-user-triggered use under attribution/rate/referrer requirements, but it also explicitly rejects generic geocoding/search integration through broad no-code, low-code, and vibe-coding style platforms unless use is a deliberate informed developer choice. Public-API is not classified by the provider under one of those named product classes, so this milestone does not claim that every Public-API interaction would violate the policy. The admission conclusion is narrower: a generic multi-provider browser workbench should not keep advertising the shared public Nominatim instance when the policy does not provide a comfortable durable basis for that generic exposure and a browser-ready duplicate capability already exists. The local candidate therefore removes `nominatim-search` instead of treating `manual-only` structured-agent execution as sufficient policy isolation; Human UI exposure would otherwise remain.

The replacement capability is the already-admitted `geocoding-search` backed directly by the Open-Meteo Geocoding API. Current provider documentation defines `https://geocoding-api.open-meteo.com/v1/search` with required `name`, optional result `count`, language/format controls, city/place or postal-code lookup, two-character exact-name matching, three-or-more-character prefix matching, GeoNames-based location data, provider integer location IDs, WGS84 latitude/longitude, and optional country/timezone/elevation/population/postcode metadata. Fresh bounded probes under `Origin: https://yapweijun1996.github.io` returned HTTP 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` for the canonical `Singapore` request and six native location records. The provider also accepted an undeclared `foo=bar` parameter and duplicate conflicting `count=6&count=2` with HTTP 200, while a coherent no-match response omitted `results` and retained `generationtime_ms`. Transport success therefore cannot establish exact workbench request identity.

The local candidate now routes `geocoding-search` through a dedicated `OpenMeteoGeocodingPreview` in the existing Catalog Family lazy bundle. Semantic trust requires the exact successful HTTPS `/v1/search` request with exactly one each of non-empty `name`, canonical integer `count`, `language=en`, and `format=json`, no extra/duplicate keys, and values within the shared catalog field contract. Trusted rows require a unique positive provider location ID, non-empty name, native finite WGS84 coordinates, and correctly typed optional provider fields; numeric strings are not silently coerced. Malformed, duplicate, or over-limit rows are withheld. A documented no-match envelope maps to `empty`; request/envelope/identity contradiction maps to `invalid`; mixed trusted and untrusted evidence maps to `partial`; complete evidence maps to `ready`. Human UI and Agent-readable DOM consume the same ViewModel and expose stable request/count/location-ID evidence. The shared input SSOT now requires `name.minLength=2` and integer `count.step=1`; explicit invalid values remain visible to common pre-network validation instead of being silently replaced or clamped. The usage note explicitly describes place/postal-code search and does not claim street-address geocoding.

The current catalog therefore becomes **197 catalog APIs / 195 structured-agent runnable / 189 automated-verification-eligible / 5 native WebMCP tools**. `nominatim-search` is absent from Human catalog, machine catalog, and WebMCP open/run surfaces; its old deep link fails closed to canonical `countries`. No replacement API was added merely to preserve a round catalog number because the hardened Open-Meteo path already preserves the real place/postal geocoding capability.

## 2026-09-17 — data.gov.sg request-bound taxi-availability semantics (local-only)

The remaining generic `LocationPreview` audit selected `data-gov-taxi` because the old family mapper trusted only the first feature, accepted coordinate arrays longer than two items, coerced numeric strings through display helpers, discarded malformed positions without a semantic warning, invented `Available taxi N` labels, and never received the successful run's executed request. Current official data.gov.sg dataset/OpenAPI evidence defines `GET https://api.data.gov.sg/v1/transport/taxi-availability` as a GeoJSON `FeatureCollection` containing one `MultiPoint` feature with exact `[longitude, latitude]` numeric pairs plus `properties.timestamp` and integer `taxi_count`. The timestamp is data.gov.sg's acquisition/scrape time because LTA supplies no per-taxi metadata; recommended polling is every minute. The provider's currently observed `properties.api_info.status` is outside that published TaxiLocations schema and is not trusted for readiness. Official dataset contract: https://data.gov.sg/collections/352/datasets/d_e25662f1a062dd046453926aa284ba64/view .

The local candidate now routes only `data-gov-taxi` through API-owned `DataGovTaxiAvailabilityPreview` in the existing lazy Catalog Family bundle and reports the dedicated `taxi-availability` layout. Semantic trust requires the actual successful bodyless `GET` to the exact HTTPS host/path with no query, hash, credentials, alternate port, or trailing slash. The response must be one `FeatureCollection` / `Feature` / `MultiPoint`, with a parseable date-time, native non-negative safe-integer `taxi_count`, exact native finite two-item coordinate pairs inside WGS84 bounds, and `taxi_count === coordinates.length`. Duplicate coordinate pairs are preserved because they are provider availability positions, not stable taxi identities. A coherent native zero plus `coordinates: []` is the only `empty` state. Request/envelope/count/timestamp mismatches and wholly malformed coordinate batches are `invalid`; mixed valid/malformed coordinates are `partial` with malformed positions withheld. Optional `api_info.status` cannot promote an invalid core response.

The card shows the complete available-taxi count and data.gov.sg acquisition time, explicitly says that the timestamp is not per-taxi observation time and that anonymous positions are not stable taxi identities, and bounds its map/list sample to eight validated coordinates while retaining complete provider/count evidence in Agent-readable DOM. The adapter does not invent a closed visibility enum that the primary documentation does not publish. Numeric strings and zero-valued orbital measurements fail closed. Supplemental day-number and solar context is strictly parsed without misclassifying the provider's documented 0–360 solar-longitude convention as WGS84; malformed supplemental context yields `partial`, while request, identity, position, or core-measurement contradictions yield `invalid`. ## 2026-09-17 — Zippopotam.us request-bound postcode geolocation semantics (local-only)

A remaining generic Geo audit selected `zippopotam-postcode` ahead of Open Brewery DB because the catalog promises postcode geolocation while the generic `LocationPreview` recursively coerced arbitrary coordinate-like values, invented fallback place labels, and did not bind its result to the successful executed request. The current official [Zippopotam.us v1 documentation](https://docs.zippopotam.us/docs/v1/) defines the postal lookup as `GET /{country}/{postalcode}`, requires an ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 country path plus a country-specific postal-code path, and explicitly notes that one postal code can return multiple places. The provider's [official OpenAPI contract](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/zippopotamus/spec/master/v1/openapi.yaml) and [postal-code schema](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/zippopotamus/spec/master/v1/schema/postal-code.yaml) define `post code`, country, country abbreviation, a `places` array, place/state names, and latitude/longitude as JSON strings. The provider's [browser CORS guidance](https://docs.zippopotam.us/docs/getting-started/) says CORS is enabled for JavaScript consumers. These sources justify a dedicated adapter and a string-faithful coordinate parser; they do not justify adding a country-specific postal-code regex to the shared field SSOT.

The local candidate now routes `zippopotam-postcode` through API-owned `ZippopotamPostcodePreview` in the existing lazy Catalog Family bundle and reports the dedicated `postcode-geolocation` layout. Semantic trust requires the exact successful HTTPS request to `https://api.zippopotam.us/{lowercase-country}/{encoded-postal-code}` with no query, duplicate key, hash, credentials, alternate port, or trailing slash. Root provider postcode and country abbreviation must match the executed request; valid places require non-empty place names, documented coordinate strings parsed to finite WGS84 values, and unique place/coordinate identity. Numeric coordinate values, malformed rows, duplicate rows, and malformed optional state evidence are not fabricated into locations. Coherent empty place arrays map to `empty`; request/envelope/identity contradictions map to `invalid`; mixed valid and withheld evidence maps to `partial`; complete evidence maps to `ready`. Human UI and Agent-readable DOM share one ViewModel with exact request, requested/provider identity, provider/validated/withheld counts, duplicate count, place names, state metadata, and coordinates. Existing country-code SSOT hardening remains the only field-format change; the provider's deliberately broad country-specific postal-code contract remains unconstrained beyond non-empty text.

A continuation audit of the accumulated unpublished candidate found that `open-brewery-directory` was the last production route still using the recursive generic `LocationPreview`. This migration also removes the now-dead generic `LocationPreview`, its recursive coordinate crawler, and its registry loader entirely; there are no remaining production routes using that generic location renderer. ### 2026-09-17 — iNaturalist request-bound observation semantics with privacy-minimized restricted-location presentation (local-only)

A follow-up biodiversity audit revisited `inaturalist-observations` because its accumulated dedicated adapter already had exact request/observation/taxon/photo-license semantics, but earlier live verification had been blocked by transient HTTP 503 / DNS failures and the semantic card still displayed exact provider `observed_on` and `place_guess` values for obscured observations. Current iNaturalist recommended practices continue to ask API clients to stay near one request/second and roughly 10,000 requests/day and to back off on HTTP 429. Current iNaturalist licensing guidance treats observation data, photos, and sounds as separately licensed media. Most importantly for this milestone, current geoprivacy guidance explicitly warns that API responses can still expose the exact date of an obscured observation and that date/nearby-observation clues can help infer a sensitive location, while the public iNaturalist UI deliberately coarsens restricted location information.

Fresh provider evidence now distinguishes those prior failures from durable provider drift. The exact admitted request `https://api.inaturalist.org/v1/observations?taxon_name=Panthera&per_page=6&photos=true` returned HTTP 200 with browser-readable `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, native first-page metadata, six observations from a provider total of **66,497**, and provider-owned observation/taxon/photo identity. The first current live row was observation **400916099** with taxon geoprivacy `obscured`, provider-obscured public coordinates, an exact raw provider observation date, and a place label. A bounded request with an undeclared `foo=bar` parameter also returned HTTP 200; a duplicate conflicting `per_page=6&per_page=2` request returned HTTP 200 but the provider selected a different effective page size (**30**). Transport success therefore cannot establish the workbench's exact request identity; the existing fail-closed exact-request parser remains required.

The local candidate now adds a bounded privacy-minimization layer to the semantic ViewModel without altering Raw JSON. For `obscured` or `private` observations, the API-owned card no longer carries the raw exact provider date/place text into its primary semantic presentation: full dates are reduced to month-level display (or year-level when only a year is available), and provider `place_guess` is withheld as `Sensitive location label withheld`. Provider-obscured public coordinates remain visible when iNaturalist returns them, because those are the provider's intended public coordinates, together with the existing warning not to infer a more precise location. The ViewModel exposes `data-privacy-minimized-observation-count` so ordinary browser agents receive the same privacy state as human users. Photo-license semantics are unchanged: Creative Commons codes are shown per photo, `license_code=null` is treated as all rights reserved, and no reusable-rights claim is fabricated. The catalog usage note now records that API responses can retain date/location clues and that this card deliberately minimizes the primary semantic presentation while Raw JSON remains source-faithful.

Regression-first coverage proved the change was real: the new obscured-observation privacy assertions failed against the previous card before implementation. Final focused iNaturalist/App/catalog/machine/architecture/load-boundary coverage passes **193/193 tests across 6 files**. Deterministic exact four-shard Vitest coverage passes **1,288/1,288 tests across all 132 discovered test files** (`392 + 339 + 304 + 253`). `npm run build:pages` passes with **196 modules transformed**.

Contract references: https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/AudioVideo/Conceptual/iTuneSearchAPI/Searching.html , https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/AudioVideo/Conceptual/iTuneSearchAPI/UnderstandingSearchResults.html , and https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/AudioVideo/Conceptual/iTuneSearchAPI/index.html .

Trusted rows require the entity-specific provider wrapper/kind, native positive provider identity, title, artist, and required Apple HTTPS storefront link; duplicate, wrong-entity, numeric-string, malformed-link, and malformed optional evidence is withheld or marked partial. Coherent exact-request zero results map to `empty`; a non-empty batch with no trustworthy identity maps to `invalid`.

Human UI and Agent-readable DOM share this ViewModel through stable request/count/primary-identity attributes. The card presents text metadata and Apple storefront links, but deliberately embeds no artwork and no audio/video preview; promotional URLs remain source-faithful only in Raw JSON. Query relevance is not overclaimed because Apple's response does not echo the search term. Malformed and duplicate identities are withheld, while optional native numbers, dates, schedules, channels, sites, genres, ratings, and nullable image evidence are parsed without numeric-string coercion. ### 2026-09-17 — DummyJSON effective-limit recipe semantics (local-only)

Fresh browser evidence supplied by the outer operator established DummyJSON's current first-page contract under the real GitHub Pages origin. The canonical default `q=pasta&limit=6` request returned HTTP 200 with readable origin-specific CORS and `{ total: 3, skip: 0, limit: 3 }` plus three rows; `q=a&limit=6` returned 6 of 50, `q=pasta&limit=2` returned 2 of 3, and a zero match returned `{ total: 0, skip: 0, limit: 0 }`. The provider's `limit` is therefore its effective returned first-page count, `min(total, requestedLimit)`, rather than an unconditional echo of the requested limit. An undeclared `foo=bar` was silently accepted with the same HTTP-success envelope, so the exact executed-request parser remains necessary. The current provider returned HTTP 400 for a conflicting duplicate `limit=6&limit=2`; the workbench still rejects duplicates locally and makes no claim that the provider tolerates them.

The accumulated candidate now routes `dummyjson-recipes` through API-owned `DummyJsonRecipesPreview` in the existing lazy Catalog Family bundle and reports the dedicated `recipe-search` layout. Semantic trust requires the exact bodyless HTTPS `/recipes/search` request with exactly one non-empty canonical `q` and one integer `limit` from 1 through 10. Unknown or duplicate parameters, credentials, alternate ports, hashes, and trailing-slash variants fail closed. Response `total`, `skip`, and effective `limit` must be native non-negative safe integers; `skip` must be zero, effective `limit` must equal `min(total, requestedLimit)`, and the array must contain at least that trusted first-page boundary. Only those expected rows are parsed. Positive native recipe IDs and non-empty names establish identity; malformed or duplicate identities, malformed-present supplemental evidence, and trailing overflow rows are withheld and yield `partial`. Missing optional cooking, nutrition, rating, list, or image evidence remains unavailable without becoming malformed. Coherent zero results require provider `limit=0` and map to `empty`.

Human UI and Agent-readable DOM share one ViewModel with request, provider pagination, trusted/malformed/duplicate/overflow counts, and primary recipe identity. The gallery is a named semantic section, the default real-provider 3-of-6 shape maps to `ready`, and the card retains conservative wording: DummyJSON is synthetic placeholder data, while the repository code licence does not establish reuse rights for individual recipe content or images. The active generic `MediaGalleryPreview` inventory is now exactly `dogs` and `randomfox-photo`.

Checks actually run in this sandbox: focused DummyJSON/catalog/architecture/App coverage passes **214/214 tests across 4 files**; `npm run build` passes with **206 modules transformed**; and `node --check scripts/verify-dummyjson-recipes-card.mjs` passes. The permanent verifier and package script are installed for an outer network-capable run. It is designed to issue exactly one live canonical/default request under the real Pages origin, assert readable CORS plus semantic `ready`, request binding, provider identity, 390×844 reflow, and zero unnamed actions, then run a malformed/duplicate/numeric-string/overflow HTTP-200 fixture that must resolve `partial` with zero live-provider escape. The verifier was deliberately not run here because this sandbox has no network, so this milestone makes no new local live-browser PASS claim. Publication remains local-only and unpublished.

Provider rows require a valid in-year ISO date, non-empty native name, exact requested country, native boolean `nationalHoliday`, nullable-or-native subdivision arrays, and only documented holiday-type values. Duplicate identities and malformed rows are withheld; complete trusted batches map to `ready`, mixed evidence to `partial`, exact empty arrays to `empty`, and unusable non-empty batches/request mismatches to `invalid`.

Human UI and Agent-readable DOM share one ViewModel with stable request country/year, provider/trusted/malformed/duplicate counts, national/regional counts, and primary holiday date/name. The calendar exposes provider holiday types and subdivision scope without inventing a legal or employment `day off` interpretation. The direct generic `CalendarPreview` registry inventory is reduced from three routes to two (`uk-bank-holidays` and `hebcal-calendar`) without changing catalog count.
