## Hebcal Hebrew-year exact-request semantic hardening — 2026-09-18

- Root cause was a Public-API request-contract bug, not provider downtime: the previous `hebcal-calendar` request labelled `year=5786` as a Hebrew year but omitted Hebcal's `yt=H`, so Hebcal accepted the request with HTTP 200/CORS while interpreting 5786 as Gregorian CE (response range in Gregorian year 5786 and Hebrew dates around year 9546). The current documented Hebrew-year contract uses `yt=H`, `month=x`, explicit event flags, and `i=off|on` for Diaspora/Israel.
- Fresh Pages-origin probes also proved that HTTP 200 is insufficient request evidence: Hebcal tolerated an unknown `foo=bar` parameter and duplicate conflicting `year` parameters. The dedicated `HebcalCalendarPreview` therefore fails closed unless the executed request is the exact admitted bodyless HTTPS `/hebcal` request with one canonical Hebrew year, one schedule, and the fixed supported parameter set.
- Catalog SSOT now exposes only meaningful Hebrew-year inputs (`year`, `Diaspora|Israel`), preserves explicit invalid values instead of silently clamping/replacing them, documents Hebcal's CC BY 4.0 attribution boundary and 429 policy, and uses the same catalog validation for Human UI / structured agent / WebMCP execution.
- The dedicated semantic ViewModel validates the provider title/range against the executed Hebrew year and schedule, validates Gregorian/Hebrew event identity and native optional fields, withholds malformed/duplicate evidence, and shares the same trusted model between Human UI and Agent-readable DOM. It does not infer religious practice, work restrictions, or legal holiday status from provider event categories.
- The former generic `CalendarPreview` production component/loader has no remaining production consumer and was retired. `hebcal-calendar` now uses the `jewish-calendar` preview profile. No CSS was added.
- Verification: focused Vitest `208/208` PASS. Full deterministic Vitest passed in four shards after the monolithic command exceeded the VM command wall-clock without a test failure: `498 + 337 + 243 + 351 = 1,429/1,429` tests across `149/149` files. `npm run build:pages` PASS with `210` modules transformed.
- Real GitHub-Pages-origin Hebcal E2E PASS with exactly one live provider request: Hebrew year `5787`, Diaspora, `153` trusted events, range `2026-09-11..2027-10-01`, primary event `2026-09-11 / Erev Rosh Hashana`, semantic `ready`, `requestBound=true`, browser CORS readable, `390x844` horizontal overflow false, unnamed actionable controls `0`. A separate malformed/duplicate HTTP-200 fixture produced `partial` with `2` trusted / `1` malformed / `1` duplicate / `1` supplemental-malformed event and `0` live-provider escape.
- Preview lazy-bundle gate PASS: initial CSS `43,113 B`; Catalog Family result CSS `31,988 / 32,000 B`; mobile reflow and lazy-load failure recovery PASS. Provider-policy/WebMCP gate PASS: `196 catalog / 193 structured-agent runnable / 187 automated-verification eligible / 5 native WebMCP tools`. PWA gate PASS with `196` APIs visible offline and no cross-origin provider-response caching.
- Whole-candidate hygiene before this ledger-only append: `147 tracked + 316 untracked = 463` local candidate files, whitespace errors `0`, conflict markers `0`, staged `0`; `git diff --check` PASS. `HEAD=a27ac0573941119b52eae818ffb10b85310e2b0c`, branch remains ahead by `2`. ### Launch Library exact-request + cadence-aware semantic hardening (2026-09-18)

A fresh evidence-backed audit of `launch-library-upcoming` found that the route still used an older permissive `LaunchSchedulePreview` despite its domain-specific `launch-schedule` layout. The Human/agent catalog builder also silently repaired explicit invalid input (`blank query -> SpaceX`, fractional/out-of-range limit -> clamped integer), and the documented anonymous production quota of **15 requests/hour** was only prose rather than executable automated-verification policy. Official Launch Library 2 documentation confirms the current `/2.3.0/launches/upcoming/` search surface, `ordering=net`, pagination envelope, and searchable launch/provider/mission/pad/rocket/spacecraft fields; TheSpaceDevs documents anonymous production access at 15 requests/hour. The candidate now models that quota as `cadence-limited` with a **240-second minimum interval** and `retryOnNon2xx=false`, reducing the generic automated-verification-eligible set from 187 to **186** while keeping the route structured-agent runnable. This is a correctness/safety improvement, not a provider removal.

The new dedicated `LaunchLibraryUpcomingPreview` binds successful semantics to the exact executed bodyless HTTPS GET for `/2.3.0/launches/upcoming/` with exactly one `search`, one canonical integer `limit=1..6`, and `ordering=net`. Extra/duplicate parameters, credentials, alternate ports, hash/path variants, noncanonical integers, method/body changes, inconsistent first-page cardinality, or pagination links that fail to preserve the request fail closed. Native launch UUID, canonical Launch Library detail URL, NET timestamp, status identity, and documented search evidence are validated without string/number coercion; duplicate, malformed, or search-mismatched rows are withheld and surface `partial` when trustworthy rows remain. Human UI and Agent-readable DOM share this same ViewModel and expose stable request/provider/trusted/malformed/duplicate/search-mismatch evidence plus primary launch identity/status/provider. Shared field validation now owns non-empty search, bounded text length, and whole-number limit; the URL builder no longer substitutes or clamps explicit invalid values.

GLEIF exact-request semantic hardening on 2026-09-18: a bounded provider-contract audit found a concrete HTTP-200 false-ready risk in the existing `gleif-lei` request parser. The supported legal-name request is `GET https://api.gleif.org/api/v1/lei-records?filter[entity.legalName]=...&page[size]=...`; GLEIF's current official API page documents entity-name filtering plus fuzzy/full-text search over the Global LEI Index and links to the current API documentation. Fresh browser-origin-compatible probes showed the canonical Royal Bank of Canada request returning HTTP 200 with CORS `*`, 8 rows and `perPage=8`; an unknown `foo=bar` parameter currently returns 400, but duplicate supported parameters are accepted. In particular, `page[size]=8&page[size]=2` returned HTTP 200 with two rows, and conflicting duplicate legal-name filters `Royal Bank of Canada` then `Apple Inc` returned HTTP 200 with Apple Inc. effective results. The previous adapter used `URLSearchParams.get(...)`, so it could bind to the first duplicate while the provider executed a different duplicate value. This is provider permissiveness that exposed a local request-identity bug, not a provider outage.

The local candidate now treats the GLEIF executed request as a strict two-parameter contract: HTTPS only, exact `/api/v1/lei-records` path, no credentials/alternate port/hash, no extra or duplicate query keys, exactly one non-empty already-trimmed `filter[entity.legalName]`, and one canonical decimal `page[size]` from 1..50. Unsupported request identity resolves semantic `invalid` before any HTTP-success body can be trusted. The catalog SSOT now points to the current GLEIF API page, gives legal name `minLength=1`, gives count `step=1`, and no longer silently replaces blank names or parses/clamps fractional/out-of-range count input; shared Human/WebMCP validation owns those failures before provider execution. The dedicated legal-entity DOM also exposes `data-request-bound`, while provider-owned LEI remains the stable entity identity; the card deliberately does not require literal legal-name substring equality because GLEIF documents provider matching/fuzzy-search behavior.

## 2026-09-23 — Swiss Transit connection exact-input SSOT closure (local-only)

A focused remaining silent-normalization audit selected `swiss-transit-connections`. The shared card exposes required origin/destination text plus a deliberately conservative 1–10 connection count, but the URL builder still replaced explicit blank `from` / `to` values with the Zurich/Geneva demo defaults and used `clampInt()` to silently rewrite a fractional count such as `6.5` to `6`.
